Setup

init function

function init() 
init  Add all GuliweRR subfolders to the MATLAB path.
 
  Usage (run once per MATLAB session, from the project root):
    run('guliwerr/init.m')
    -- or, if guliwerr/ is already on the path --
    init()
 
  -------------------------------------------------------------------------
  TIP: To load GuliweRR automatically at every MATLAB startup, add the
  following line to your personal startup.m file.
  (In MATLAB: edit(fullfile(userpath,'startup.m')) to open or create it.)
 
    run('/full/path/to/Guliwerr/guliwerr/init.m')
 
  IMPORTANT: Do NOT use addpath(genpath(...)) on the guliwerr/ folder.
  genpath() recurses into @ClassName/ subfolders and adds them directly to
  the path, which breaks MATLAB's class-method lookup for those classes.
  This init.m adds only the parent folders, which is what MATLAB requires.
  -------------------------------------------------------------------------

Core building blocks

ElastConsts value class

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
  Elastic constants in the three formats GuliweRR uses: independent-constant vector,
   Voigt 6x6 matrix, and full 3x3x3x3 tensor, kept in sync with a symmetry class.
 
  Value class. Set*/Get* methods take the object; other methods are static.
 
 % GuliweRR v6.0 %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
 %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
  Properties %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Properties

obj.nIndepConsts (default: 0) 
number of independent constants
obj.shearAnisotropyRatio (default: NaN) 
(v_shear,max / v_shear,min)^2 over ALL directions, >= 1, rotation-invariant; stored only where a closed form exists: isotropic 1, cubic max(A_Z, 1/A_Z). NaN otherwise (no closed form; CalcAnisotropyRatio scans)
obj.symmClassNo (default: 0) 
symmetry class number -- see SymmClassLibrary
obj.tensor (default: double) 
tensor of elastic constants (3^4)
obj.vector (default: NaN) 
short vector format of independent constants
obj.voigt (default: [NaN NaN NaN NaN NaN NaN;NaN NaN NaN NaN NaN NaN;NaN NaN NaN NaN Na... 
voigt matrix   (6x6)

Methods

obj = ElastConsts(consts, symmNo) 
% Initialise elastic constants object
 % >> obj = ElastConsts(consts,symmNo)
 %
 % INPUT: 1. consts .. elastic constants (vector / Voigt 6x6 / tensor 3x3x3x3)
 %  (opt) 2. symmNo .. symmetry class number (see SymmClassLibrary); if omitted,
 %                     inferred via FindSymmClass_input — see its caveats
outTen = ElastConsts.Any2Tensor(inp, symmNo) 
% Transform any form of elastic constants (vector/voigt/tensor) to tensor
 % >> outTen = Any2Tensor(inp,symmNo)
 %
 % INPUT:  1. inp    .. elastic constants (vector / Voigt 6x6 / tensor 3x3x3x3)
 %  (opt)  2. symmNo .. symmetry class number (see SymmClassLibrary)
 % OUTPUT: outTen .. elastic tensor (3x3x3x3)
A = ElastConsts.CalcAnisotropyRatio(normElTen, angleStep) 
% Calculate generalised anisotropy ratio A
 %  as (fastest shear / slowest shear)^2 over all propagation directions
 % >> A = CalcAnisotropyRatio(normElTen,angleStep)
 %
 % INPUT: 1. normElTen  .. elastic tensor (3x3x3x3), density-normalised
 %  (opt) 2. angleStep  .. angular step in degrees (default = 4)
 % OUTPUT: generalised anisotropy ratio (>= 1; = 1 for isotropic)
 % NOTE: For cubic symmetry prefer GetAnisotropyRatio (exact Zener formula).
[vecOut, transfMat] = ElastConsts.CubicConversion(vecIn) 
% Convert vector of cubic elastic constants (c11,c12,c44) to constants (B,c',c44),
 %  where B is bulk modulus, c' = (c11-c12)/2
 % >> [vecOut,transfMat] = CubicConversion(vecIn)
 %
 % INPUT:  (opt) vecIn .. cubic elastic constants [c11,c12,c44] (3x1 or 1x3);
 %                        omit (or pass []) to get the transformation matrix alone
 % OUTPUT: 1. vecOut    .. transformed constants (B,c',c44) as a 3x1 column
 %         2. transfMat .. transformation matrix, vecOut(3x1) = transfMat(3x3)*vecIn(3x1)
[vecOut, deTransfMat] = ElastConsts.CubicDeconversion(vecIn) 
% De-Convert vector of cubic elastic constants (c11,c12,c44) from constants (B,c',c44),
 %  where B is bulk modulus, c' = (c11-c12)/2
 % >> [vecOut,deTransfMat] = CubicDeconversion(vecIn)
 %
 % INPUT:  (opt) vecIn .. constants (B,c',c44) (3x1 or 1x3); omit (or pass []) to get
 %                        the de-transformation matrix alone
 % OUTPUT: 1. vecOut      .. recovered constants (c11,c12,c44) as a 3x1 column
 %         2. deTransfMat .. inverse of CubicConversion's transformation matrix
indep2RedElastMat = ElastConsts.ElastRotReductMat(rotMat, symmNo) 
% Map from independent constants to the 15 reduced constants of the 2D RR system,
 % for a given rotation
 % >> indep2RedElastMat = ElastRotReductMat(rotMat,symmNo)
 %
 % INPUT:  1. rotMat .. matrix of direction row vectors (2x3/3x2 or 3x3)
 %         2. symmNo .. symmetry class number (see SymmClassLibrary)
 % OUTPUT: indep2RedElastMat .. 15 x noIndepConsts
 %
 % The reduction drops every Voigt pair touching index 2 (the completion axis under the
 % standard x1=direction, x2=completion, x3=plane convention): e_22 vanishes for a field
 % with no completion-axis dependence, so C_I2/C_2J terms add nothing to the strain energy.
symmNo = ElastConsts.FindSymmClass_input(inp) 
% Try to identify symmetry class based on input
 % >> symmNo = FindSymmClass_input(inp)
 %
 % INPUT:  inp .. elastic constants (vector / Voigt 6x6 / tensor 3x3x3x3)
 % OUTPUT: symmNo .. symmetry class number
 %
 % GOTCHA: reliable only from a vector of independent constants. Voigt (and tensor,
 % which is converted to Voigt first) input counts unique(cij) entries, which
 % miscounts whenever two independent constants happen to be numerically equal.
 % Prefer supplying symmNo explicitly.
A = obj.GetAnisotropyRatio(angleStep) 
% Anisotropy ratio: exact Zener 2*c44/(c11-c12) for cubic, otherwise the
 % generalised form (v_shear_max/v_shear_min)^2 from an angular scan
 % >> A = GetAnisotropyRatio(obj,angleStep)
 %
 % INPUT (opt): angleStep .. scan step [degrees] (default = 4); ignored for
 %                           isotropic/cubic, which need no scan
[vecOut, transfMatrix] = obj.GetCubicConversion() 
% Equivalent cubic constants [B, c', c44] (cubic symmetry only)
 % >> [vecOut,transfMatrix] = GetCubicConversion(obj)
 %
 % OUTPUT: vecOut(3x1) = transfMatrix(3x3) * [c11;c12;c44]
[vecOut, transfMatrix] = obj.GetHexagonalConversion() 
% Equivalent hexagonal physical-mode constants [C11, C33, G1, G2, G3] (hexagonal only)
 % >> [vecOut,transfMatrix] = GetHexagonalConversion(obj)
 %
 % OUTPUT: vecOut(5x1) = transfMatrix(5x5) * [c11;c12;c13;c33;c44]
[c11_hill, c44_hill] = obj.GetHillAverages() 
% Hill averages: c11_hill == M (longitudinal), c44_hill == G (shear)
 % >> [c11_hill,c44_hill] = GetHillAverages(obj)
[indSet_ReductElast, nReductElast] = ElastConsts.GetIndOf2DReduct_15of21() 
% Indices of the 15 (of 21) elastic constants surviving the 2D reduction
 % >> [indSet_ReductElast,nReductElast] = GetIndOf2DReduct_15of21()
 %
 % See ElastRotReductMat for which Voigt pairs are dropped and why.
redElast = obj.GetReducedElastConsts(rotMat) 
% Get the 15 reduced elastic constants (2D RR system) for a given rotation
 % >> redElast = GetReducedElastConsts(obj,rotMat)
 %
 % INPUT (opt): rotMat .. rotation matrix, columns [propagation | surface normal]
 %                       (3x2; default propagation [100], normal [001])
[c11_reuss, c44_reuss] = obj.GetReussAverages() 
% Reuss averages: c11_reuss == M (longitudinal), c44_reuss == G (shear)
 % >> [c11_reuss,c44_reuss] = GetReussAverages(obj)
rotElTen = obj.GetRotElastTensor(rotMat) 
% Elastic tensor (3x3x3x3) rotated by the given rotation matrix
 % >> rotElTen = GetRotElastTensor(obj,rotMat)
 %
 % INPUT (opt): rotMat .. rotation matrix, columns [propagation | surface normal]
 %                        (3x2; default propagation [100], normal [001])
rotElVoigt = obj.GetRotElastVoigt(rotMat) 
% Voigt matrix (6x6) of the elastic tensor rotated by the given rotation matrix
 % >> rotElVoigt = GetRotElastVoigt(obj,rotMat)
 %
 % INPUT (opt): rotMat .. rotation matrix, columns [propagation | surface normal]
 %                        (3x2; default propagation [100], normal [001])
[vecOut, transfMatrix] = obj.GetTetragonalConversion() 
% Equivalent tetragonal constants [B, c33, c', c'', c44, c66] (tetragonal type I only)
 % >> [vecOut,transfMatrix] = GetTetragonalConversion(obj)
 %
 % OUTPUT: vecOut(6x1) = transfMatrix(6x6) * vecInput(6x1)
[c11_voigt, c44_voigt] = obj.GetVoigtAverages() 
% Voigt averages: c11_voigt == M (longitudinal), c44_voigt == G (shear)
 % >> [c11_voigt,c44_voigt] = GetVoigtAverages(obj)
E = obj.GetYoungsModulus(rotMat) 
% Young's modulus along the rotated x1 direction
 % >> E = GetYoungsModulus(obj,rotMat)
 %
 % INPUT (opt): rotMat .. rotation matrix, columns [propagation | surface normal]
 %                       (3x2; default propagation [100], normal [001])
[vecOut, transfMat] = ElastConsts.HexagonalConversion(vecIn) 
% Convert hexagonal elastic constants (c11,c12,c13,c33,c44) to the physical-mode
 %  basis (C11,C33,G1,G2,G3): uniaxial elongations C11 (basal plane) and C33 (c-axis),
 %  and shear moduli G1 = c44 (axial shear), G2 = (c11-c12)/2 (basal shear) and
 %  G3 = (c11+c33-2*c13)/4 (breathing shear, volume-preserving c/a distortion).
 %  G1 = G2 = G3 in the isotropic limit.
 % >> [vecOut,transfMat] = HexagonalConversion(vecIn)
 %
 % INPUT:  (opt) vecIn .. hexagonal elastic constants [c11,c12,c13,c33,c44]
 %                        (5x1 or 1x5); omit (or pass []) to get the
 %                        transformation matrix alone
 % OUTPUT: 1. vecOut    .. transformed constants (C11,C33,G1,G2,G3) as a 5x1 column
 %         2. transfMat .. transformation matrix, vecOut(5x1) = transfMat(5x5)*vecIn(5x1)
[vecOut, deTransfMat] = ElastConsts.HexagonalDeconversion(vecIn) 
% De-Convert vector of hexagonal elastic constants (c11,c12,c13,c33,c44) from the
 %  physical-mode basis (C11,C33,G1,G2,G3) defined in HexagonalConversion
 % >> [vecOut,deTransfMat] = HexagonalDeconversion(vecIn)
 %
 % INPUT:  (opt) vecIn .. constants (C11,C33,G1,G2,G3) (5x1 or 1x5); omit (or pass [])
 %                        to get the de-transformation matrix alone
 % OUTPUT: 1. vecOut      .. recovered constants (c11,c12,c13,c33,c44) as a 5x1 column
 %         2. deTransfMat .. inverse of HexagonalConversion's transformation matrix
[c11_hill, c44_hill] = ElastConsts.HillAverages(elVoigt) 
% Hill averages -- arithmetic mean of the Voigt and Reuss bounds (Hill 1952), the
 %  standard practical estimate of a polycrystal's isotropic elastic moduli
 % >> [c11_hill,c44_hill] = HillAverages(elVoigt)
 %
 % INPUT:  elVoigt .. Voigt matrix of elastic constants (6x6)
 % OUTPUT: c11_hill .. M, P-wave (longitudinal) modulus
 %         c44_hill .. G, S-wave (transverse) modulus
posList = ElastConsts.IndepVoigtPositions(symmNo) 
% Grid positions of each independent Voigt constant, for symmetry class symmNo
 % >> posList = IndepVoigtPositions(symmNo)
 %
 % INPUT:  symmNo   .. symmetry class number (see SymmClassLibrary)
 % OUTPUT: posList  .. cell array of [row,col] pairs (upper-triangle 6x6 Voigt
 %                     positions), one per obj.vector entry, in the SAME order
 %                     Vector2Voigt reads inpVec(k) for that symmNo
 %
 % Derived from Vector2Voigt, the one table of the symmetry classes: constant k
 % sits at the first upper-triangle position, row by row, where the Voigt matrix
 % of the unit vector e_k holds a 1 and that of every other e_j a 0 (e.g. cubic
 % c11 at [1,1], not at [2,2]; hexagonal c66 = (c11-c12)/2 is no position).
 % Cached per class (the ElastConsts constructor calls this through Voigt2Vector).
[c11_reuss, c44_reuss] = ElastConsts.ReussAverages(elVoigt) 
% Reuss averages -- moduli of the equivalent statistically isotropic material under
 %  uniform STRESS (Reuss 1929), the lower bound (VoigtAverages gives the upper)
 % >> [c11_reuss,c44_reuss] = ReussAverages(elVoigt)
 %
 % INPUT:  elVoigt .. Voigt matrix of elastic constants (6x6)
 % OUTPUT: c11_reuss .. M, P-wave (longitudinal) modulus
 %         c44_reuss .. G, S-wave (transverse) modulus
 %
 % General-symmetry Reuss K and G from the compliance S = inv(elVoigt), combined as
 % M = K + 4/3*G; K_reuss equals Voigt2Bulk's bulk modulus.
frame = ElastConsts.RightHandFrame(twoVecs, noteMsg) 
% Build a right-hand orthonormal 3x3 frame (row vectors) from two direction vectors
 % >> frame = ElastConsts.RightHandFrame(twoVecs,noteMsg)
 %
 % INPUT:  1. twoVecs .. 2x3 or 3x2 matrix of two direction vectors; a 3x3 is also
 %                       accepted and only its first two ROWS are read (its third
 %                       row is recomputed, so handedness is imposed, not preserved)
 %  (opt)  2. noteMsg .. text of the warning ElastConsts:RightHandFrame:notPerpendicular,
 %                       raised when the two input vectors are not perpendicular
 %                       (|n1.n2| > 1e-3, i.e. when the second one gets adjusted)
 % OUTPUT: frame .. 3x3 matrix of row vectors:
 %                    row 1 -- n1, the normalised FIRST input vector (preserved)
 %                    row 2 -- n2, the second vector made exactly perpendicular to n1
 %                    row 3 -- n3 = cross(n1,n2), right-hand completion
 %
 % ROLE-AGNOSTIC by design: callers disagree on what the two vectors mean
 % (Material.CompleteDirMat passes [surface normal; propagation],
 % ElastConsts.RotateTensor passes [propagation; surface normal]), so do not add
 % role-specific behaviour here.
rotTen = ElastConsts.RotateTensor(inpTen, rotMat) 
% Rotate tensor based on the rotation matrix
 % >> rotTen = RotateTensor(inpTen,rotMat)
 % INPUT:  1. tensor 3x3x3x3
 %         2. rotation matrix (2x3/3x2, or 3x3 row vectors)
 % OUTPUT: rotated tensor 3x3x3x3
obj = obj.SetNewSymmClass(newSymmNo) 
% Rebuild the object under a different symmetry class (from its Voigt matrix)
 % >> obj = SetNewSymmClass(obj,newSymmNo)
[symmC, nConsts] = ElastConsts.SymmClassLibrary(symmNo) 
% Library of symmetry classes and number of independent constants
 % >> [symmC,nConsts] = SymmClassLibrary(symmNo)
 %
 % INPUT:  symmNo .. symmetry class number; omit to print and return all ten
 % OUTPUT: 1. symmC   .. symmetry class name (or all names, for no input)
 %         2. nConsts .. number of independent constants (or all counts)
vec = ElastConsts.Tensor2Vector(inpTen, symmNo) 
% Transform tensor of elast consts to vector of indep. consts
 % >> vec = Tensor2Vector(inpTen,symmNo)
 %
 % INPUT:  1. inpTen .. elastic tensor (3x3x3x3)
 %  (opt)  2. symmNo .. symmetry class number; inferred if omitted or 0
 % OUTPUT: vec .. row vector of independent constants
cij = ElastConsts.Tensor2Voigt(inpTen) 
% Transform elastic tensor (3x3x3x3) to Voigt matrix (6x6)
 % >> cij = Tensor2Voigt(inpTen)
E = ElastConsts.Tensor2Youngs(elTen, rotMat) 
% Young's modulus along the x1 axis of the (optionally rotated) elastic tensor
 % >> E = Tensor2Youngs(elTen,rotMat)
 %
 % INPUT:  1. elTen  .. elastic tensor (3x3x3x3)
 %  (opt)  2. rotMat .. rotation matrix (2x3/3x2, or 3x3 row vectors)
[vecOut, transfMat] = ElastConsts.TetragonalConversion(vecIn) 
% Calculate equivalent tetragonal constants B, c33, c', c'', c44, c66
 % >> [vecOut,transfMat] = TetragonalConversion(vecIn)
 %
 % INPUT:  (opt) vecIn .. tetragonal elastic constants (6x1 or 1x6); omit (or pass [])
 %                        to get the transformation matrix alone
 % OUTPUT: 1. vecOut    .. transformed constants (B,c33,c',c'',c44,c66) as a 6x1 column
 %         2. transfMat .. transformation matrix, vecOut(6x1) = transfMat(6x6)*vecIn(6x1)
[vecOut, deTransfMat] = ElastConsts.TetragonalDeconversion(vecIn) 
% De-Convert vector of tetragonal elastic constants (c11,c12,c13,c33,c44,c66) from
 %  constants (B,c33,c',c'',c44,c66) -- see TetragonalConversion for the definitions
 % >> [vecOut,deTransfMat] = TetragonalDeconversion(vecIn)
 %
 % INPUT:  (opt) vecIn .. constants (B,c33,c',c'',c44,c66) (6x1 or 1x6); omit (or pass
 %                        []) to get the de-transformation matrix alone
 % OUTPUT: 1. vecOut      .. recovered constants (c11,c12,c13,c33,c44,c66) as a 6x1 column
 %         2. deTransfMat .. inverse of TetragonalConversion's transformation matrix
cij = ElastConsts.Vector2Voigt(inpVec, symmNo) 
% Transform vector of independent elast. consts into Voigt matrix
 % >> cij = Vector2Voigt(inpVec,symmNo)
 %
 % INPUT:  1. inpVec .. vector of independent elastic constants
 %  (opt)  2. symmNo .. symmetry class number; inferred if omitted or 0
 % OUTPUT: cij .. Voigt matrix (6x6)
 %
 % The one table of the symmetry classes: IndepVoigtPositions is derived from it.
B = ElastConsts.Voigt2Bulk(elVoigt) 
% Calculate Bulk modulus
 % >> B = Voigt2Bulk(elVoigt)
 %
 % INPUT:  elVoigt .. elastic Voigt matrix (6x6)
vec = ElastConsts.Voigt2Vector(cij, symmNo) 
% Transform Voigt matrix to vector of indep. consts
 % >> vec = Voigt2Vector(cij,symmNo)
 %
 % INPUT:  1. cij    .. Voigt matrix (6x6)
 %  (opt)  2. symmNo .. symmetry class number; inferred if omitted or 0
 % OUTPUT: vec .. row vector of independent constants, in IndepVoigtPositions order
[c11_voigt, c44_voigt] = ElastConsts.VoigtAverages(elVoigt) 
% Voigt averages — the moduli of the equivalent statistically isotropic material
 % >> [c11_voigt,c44_voigt] = VoigtAverages(elVoigt)
 %
 % INPUT:  elVoigt .. Voigt matrix of elastic constants (6x6)
 % OUTPUT: c11_voigt .. M, P-wave (longitudinal) modulus
 %         c44_voigt .. G, S-wave (transverse) modulus
 % Kube & Turner 2015, Eq. (11), with its typographical errors corrected.

Material value class

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
  Material parameters for the GuliweRR Ritz-Rayleigh calculation: elastic constants,
   density, and orientation (direction matrix).
 
  Value class. Set*/Get* methods take the object; other methods are static.
 
 % GuliweRR v6.0 %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
 %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
  Properties %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Properties

obj.dirMat (default: [1 0 0;0 0 1;0 -1 0]) 
row 1 — surface normal (plane)
obj.elConsts (default: ElastConsts) 
elastic constants (ElastConsts object)
obj.name (default: "mater") 
material name
obj.rho (default: 1) 
density  [g·cm⁻³]

Methods

obj = Material(elConsts, rho, dirMat, name) 
% Initialise Material object
 % >> obj = Material(elConsts,rho,dirMat,name)
 %
 % INPUT (all opt): 1. elConsts .. ElastConsts object, numeric vector, or
 %                                 cell {consts} / {consts, symmNo}
 %                  2. rho      .. density [g·cm⁻³]
 %                  3. dirMat   .. orientation matrix (2×3, 3×2, or 3×3 row vectors)
 %                  4. name     .. material name [string]
rotMat = Material.CalcRotMat(dirMat, angleDeg) 
% Build a rotation matrix (3×2) from a direction matrix and an angle
 % >> rotMat = CalcRotMat(dirMat,angleDeg)
 %
 % INPUT:  1. dirMat   .. 3×3 direction matrix (row vectors; complete right-hand system)
 %  (opt)  2. angleDeg .. in-plane rotation angle [degrees] (default = 0)
 % OUTPUT: rotMat .. 3×2 matrix for ElastConsts.RotateTensor:
 %             column 1 — propagation direction, rotated within the surface plane
 %                        (the plane spanned by dirMat rows 2 and 3)
 %             column 2 — surface normal (dirMat row 1), the pivot; unchanged
dirMat = Material.CompleteDirMat(inp) 
% Build a right-hand orthonormal coordinate system from two direction vectors
 % >> dirMat = CompleteDirMat(inp)
 %
 % INPUT:  inp .. 2×3 or 3×2 matrix containing two row vectors [n1; n2]
 %                (a 3×3 is accepted too — only its first two rows are read, so
 %                 its third row is REBUILT right-handed, not preserved)
 % OUTPUT: dirMat .. 3×3 matrix (row vectors):
 %                     row 1 — n1 (first input vector, preserved)
 %                     row 2 — n2 (adjusted to be perpendicular to n1; warning
 %                             ElastConsts:RightHandFrame:notPerpendicular if it was not)
 %                     row 3 — n3 = cross(n1, n2) (right-hand completion)
[phi1, Phi, phi2] = Material.DCM2Euler(dcm) 
% Extract Bunge (ZXZ) Euler angles from a direction-cosine matrix
 % >> [phi1,Phi,phi2] = Material.DCM2Euler(dcm)
 %
 % INPUT:  dcm .. 3x3 direction-cosine matrix (rows normalised internally, handedness
 %                preserved as given); OR a 2x3/3x2 vector pair [n1;n2], right-hand
 %                completed via CompleteDirMat
 % OUTPUT: phi1,Phi,phi2 .. Bunge-ZXZ Euler angles [degrees], in [0,360) resp. [0,180]
 %
 % CONVENTION: g = Rz(phi2)*Rx(Phi)*Rz(phi1) transforms SPECIMEN-frame components into
 % CRYSTAL-frame components; dcm = g', so dcm's rows are the specimen axes in crystal
 % components. Row/column 3 is Bunge's distinguished ("tilt") axis.
 %
 % Raw Bunge-frame conversion: for a Material.dirMat (input coordinates, row 1 = surface
 % normal -- see docs/reference/architecture/core.md) use Material.DirMat2Euler instead.
 %
 % Do NOT call this on Material.GetStandardDirMat's output: its [3,2,1] reorder is an
 % odd permutation, so the result is improper (det = -1) and has no Bunge decomposition.
 % Angles still come back, but describe no physical rotation and will not round-trip.
 %
 % Same convention as MTEX (orientation.byEuler(...).matrix == dcm) and as the Nottingham
 % SRAS orientation output.
[phi1, Phi, phi2] = Material.DirMat2Euler(dirMat) 
% Extract Bunge (ZXZ) Euler angles from a Material.dirMat orientation
 % >> [phi1,Phi,phi2] = Material.DirMat2Euler(dirMat)
 %
 % INPUT:  dirMat .. 3x3 Convention-A direction matrix (row 1 = surface normal); OR a
 %                   2x3/3x2 vector pair [n1;n2], right-hand completed via CompleteDirMat
 % OUTPUT: phi1,Phi,phi2 .. Bunge-ZXZ Euler angles [degrees], in [0,360) resp. [0,180],
 %                          exact inverse of Material.Euler2DirMat
 %
 % Right-hand completes the input (a no-op for any genuine Material.dirMat), reorders it
 % [2,3,1] onto Bunge's row-3 tilt axis and calls Material.DCM2Euler -- call that
 % directly for a dcm already in Bunge's own row order.
 %
 % Same Bunge convention as MTEX and the Nottingham SRAS orientation output.
[hkl, residualDeg] = Material.DirMat2Miller(dirCos, symmNo, latticeParams, indexType, maxIndex) 
% Find the smallest-integer Miller index whose direction best matches a direction cosine
 % >> [hkl,residualDeg] = Material.DirMat2Miller(dirCos,symmNo,latticeParams,indexType,maxIndex)
 % >> spec = Material.DirMat2Miller(symmNo)          -- list required latticeParams fields
 % >> spec = Material.DirMat2Miller([],symmNo)       -- same, explicit form
 %
 % Indexing has no exact general solution -- an arbitrary direction need not lie along
 % any low-index crystallographic direction. ALWAYS check residualDeg.
 %
 % INPUT:  1. dirCos        .. 1x3 (or 3x1) direction-cosine vector (need not be
 %                             pre-normalised)
 %         2. symmNo        .. symmetry class number (see SymmClassLibrary)
 %  (opt)  3. latticeParams .. same as Miller2DirMat (call with dirCos=[] to see requirements)
 %  (opt)  4. indexType     .. 'direction' or 'planeNormal' (see Miller2DirMat)
 %                             [default 'direction']
 %  (opt)  5. maxIndex      .. search |h|,|k|,|l| up to this integer [default 12]
 % OUTPUT: hkl         .. best-fit small-integer Miller index -- [h k l], or Miller-Bravais
 %                        [h k i l] (i=-(h+k)) for trigonal/hexagonal with
 %                        indexType 'planeNormal'
 %         residualDeg .. angular misfit [degrees] between hkl's direction and dirCos;
 %                        0 = exact match
 %
 % Brute-force over reduced integer triples scored against Miller2DirMat: exact for all
 % 7 crystal systems, but O(maxIndex^3) -- not for a hot loop.
dcm = Material.Euler2DCM(phi1, Phi, phi2) 
% Build a direction-cosine matrix from Bunge (ZXZ) Euler angles
 % >> dcm = Material.Euler2DCM(phi1,Phi,phi2)
 %
 % INPUT:  phi1,Phi,phi2 .. Bunge-ZXZ Euler angles [degrees]
 % OUTPUT: dcm .. 3x3 direction-cosine matrix, exact inverse of Material.DCM2Euler
 %                (see there for the convention)
 %
 % Raw Bunge frame: row/column 3 is Bunge's tilt axis, NOT GULIWERR's surface normal
 % (row 1 of a Convention-A dirMat -- see docs/reference/architecture/core.md). For a
 % ready-to-use Material.dirMat, use Material.Euler2DirMat.
dirMat = Material.Euler2DirMat(phi1, Phi, phi2) 
% Build a Material.dirMat orientation from Bunge (ZXZ) Euler angles
 % >> dirMat = Material.Euler2DirMat(phi1,Phi,phi2)
 %
 % INPUT:  phi1,Phi,phi2 .. Bunge-ZXZ Euler angles [degrees]
 % OUTPUT: dirMat .. 3x3 Convention-A direction matrix (row 1 = surface normal, row 2 =
 %                   in-plane propagation reference, row 3 = completion), exact inverse
 %                   of Material.DirMat2Euler
 %
 % Reorders Material.Euler2DCM's output [3,1,2], moving Bunge's row-3 tilt axis onto
 % row 1 (surface normal) -- call Euler2DCM directly for Bunge's own row order.
[obj, entry] = Material.FromLibrary(name, dirMat, varargin) 
% Build a Material from the materials library (guliwerr/data/materialsLibrary.csv)
 % >> [obj,entry] = Material.FromLibrary(name,dirMat,'T',T,'variant',variant)
 % >> [obj,entry] = Material.FromLibrary(name,'T',T)        % dirMat may be left out
 %
 % INPUT:  1. name    .. library name or alias, case-insensitive (e.g. 'Cu', 'copper')
 %  (opt)  2. dirMat  .. orientation matrix (see Material); [] or omitted: Material default
 %  (opt)  'T'        .. temperature: number in K or 'RT'; must match a stored row
 %  (opt)  'variant'  .. variant label of the row (Material.ListLibrary shows them)
 % OUTPUT: 1. obj   .. Material (name = library name, plus the variant if not default)
 %         2. entry .. one-row table with the data set's reference, source and notes
 %
 % Without 'T' or 'variant' the material's default row is returned. For rows with status
 % 'provisional' (secondary source only, or a doubt given in the row's note) a one-line
 % note saying why is printed.
rotDirMat = obj.GetRotDirMat(angleDeg) 
% Direction matrix rotated in-plane by the given angle (row 1, the surface
 % normal, is the pivot and stays unchanged)
 % >> rotDirMat = GetRotDirMat(obj,angleDeg)
rotElTensor = obj.GetRotElastTensor(angleDeg, relativeToggle) 
% Elastic tensor (3×3×3×3) rotated by the given in-plane angle
 % >> rotElTensor = GetRotElastTensor(obj,angleDeg,relativeToggle)
 %
 % INPUT:  1. angleDeg       .. rotation angle [degrees], required (0 = the
 %                             dirMat frame; the unrotated crystal tensor is
 %                             obj.elConsts.tensor)
 %  (opt)  2. relativeToggle .. false — relative to dirMat (default); true — absolute frame
rotElVoigt = obj.GetRotElastVoigt(angleDeg, relativeToggle) 
% Voigt matrix (6×6) of the elastic tensor rotated by the given in-plane angle
 % >> rotElVoigt = GetRotElastVoigt(obj,angleDeg,relativeToggle)
 %
 % INPUT:  1. angleDeg       .. rotation angle [degrees], required (0 = the
 %                             dirMat frame; the unrotated crystal matrix is
 %                             obj.elConsts.voigt)
 %  (opt)  2. relativeToggle .. false — relative to dirMat (default); true — absolute frame
rotMat = obj.GetRotMat(angleDeg, relativeToggle) 
% Get rotation matrix for a given in-plane rotation angle
 % >> rotMat = GetRotMat(obj,angleDeg,relativeToggle)
 %
 % INPUT:  1. angleDeg       .. rotation angle [degrees]
 %  (opt)  2. relativeToggle .. logical: false — rotate within obj.dirMat (default)
 %                             true — rotate in the absolute [100]/[010] frame
 % OUTPUT: rotMat .. 3×2, columns [rotated propagation | surface normal]
stdDirMat = Material.GetStandardDirMat(dirMat, angleDeg) 
% Reorder (and optionally rotate) a direction matrix into the standard
 % x1=propagation, x2=in-plane, x3=surface-normal convention of the literature
 % (Every, Maznev), from the internal row order [plane; direction; completion]
 % >> stdDirMat = Material.GetStandardDirMat(dirMat, angleDeg)
 %
 % INPUT:  1. dirMat   .. 3×3 direction matrix (rows: [plane; direction; completion])
 %  (opt)  2. angleDeg .. in-plane rotation angle [degrees] (default = 0)
 % OUTPUT: stdDirMat .. 3×3 direction matrix, rows [completion; direction; plane]
 %
 % The angle is applied here, before the reorder, because RotDirMatFromAngle mixes
 % rows 2 & 3 — rotating an already-reordered matrix would swing the propagation
 % direction towards the surface normal. So pass angle 0 downstream, e.g.
 % Material.CalcRotMat(stdDirMat, 0).
elTensor = obj.GetStandardElastTensor(angleDeg) 
% Elastic tensor (3×3×3×3) in the standard solver frame: x1 = propagation,
 % x2 = in-plane completion, x3 = surface normal (Every/Maznev convention)
 % >> elTensor = GetStandardElastTensor(obj,angleDeg)
 %
 % INPUT (opt): angleDeg .. propagation angle [degrees] within the surface
 %                          plane, from dirMat row 2 (default = 0)
 %
 % The angle is applied once, by GetStandardDirMat; the tensor rotation then
 % takes angle 0 (passing the angle to both would rotate twice;
 % docs/pitfalls.md OTH1). The one place the solvers, the limiting
 % velocities and the plots get their standard-frame tensor from.
lib = Material.ListLibrary() 
% List the materials library (guliwerr/data/materialsLibrary.csv)
 % >> lib = Material.ListLibrary()
 %
 % OUTPUT: lib .. table, one row per data set (see Material.ReadLibraryFile);
 %                with no output argument a short overview is printed instead
dcm = Material.Miller2DirMat(hkl, symmNo, latticeParams, indexType) 
% Convert a Miller (or Miller-Bravais) index to a direction-cosine row vector
 % >> dcm = Material.Miller2DirMat(hkl,symmNo,latticeParams,indexType)
 % >> spec = Material.Miller2DirMat(symmNo)          -- list required latticeParams fields
 % >> spec = Material.Miller2DirMat([],symmNo)       -- same, explicit form
 %
 % INPUT:  1. hkl           .. Miller index [h k l]; OR Miller-Bravais [h k i l]
 %                             (i = -(h+k); trigonal/hexagonal PLANE NORMALS only)
 %         2. symmNo        .. symmetry class number (see SymmClassLibrary)
 %  (opt)  3. latticeParams .. struct of lattice-shape parameters -- ratios and angles
 %                             only, absolute scale never affects a direction cosine.
 %                             Call with hkl=[] (or just symmNo) to list the fields
 %                             symmNo requires; unrequired fields are ignored:
 %                               b_over_a, c_over_a .. axis-length ratios
 %                               alpha,beta,gamma   .. interaxial angles [degrees]
 %                                 (alpha = angle(b,c), beta = angle(a,c), gamma = angle(a,b))
 %  (opt)  4. indexType     .. 'direction' (real-space [uvw], DEFAULT) or 'planeNormal'
 %                             (reciprocal-space (hkl)). NOT parallel except for cubic
 %                             symmetry -- a surface normal is a planeNormal, a
 %                             propagation direction is a direction.
 % OUTPUT: dcm .. 1x3 unit direction-cosine row vector in the Cartesian crystallophysical
 %                frame (a along X, b in the XY-plane, c completing the cell); OR, in
 %                requirements-listing mode, the LatticeParamSpec struct
rotDirMat = Material.RotDirMatFromAngle(dirMat, angleDeg) 
% Rotate a direction matrix in-plane by the given angle
 % >> rotDirMat = RotDirMatFromAngle(dirMat,angleDeg)
 %
 % INPUT:  1. dirMat   .. 3×3 direction matrix (row vectors; complete right-hand system)
 %         2. angleDeg .. in-plane rotation angle [degrees]
 % OUTPUT: rotDirMat .. 3×3 direction matrix, rows 2 and 3 rotated by angleDeg from row 2
 %                      towards row 3 (counter-clockwise seen looking down the surface
 %                      normal, row 1, onto the surface); with Euler2DirMat this is from
 %                      specimen X towards Y. Row 1 is the pivot and stays unchanged.
 %                      Sole definition of the in-plane rotation's sign convention.
obj = obj.SetDensity(rho) 
% Set density of the material [g·cm⁻³] (positive scalar)
 % >> obj = SetDensity(obj,rho)
obj = obj.SetDirMat(matr) 
% Set orientation matrix; the result is always right-hand orthonormal
 % >> obj = SetDirMat(obj,matr)
 %
 % INPUT: matr .. 2×3, 3×2, or 3×3 matrix of direction row vectors
obj = obj.SetElastConsts(elConsts) 
% Set elastic constants of the material
 % >> obj = SetElastConsts(obj,elConsts)
 %
 % INPUT: elConsts .. ElastConsts object, numeric vector, or cell {consts[,symmNo]}
obj = obj.SetName(name) 
% Set material name
 % >> obj = SetName(obj,name)

CalcParams value class

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
  Calculation parameters for the GuliweRR Ritz-Rayleigh solver: Legendre integration
   matrices and the sampled Legendre basis.
 
  Value class. Set*/Get* methods take the object; other methods are static.
 
 % GuliweRR v6.0 %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
 %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
  Properties %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Properties

obj.basisVecL (default: 150) 
length of the full basis vector = 3 × legOrder
obj.legDiag 
Legendre integration matrix — diagonal part   (legOrder × legOrder)
obj.legNondiag 
Legendre integration matrix — non-diagonal     (legOrder × legOrder)
obj.legOrder (default: 50) 
max order of Legendre polynomials
obj.polyLeg 
Legendre-function values at spatial mesh  (legOrder × nMesh)
obj.realThickness (default: NaN) 
real thickness, same units as the wavelength Structure.ApplyWavelength is called with; NaN = unset
obj.relDepth (default: 20) 
domain depth relative to the wavelength, which is fixed at 1 throughout the solvers (|k_par| = 1)

Methods

obj = CalcParams(relDepth, legOrder, realThickness) 
% Initialise CalcParams object
 % >> obj = CalcParams(relDepth,legOrder,realThickness)
 %
 % INPUT (opt): 1. relDepth      .. relative domain depth (default = 20)
 %              2. legOrder      .. max Legendre order (default = 50)
 %              3. realThickness .. real thickness, in the units ApplyWavelength
 %                                  will use (default = NaN, unset)
pLeg = CalcParams.BasisDecomposition2D(legO, meshSpacing) 
% Sample the Legendre basis functions on a uniform mesh of [-1, 1]
 % >> pLeg = BasisDecomposition2D(legO,meshSpacing)
 %
 % INPUT:  1. legO        .. max order of the Legendre polynomial
 %  (opt)  2. meshSpacing .. spacing of the spatial discretisation (default = 0.02)
 % OUTPUT: pLeg .. normalised Legendre-function values (legO × nMesh); row k holds
 %                 degree k-1. Used for modal-shape reconstruction from RR eigenvectors.
[d, n] = CalcParams.LegendreMatrixDiagNondiag(legO) 
% Generate diagonal and non-diagonal Legendre integration matrices
 % >> [d,n] = LegendreMatrixDiagNondiag(legO)
 %
 % INPUT:  legO .. max order of the Legendre polynomials
 % OUTPUT: d    .. D_mn = integral( dP_m/dx * dP_n/dx )  (legO × legO, symmetric)
 %         n    .. N_mn = integral( dP_m/dx * P_n )      (legO × legO, lower-triangular)
 %   Polynomials are L2-normalised over [-1, 1]; both matrices are checkerboard-sparse
 %   (N nonzero only where m+n is odd, D only where m+n is even).
obj = obj.SetLegOrder(newLegOrder) 
% Set max Legendre polynomial order (positive integer) and recompute all
 % dependent matrices
 % >> obj = SetLegOrder(obj,newLegOrder)
obj = obj.SetRealThickness(newRealThickness) 
% Set real physical thickness (same units ApplyWavelength will use; NaN = unset)
 % >> obj = SetRealThickness(obj,newRealThickness)
obj = obj.SetRelDepth(newRelDepth) 
% Set relative depth of the domain (positive scalar; NaN = a film's placeholder
 % until Structure.ApplyWavelength derives it)
 % >> obj = SetRelDepth(obj,newRelDepth)

Structure value class

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
  The GuliweRR computational domain — a stack of layers, each described by a Material
   and a CalcParams object.
 
  Value class: AddLayer, RemoveLayer and ModifyLayer return the modified object.
 
 % GuliweRR v6.0 %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
 %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
  Properties %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Properties

obj.calcParams 
layer array: CalcParams(1)=bottom … CalcParams(nLay)=top
obj.mater 
layer array: Material(1)=bottom … Material(nLay)=top
obj.nLay (default: 0) 
number of layers in the structure
obj.nMaxModes (default: 500) 
maximum number of computed modes
obj.name (default: 'str 1') 
structure name
obj.strBasisVecL (default: 0) 
sum of basisVecL across all layers

Methods

obj = Structure(materIn, calcParamsIn, name, nMaxModes) 
% Initialise Structure object, optionally adding a first layer
 % >> obj = Structure(materIn,calcParamsIn,name,nMaxModes)
 %
 % INPUT (all opt): 1. materIn      .. Material for the first layer; [] adds no layer
 %                  2. calcParamsIn .. CalcParams for it (default if empty)
 %                  3. name         .. structure name [string]
 %                  4. nMaxModes    .. max number of modes to compute (default = 500)
 %
 % An empty materIn is what suppresses AddLayer, not the argument count, so
 % Structure([],[],'strRef',500) sets name/nMaxModes on an empty structure.
obj = obj.AddLayer(mater0, calcParams0) 
% Append a layer to the top of the structure
 % >> obj = AddLayer(obj,mater0,calcParams0)
 %
 % INPUT:  1. mater0      .. Material for the new layer (default if empty)
 %  (opt)  2. calcParams0 .. CalcParams for the new layer (default if empty)
obj = obj.ApplyWavelength(wavelengthReal) 
% Derive relDepth = realThickness / wavelengthReal for every FILM layer
 % >> obj = ApplyWavelength(obj,wavelengthReal)
 %
 % INPUT: wavelengthReal .. real wavelength, same units as realThickness, which
 %                          every film layer (2..nLay) must have set
 %
 % The substrate (layer 1) is skipped: legOrder stays fixed across a wavelength
 % sweep, so a derived substrate relDepth would grow arbitrarily large at a
 % long-wavelength point and undermesh the substrate's Legendre basis. Set it
 % deliberately with SetRelDepth if needed.
obj = obj.ModifyLayer(indLay, mater0, calcParams0) 
% Replace the material and/or calcParams of an existing layer
 % >> obj = ModifyLayer(obj,indLay,mater0,calcParams0)
 %
 % INPUT:  1. indLay      .. layer index to modify (empty → top-most layer)
 %         2. mater0      .. new Material (empty → keep existing)
 %  (opt)  3. calcParams0 .. new CalcParams (empty → keep existing)
obj = obj.RemoveLayer(ind) 
% Remove a layer from the structure by index
 % >> obj = RemoveLayer(obj,ind)
 %
 % INPUT: ind .. index of the layer to remove (1 = bottom, nLay = top)
tf = obj.isMirrorSymmetric(tol) 
% Is the plate invariant under the mid-plane mirror z -> D-z?
 % >> tf = isMirrorSymmetric(obj, tol)
 %
 % INPUT (opt): tol .. relative tolerance for the comparisons (default = 1e-10)
 % OUTPUT: tf .. true iff, for every i and j = nLay+1-i (the middle layer of an
 %               odd stack paired with itself), relDepth and density match and
 %               layer j's standard-frame tensor equals the MIRROR IMAGE of
 %               layer i's: C_j = M C_i M, M = diag(1,1,-1)
 %
 % This is the exact condition for the free-plate operator to commute with the
 % mirror, i.e. for every mode to have a definite mid-plane parity (u1, u2 even
 % and u3 odd, or the reverse). Pairing a layer with its mirror image, not with
 % an identical copy, makes one test cover both requirements: the stack must be
 % symmetric AND each crystal must look the same from either face -- a single
 % cubic (111) layer fails (3-fold axis along the normal, C14-type entries),
 % (001)/(110) and isotropic layers pass. It also accepts a stack of two
 % mirror-twin layers, and identical layers given by equivalent dirMats.
 %
 % The x3 mirror commutes with every in-plane rotation, so the result does not
 % depend on the propagation angle (checked at angle 0). Conservative: a plate
 % that is symmetric only after merging identical adjacent layers (e.g. one
 % material split 0.1/0.3) returns false; classifyBranches then falls back to
 % confidence, which is safe.
 %
 % Exact parity does NOT make the Lamb/TH half of the four mode-type labels
 % exact: that needs the sagittal mirror too (isSagittalMirrorSymmetric).
tf = obj.isSagittalMirrorSymmetric(angleDeg, tol) 
% Is the plate invariant under the sagittal mirror x2 -> -x2 at this angle?
 % >> tf = isSagittalMirrorSymmetric(obj, angleDeg, tol)
 %
 % INPUT: 1. angleDeg .. propagation angle [deg], as the sweeps take it
 %  (opt) 2. tol      .. relative tolerance (default = 1e-10)
 % OUTPUT: tf .. true iff every layer's standard-frame tensor at angleDeg is
 %               unchanged by M = diag(1,-1,1): C = M C M
 %
 % Where this holds, the SH (u2) motion decouples from the sagittal (u1, u3)
 % motion, so the Lamb/TH split of the mode labels is exact. Together with
 % isMirrorSymmetric the tensor is orthotropic in the propagation frame and all
 % four labels are exact. Depends on the angle (e.g. Cu(001): true along <100>
 % and <110>, false at 30 deg); layer order and thickness play no role.

ProbeKernel value class

% ProbeKernel — the wavevectors a measurement couples to: its k-space weight W(k).
 % EXPERIMENTAL: tried on Fe3Al(316) only, not validated against measurements; the
 % interface may still change.
 %
 % A measurement never excites or detects one wavevector. A finite source pattern and
 % a finite detection weight couple to a cloud of wavevectors around the nominal +-k0
 % (k0 = 2 pi / grating period), and the signal is the coherent (complex) sum
 %
 %     s(omega) = integral d^2k  W(k) G(k, omega),     W(k) = D^(k) S^(k),
 %
 % S^ / D^ the 2D Fourier transforms of the source pattern and of the detection weight
 % (any phase included, e.g. a detector placed beside the pattern). On a half-space G
 % has no length scale, G(k, omega) = g(theta, omega/k)/k, so the direction spread
 % needs one Green solve per direction and the |k| spread only resamples each
 % direction's spectrum in velocity (SpotGreen). The 1/k cancels the polar area element
 % k dk dtheta, so the quadrature weight of a node is W x dtheta x d(delta).
 %
 % This class only DESCRIBES W; it knows nothing about Green's functions. The
 % technique enters only through the constructors (ProbeKernel.tgs, ...), so the same
 % machinery serves any patterned-source / finite-detection measurement.
 %
 % Coordinates: W is a function of kappa = k / k0 in the frame of the displayed
 % direction (kappa_x along it), over the WHOLE plane -- the lobe at kappa = (+1, 0)
 % and the one at (-1, 0) are both part of the kernel. Each node is evaluated with the
 % Green component in its own propagation frame (force/displacement axes turn with
 % k), so a +-k symmetric kernel reproduces the Maznev +-k symmetrised response: a
 % point kernel with component 'G13' gives exactly G13_eff = (G13_+ - G13_-)/2, the
 % TGS response every Calc result stores. A kernel that is not +-k symmetric (a
 % detector on one side) weights the two sides differently, as it should.
 %
 % Constructors:
 %   ProbeKernel.point(component)                       -- no smearing (reference)
 %   ProbeKernel.gaussian(sigmaRelK, sigmaAngDeg, ...)  -- generic, for fitting
 %   ProbeKernel.tgs(gratingPeriod, sourceRadii, probeRadii, ...) -- from geometry
 %   ProbeKernel.sras(gratingPeriod, nFringes, ...)     -- binary mask + detector beside it
 %   ProbeKernel.fromRealSpace(sourceImg, detectorImg, pixelSize, gratingPeriod, ...)
 %                                                      -- any pattern, e.g. a camera image
 %   ProbeKernel(weightFcn, angHalfWidth, relKHalfWidth, ...)     -- any W
 %
 % Value class (a copy, not a shared reference, travels with every assignment).
 % Half-space use only for now (SpotGreen); a layered stack would need extra
 % solves for the |k| spread, since there |k| sets kd.
 %
 % GuliweRR v6.0

Properties

ProbeKernel.MAX_DIR_STEP (default: 0.5) 
deg -- default direction step ceiling
ProbeKernel.NICE_DIR_STEPS (default: [0.5 0.25 0.2 0.125 0.1 0.05 0.025 0.02 0.0125 0.01 0.005]) 
deg -- default direction steps are taken from this list (1/step an integer), so integer-degree angles share the solved directions in a sweep
ProbeKernel.SAMPLES_PER_OSC (default: 8) 
sras / fromRealSpace: default quadrature (and stored grid) step = the kernel's shortest oscillation / this
ProbeKernel.SRAS_HARMONICS (default: 7) 
sras: mask harmonics |n| <= this summed (their tails reach the fundamental lobes)
ProbeKernel.STEPS_PER_SIGMA (default: 4) 
default quadrature: at least 4 nodes per sigma
ProbeKernel.TRUNC_SIGMA (default: 3) 
Gaussian constructors: nodes out to 3 sigma
obj.angHalfWidth (default: 0) 
deg -- nodes within +-angHalfWidth of 0 and of 180
obj.component (default: 'G13') 
Green component, own propagation frame: 'GIJ', I = force, J = displacement (1 = propagation, 3 = surface normal)
obj.dirStep (default: 1) 
deg -- default direction step of the quadrature
obj.name (default: 'point') 
short label (plots, records)
obj.params (default: struct) 
the constructor's physical inputs, for the record
obj.relKHalfWidth (default: 0) 
nodes at |k|/k0 - 1 within +-relKHalfWidth
obj.relKStep (default: 1) 
default relative |k| step of the quadrature
obj.weightFcn 
W(kx, ky): complex weight, kappa = k/k0 in the displayed direction's frame, whole plane; elementwise on equal-size arrays

Methods

obj = ProbeKernel(weightFcn, angHalfWidth, relKHalfWidth, dirStep, relKStep, component, name, params) 
% A kernel from any weight function
 % >> k = ProbeKernel(weightFcn, angHalfWidth, relKHalfWidth, dirStep, relKStep)
 % >> k = ProbeKernel(weightFcn, angHalfWidth, relKHalfWidth, dirStep, relKStep, component, name, params)
 %
 % INPUT:  1. weightFcn     .. @(kx, ky) complex W, kappa = k/k0 in the displayed
 %                             direction's frame (kx along it), whole plane --
 %                             include the -k0 lobe if the measurement has one
 %         2. angHalfWidth  .. deg, >= 0: nodes within +-this of 0 and of 180 deg
 %         3. relKHalfWidth .. >= 0, < 1: nodes at |kappa| - 1 within +-this
 %         4. dirStep       .. deg, > 0: default direction step (ignored if
 %                             angHalfWidth = 0)
 %         5. relKStep      .. > 0: default |k| step (ignored if relKHalfWidth = 0)
 %  (opt)  6. component     .. 'GIJ', own propagation frame (default 'G13')
 %  (opt)  7. name          .. label (default 'custom')
 %  (opt)  8. params        .. struct of physical inputs, for the record
s = ProbeKernel.defaultDirStep(sigmaAngDeg) 
% The largest NICE_DIR_STEPS entry <= sigma / STEPS_PER_SIGMA (and <= MAX_DIR_STEP)
s = ProbeKernel.defaultRelKStep(sigmaRelK) 
% sigma / STEPS_PER_SIGMA (SpotGreen.options caps it at Eta / ETA_STEPS)
k = ProbeKernel.fromRealSpace(sourceImg, detectorImg, pixelSize, gratingPeriod, varargin) 
% Kernel from real-space images of the source pattern and of the detection weight
 % EXPERIMENTAL: checked against ProbeKernel.sras only.
 % >> k = ProbeKernel.fromRealSpace(sourceImg, detectorImg, pixelSize, gratingPeriod)
 % >> k = ProbeKernel.fromRealSpace(..., 'LobeRadius', 0.4, 'KnifeEdgeDir', 0, ...)
 %
 % INPUT:  1. sourceImg   .. real matrix, source intensity S(y, x) (rows = y, columns
 %                           = x; the grating vector k0 along +x)
 %         2. detectorImg .. real matrix of the same size, detection weight D(y, x)
 %                           (e.g. the probe spot); the two share one pixel grid,
 %                           whose origin does not matter
 %         3. pixelSize   .. pixel pitch (any length unit)
 %         4. gratingPeriod .. Lambda, same unit
 %  (opt)  Name-Value:
 %         'LobeRadius'   .. W is kept within this distance (in k0) of +-k0 (default
 %                           0.4; < 0.9)
 %         'KnifeEdgeDir', 'KFactors', 'Component', 'DirStep', 'RelKStep' .. as
 %                           ProbeKernel.sras
 %
 % W(k) = S^(k) D^(-k), with S^(k) = sum S e^{-i k.r} evaluated by a separable direct
 % DFT on a Cartesian grid around each lobe (no padding, exact at the grid points),
 % then interpolated (cubic) for the quadrature nodes. The grid and default steps
 % resolve the shortest oscillation the images can produce, Lambda / L_support (L =
 % diagonal of the box holding every pixel above 1e-3 of either image's maximum),
 % with SAMPLES_PER_OSC points. Knife-edge and |k| factors are applied analytically
 % on top, as in sras. Pixels are treated as point samples.
k = ProbeKernel.gaussian(sigmaRelK, sigmaAngDeg, varargin) 
% A +-k symmetric Gaussian kernel in |k| and direction (polar form)
 % >> k = ProbeKernel.gaussian(sigmaRelK, sigmaAngDeg)
 % >> k = ProbeKernel.gaussian(sigmaRelK, sigmaAngDeg, 'Component', 'G13', 'DirStep', 0.25, 'RelKStep', 1e-3)
 %
 % INPUT:  1. sigmaRelK   .. std of |k|/k0 (relative velocity smearing), >= 0
 %         2. sigmaAngDeg .. std of the direction [deg], >= 0
 %  (opt)  Name-Value:
 %         'Component'    .. default 'G13'
 %         'DirStep', 'RelKStep' .. quadrature steps (default: >= STEPS_PER_SIGMA
 %                 nodes per sigma, direction step from NICE_DIR_STEPS; SpotGreen
 %                 further caps the |k| step at its damping / 4)
 %
 % W = exp(-(|kappa| - 1)^2 / (2 sigmaRelK^2) - dphi^2 / (2 sigmaAng^2)) around
 % each of the two lobes (dphi from 0 or 180 deg); a zero sigma is a delta in
 % that coordinate. Nodes out to TRUNC_SIGMA sigma.
k = ProbeKernel.point(component) 
% No smearing: the nominal +-k0 only (the reference, = the plain Green map)
 % >> k = ProbeKernel.point()            % 'G13': gives G13_eff
 % >> k = ProbeKernel.point('G33')
q = obj.sample(dirStep, relKStep) 
% Quadrature nodes and normalised complex weights of the kernel
 % >> q = k.sample()
 % >> q = k.sample(dirStep, relKStep)
 %
 % INPUT:  (opt) 1. dirStep  .. deg (default obj.dirStep)
 %         (opt) 2. relKStep .. (default obj.relKStep)
 % OUTPUT: q .. struct:
 %              offsets  [1 x nO] deg -- node directions relative to the displayed
 %                       one: (-m:m) x dirStep around 0, then the same around 180
 %              dirIndex [1 x nO] integer -m..m of each offset within its lobe
 %              delta    [nD x 1] -- |k|/k0 - 1 = (-n:n)' x relKStep
 %              kIndex   [nD x 1] integer -n..n
 %              w        [nD x nO] complex, sum(abs(w(:))) = 1
 %              dirStep, relKStep -- the steps used
 %
 % Weight = W(kappa) x dirStep x relKStep at kappa = (1 + delta)(cos o, sin o): the
 % polar area element k dk dtheta and G's 1/k cancel (see the class header).
 % Normalised by the total |w| so that a ridge flat in angle and |k| keeps its
 % height when the kernel has one phase (Gaussian TGS: the result at zero width
 % is exactly G_eff); a kernel with a phase ramp is reduced by its own
 % incoherence, which is physical. Directions or |k| rows whose weight is below
 % 1e-9 of the largest are dropped (they cost a solve each and add nothing).
k = ProbeKernel.sras(gratingPeriod, nFringes, varargin) 
% sras kernel: binary fringes in a disc, point-like detector beside the pattern
 % EXPERIMENTAL: not yet compared with a measured sras map; the source,
 % detection and beam models are assumptions (see the end of this help).
 % >> k = ProbeKernel.sras(gratingPeriod, nFringes)
 % >> k = ProbeKernel.sras(gratingPeriod, nFringes, 'DetectorOffset', [x y], 'KnifeEdgeDir', 0, ...)
 %
 % INPUT:  1. gratingPeriod .. Lambda (any length unit)
 %         2. nFringes      .. fringe periods across the disc diameter: R = nFringes
 %                             Lambda / 2
 %  (opt)  Name-Value (lengths in the unit of gratingPeriod):
 %         'DutyCycle'      .. bright fraction of a period, (0, 1) (default 0.5)
 %         'DetectorOffset' .. [along k, across k] from the disc centre (default
 %                             [1.1 R, 0]: on the centre line, beside the disc)
 %         'DetectorRadius' .. 1/e^2 intensity radius of the probe spot (finite, default 0)
 %         'KnifeEdgeDir'   .. deg from k: the knife edge measures the surface slope
 %                             along this direction (default 0); NaN = the detector
 %                             measures the displacement itself (no slope factor)
 %         'KFactors'       .. true (default): amplitude factors |k| of the
 %                             thermoelastic force (i k T) and of the slope
 %                             (i k.n); false: their directions only
 %         'EdgeBlur'       .. std of a Gaussian blur of the imaged mask (default 0)
 %         'BeamRadius'     .. 1/e^2 intensity radius of a Gaussian illuminating beam,
 %                             centred on the disc (default Inf: flat-top)
 %         'Rings'          .. Airy rings kept around each lobe (default 2): the
 %                             lobe is cut on a zero of the disc's transform
 %         'Component'      .. default 'G13'
 %         'DirStep', 'RelKStep' .. quadrature steps (default: SAMPLES_PER_OSC nodes
 %                             per shortest oscillation of W; SpotGreen caps the |k|
 %                             step at its damping / 4)
 %
 % Source: the mask's Fourier series, sq(x) = D + sum_n sin(n pi D)/(n pi) e^{i n k0 x}
 % (bright stripe centred on the disc), times the disc, so each harmonic is an Airy
 % lobe A(|k - n k0 x^|), A(q) = 2 J1(qR)/(qR). Harmonics |n| <= SRAS_HARMONICS are
 % summed, so the tails of the DC and 3k0 lobes reach the fundamental lobes as they
 % should; the 3k0 lobe itself (apparent velocity 3v) and, for D ~= 0.5, the 2k0 one
 % (apparent 2v) are outside the kernel (relKHalfWidth < 1) and not modelled.
 % A Gaussian beam (BeamRadius w) makes the envelope exp(-2 r^2 / w^2) inside the disc:
 % each lobe is then its Hankel transform (tabulated once per kernel, normalised to 1
 % at q = 0) instead of the Airy pattern. A soft envelope is what lowers the Airy rings,
 % i.e. the side lobes of the measured line; EdgeBlur cannot do it without washing out
 % the fringes (it acts at |k| ~ k0, nearly constant over the lobe).
 % Detection: a probe spot at x_d gives D^(-k) = e^{i k.x_d} exp(-k^2 r_d^2 / 8), the
 % knife edge a factor i k.n. The phase ramp selects the lobe whose packet travels
 % towards the detector: the other lobe cancels coherently, so sras sees the
 % one-sided G(+k) towards the detector, not the symmetrised G13_eff. W is cut to
 % zero outside a circle of radius JINC_ZEROS(Rings + 1) / (k0 R) around each lobe
 % (sum |W| over Airy rings does not converge, so the cut fixes the normalisation),
 % or 4 sigma of the beam's Gaussian lobe, Lambda / (pi w), if that is larger.
 % Depends only on nFringes, x_d / Lambda and r_d / Lambda: a half-space has no
 % length scale. ASSUMPTIONS (unchecked against a measured sras map): thermoelastic
 % in-plane force along k (own-frame G13), knife edge = surface slope, |k| amplitude
 % factors, Gaussian beam (BeamRadius) centred on the disc.
k = ProbeKernel.tgs(gratingPeriod, sourceRadii, probeRadii, varargin) 
% Transient-grating kernel from the measurement geometry
 % >> k = ProbeKernel.tgs(gratingPeriod, sourceRadii, probeRadii)
 % >> k = ProbeKernel.tgs(gratingPeriod, sourceRadii, probeRadii, 'Component', 'G13', 'DirStep', 0.25)
 %
 % INPUT:  1. gratingPeriod .. Lambda (any length unit)
 %         2. sourceRadii   .. 1/e^2 INTENSITY radius of the excitation spot, same
 %                             unit: scalar (circular) or [along k, across k]
 %         3. probeRadii    .. the same for the detection (probe/reference) spot;
 %                             [] = a detection weight much wider than the source
 %  (opt)  Name-Value 'Component', 'DirStep', 'RelKStep' .. as ProbeKernel.gaussian
 %
 % Interference fringes under a Gaussian envelope, I = E (1 + cos k0 x), have the
 % spectrum E^(k -+ k0)/2: a Gaussian cloud around each of +-k0, with
 % sigma_k = 2 / radius for E = exp(-2 x^2/a^2 - 2 y^2/b^2). The detection weight
 % (probe field x reference field, both from the probe spot) multiplies it, so
 % 1/sigma^2 adds: in units of k0,
 %     sigmaRelK = Lambda / (pi sqrt(a_s^2 + a_p^2))   (|k| spread, along k)
 %     sigmaAng  = Lambda / (pi sqrt(b_s^2 + b_p^2))   [rad] (direction spread)
 % The exact Cartesian Gaussian is used (not its polar approximation). Both
 % lobes have the same weight: the zero-width limit is the G13_eff convention.
 % UNCHECKED against a measured tgs map; the detection-weight model (heterodyne,
 % reference sharing the probe spot) is an assumption.
component = ProbeKernel.validateComponent(component) 
% 'GIJ' with I, J in 1..3 (own propagation frame, one-sided notation)
 % >> component = ProbeKernel.validateComponent('G13')

ParallelPolicy value class

% ParallelPolicy — shared parallel-execution policy for GuliweRR sweep classes
 %
 % Two decisions, made in one place for every sweep class: whether a loop of
 % independent iterations runs in parallel ("auto"/"always"/"never"), and
 % acquisition of a MATLAB THREAD pool. Threads, not processes, because a
 % thread pool shares memory instead of copying broadcast data (elastic
 % tensors, CalcParams, rawByDepthCell) into every worker.
 %
 % Stateless static class.
 %
 % GuliweRR v6.0

Properties

ParallelPolicy.AUTO_THRESHOLD (default: 20) 
"auto" goes parallel once nItems >= this; a single benchmarked threshold, not scaled by problem size

Methods

obj = ParallelPolicy() 
% ParallelPolicy — shared parallel-execution policy for GuliweRR sweep classes
 %
 % Two decisions, made in one place for every sweep class: whether a loop of
 % independent iterations runs in parallel ("auto"/"always"/"never"), and
 % acquisition of a MATLAB THREAD pool. Threads, not processes, because a
 % thread pool shares memory instead of copying broadcast data (elastic
 % tensors, CalcParams, rawByDepthCell) into every worker.
 %
 % Stateless static class.
 %
 % GuliweRR v6.0
ok = ParallelPolicy.ensureThreadPool() 
% Make a MATLAB thread pool the active parallel pool, starting one if needed
 % and swapping out any active process pool
 % >> ok = ParallelPolicy.ensureThreadPool()
 %
 % OUTPUT: ok .. true if a thread pool is ready; false (with a warning, not an
 %               error) if parallel execution is unavailable here — callers
 %               must treat false as "run serial"
 %
 % An active process pool is deleted to make room (MATLAB allows one pool),
 % with the warning ParallelPolicy:ensureThreadPool:replacedPool.
tf = ParallelPolicy.isOnWorker() 
% True when running inside a parallel worker (thread or process pool)
 % >> tf = ParallelPolicy.isOnWorker()
 %
 % OUTPUT: tf .. true on a pool worker, false on the client (or without the
 %               Parallel Computing Toolbox)
 %
 % A loop that goes parallel on its own (e.g. GreenScore.reference) must stay
 % serial on a worker: there is no pool there (gcp is empty), and
 % ensureThreadPool would try to start one. getCurrentTask does not work in a
 % thread pool (it errors), so thread workers are recognised by MATLAB's
 % internal parallel.internal.pool.isPoolThreadWorker (present in R2025b) and
 % process workers by getCurrentTask. Any failure means "client".
tf = ParallelPolicy.shouldUseParallel(useParallelSetting, nItems) 
% Decide whether a loop of nItems independent iterations should run in parallel
 % >> tf = ParallelPolicy.shouldUseParallel(useParallelSetting, nItems)
 %
 % INPUT:  1. useParallelSetting .. "auto" | "always" | "never"
 %         2. nItems             .. number of independent loop iterations
 % OUTPUT: tf .. true/false
solvers/christoffel

Christoffel solver

Christoffel value class

% Static methods for bulk-wave Christoffel calculations.
 % All methods are static — do not instantiate this class.
 %
 % Usage:  vels = Christoffel.phaseVels(elTensor, dirVec)
 %         [vG, vP] = Christoffel.groupVelVectorsForDir(elTensor, dirVec)
 %
 % Input convention:
 %   phaseVelScan takes a Material object; the other methods (matrix,
 %   phaseVels, phaseVelAngleDeriv, groupVelVectorsForDir) take tensor + rho
 %   directly.
 %
 %   Exception: phaseVelAngleDeriv takes its angle in RADIANS, not degrees
 %   (angle-derivative formulas are radian-native) — see its own docblock.
 %
 % GuliweRR v6.0

Methods

obj = Christoffel() 
% Static methods for bulk-wave Christoffel calculations.
 % All methods are static — do not instantiate this class.
 %
 % Usage:  vels = Christoffel.phaseVels(elTensor, dirVec)
 %         [vG, vP] = Christoffel.groupVelVectorsForDir(elTensor, dirVec)
 %
 % Input convention:
 %   phaseVelScan takes a Material object; the other methods (matrix,
 %   phaseVels, phaseVelAngleDeriv, groupVelVectorsForDir) take tensor + rho
 %   directly.
 %
 %   Exception: phaseVelAngleDeriv takes its angle in RADIANS, not degrees
 %   (angle-derivative formulas are radian-native) — see its own docblock.
 %
 % GuliweRR v6.0
[vG, vP, eigVecsOut] = Christoffel.groupVelVectorsForDir(elTensor, dirVec, rho) 
% Bulk group-velocity vectors for an ARBITRARY 3D direction
 % >> [vG, vP, eigVecsOut] = Christoffel.groupVelVectorsForDir(elTensor, dirVec, rho)
 %
 % INPUT:  1. elTensor .. 3x3x3x3 tensor of elastic constants
 %         2. dirVec   .. propagation direction (3-vector, need not be normalised)
 %  (opt)  3. rho      .. density (default = 1)
 % OUTPUT: vG         .. 3x3, columns are group velocity vectors [qL, qT1, qT2]
 %         vP         .. [3x1] phase velocities, descending
 %         eigVecsOut .. 3x3, columns are polarisation eigenvectors
 %
 % Royer–Dieulesaint Eq. 4.24 contraction, vG_i = C(i,j,k,l) g(j) g(k) s(l) / rho
 % (g the polarisation, s = n / vP the slowness), for an arbitrary 3D
 % direction, e.g. full-sphere ray surfaces. See docs/reference/architecture/christoffel.md.
[eigVals, eigVecs, GammaMat] = Christoffel.matrix(elTensor, dirVec, rho, normalizeDir) 
% Christoffel matrix and its eigendecomposition
 % >> [eigVals, eigVecs, GammaMat] = Christoffel.matrix(elTensor, dirVec, rho, normalizeDir)
 %
 % INPUT:  1. elTensor    .. 3×3×3×3 tensor of elastic constants
 %         2. dirVec      .. propagation direction (3-vector)
 %  (opt)  3. rho         .. density (default = 1; pass 1 for density-normalised input)
 %  (opt)  4. normalizeDir .. normalise dirVec before use (default = true)
 % OUTPUT: 1. eigVals    .. eigenvalues sorted descending (= rho*v_P^2 for L, T1, T2)
 %         2. eigVecs    .. corresponding eigenvectors as columns (polarisations)
 %         3. GammaMat   .. 3×3 Christoffel matrix
[vels, dVels, eigVecs] = Christoffel.phaseVelAngleDeriv(elTensor, angleRad, rho) 
% Phase velocities and their first derivative w.r.t. sagittal-plane angle
 % >> [vels, dVels, eigVecs] = Christoffel.phaseVelAngleDeriv(elTensor, angleRad, rho)
 %
 % INPUT:  1. elTensor  .. 3×3×3×3 tensor of elastic constants
 %         2. angleRad  .. sagittal-plane angle IN RADIANS (direction
 %                         [cos; 0; sin]), unlike phaseVelScan's degrees.
 %                         Angle-derivative formulas are radian-native; mixing
 %                         the two costs a silent factor of 180/pi.
 %  (opt)  3. rho        .. density (default = 1)
 % OUTPUT: 1. vels   .. [3×1] phase velocities [vL; vT1; vT2], descending
 %         2. dVels  .. [3×1] d(vels)/d(angleRad), same order
 %         3. eigVecs .. [3×3] polarisations (columns, same order) -- exactly what
 %                       Christoffel.matrix returns along [cos; 0; sin], from the same solve
 %
 % Derivation: docs/reference/architecture/christoffel.md.
vels = Christoffel.phaseVelScan(mater, angleDeg) 
% Phase velocities scanned over rotation angles for a Material object
 % >> vels = Christoffel.phaseVelScan(mater, angleDeg)
 %
 % INPUT:  1. mater    .. Material object
 %  (opt)  2. angleDeg .. rotation angles in degrees (default = 0)
 % OUTPUT: vels(wavetype, angle) — [vL; vT1; vT2] at each angle
vels = Christoffel.phaseVels(elTensor, dirVec, rho) 
% Phase velocities for given tensor and direction
 % >> vels = Christoffel.phaseVels(elTensor, dirVec, rho)
 %
 % INPUT:  1. elTensor .. 3×3×3×3 tensor of elastic constants
 %         2. dirVec   .. propagation direction (3-vector)
 %  (opt)  3. rho      .. density (default = 1)
 % OUTPUT: [vL; vT1; vT2] phase velocities, sorted descending

LimitingVels value class

% Static methods for computing limiting bulk-wave velocities.
 %
 % Limiting velocities belong to the bulk waves with wavevector in the sagittal
 % plane whose group velocity there has no surface-normal component (vg2 = 0):
 % the energy flux runs parallel to the surface, and the limiting velocity is
 % its along-surface component vg1, equal to the phase velocity along the
 % surface, v/cos(theta*). The lowest one bounds the existence domain of SAW.
 %   findRoots  — limiting velocities for one pre-rotated tensor (the
 %                rotation to the standard frame is the caller's, e.g.
 %                CalcCommon.limitingAndBulk)
 %   refineRoot — re-find ONE already-located root in a small bracket
 %
 % GuliweRR v6.0

Methods

obj = LimitingVels() 
% Static methods for computing limiting bulk-wave velocities.
 %
 % Limiting velocities belong to the bulk waves with wavevector in the sagittal
 % plane whose group velocity there has no surface-normal component (vg2 = 0):
 % the energy flux runs parallel to the surface, and the limiting velocity is
 % its along-surface component vg1, equal to the phase velocity along the
 % surface, v/cos(theta*). The lowest one bounds the existence domain of SAW.
 %   findRoots  — limiting velocities for one pre-rotated tensor (the
 %                rotation to the standard frame is the caller's, e.g.
 %                CalcCommon.limitingAndBulk)
 %   refineRoot — re-find ONE already-located root in a small bracket
 %
 % GuliweRR v6.0
B = LimitingVels.canonicalBasis(E) 
% Basis-independent orthonormal basis of the span of orthonormal columns E
 % >> B = LimitingVels.canonicalBasis(E)
 %
 % From the projector P = E E' (the same matrix for ANY orthonormal basis of
 % the subspace, to round-off): take P's longest column (ties within 1e-10
 % relative -> the lowest index), normalise it, deflate P by it, repeat. Its
 % own entry P(c,c) > 0 fixes the sign. Cu(001) at 0 deg (eigenspace y-z)
 % gives (0,1,0) then (0,0,1).
[vLim_sA, eigenVecs_sA, thetaRoots_sA] = LimitingVels.findRoots(rotElTensor, rho, alphaPrecision, relTol) 
% Limiting velocities for a single pre-rotated elastic tensor
 % >> [vLim_sA, eigenVecs_sA, thetaRoots_sA] = LimitingVels.findRoots(rotElTensor, rho, alphaPrecision, relTol)
 %
 % INPUT:  1. rotElTensor    .. 3×3×3×3 rotated elastic tensor
 %  (opt)  2. rho            .. density (default = 1)
 %  (opt)  3. alphaPrecision .. sagittal-plane scan step [rad] (default = 1e-2)
 %  (opt)  4. relTol         .. accept threshold, relative to the in-plane
 %                              group-velocity magnitude at the candidate
 %                              root (default = 1e-3)
 % OUTPUT: 1. vLim_sA{iwt}      .. limiting velocities for wave type iwt
 %         2. eigenVecs_sA{iwt}  .. corresponding eigenvectors
 %         3. thetaRoots_sA{iwt} .. sagittal-plane angle theta* [rad] of each
 %                                 accepted root, SAME ORDER as vLim_sA{iwt}
 %
 % Coarse islocalmin scan over |vg2|, then a bounded fminbnd
 % refinement of every candidate before the accept/reject test.
 % islocalmin (rather than a sign change) is what also catches
 % tangential touches — bulk waves grazing near-zero out-of-plane
 % group velocity without crossing sign, a real surface-skimming
 % feature. Algorithm: docs/reference/architecture/christoffel.md.
 %
 % fminbnd runs at TolX 1e-12, not its default 1e-4 rad. At a steep
 % sign crossing the residual |vg2| left by a theta error is slope x
 % error: a strongly anisotropic cubic can cross at |dvg2/dtheta| of tens
 % of km/s/rad, where the default TolX leaves a residual above the accept
 % tolerance relTol x vg1 and a genuine root would be rejected. The theta
 % error would also move the accepted velocity, as vg1 is not stationary
 % at the root. Precision actually reached: fminbnd stops at
 % sqrt(eps)|theta| + TolX/3, so the relative term (~1.5e-8 rad at theta ~ 1)
 % dominates and a TolX below ~1e-9 changes nothing; theta* is good to about
 % 1e-8 rad, enough for the accept test and for refineRoot's finite
 % differences at steps >= 1e-3 GPa.
 %
 % thetaRoots_sA lets a caller anchor a root by its theta* and
 % re-find it with refineRoot instead of re-running the whole scan.
[vLim, thetaRoot, pol, residual, ok] = LimitingVels.refineRoot(rotElTensor, rho, thetaGuess, iwt, halfWidth, relTol) 
% Re-find ONE already-located sagittal-plane root in a small bracket
 % >> [vLim, thetaRoot, pol, residual, ok] = LimitingVels.refineRoot(rotElTensor, rho, thetaGuess, iwt, halfWidth, relTol)
 %
 % INPUT:  1. rotElTensor .. 3×3×3×3 rotated elastic tensor
 %         2. rho         .. density
 %         3. thetaGuess  .. sagittal-plane angle [rad] of the root being
 %                           tracked, from the previous accepted iterate
 %         4. iwt         .. wave-type branch index (1 = longitudinal,
 %                           2 = fast shear, 3 = slow shear), the same
 %                           descending-eigenvalue convention findRoots uses
 %  (opt)  5. halfWidth   .. bracket half-width [rad] (default = 0.05, about
 %                           2.9 deg, five steps of findRoots' coarse grid)
 %  (opt)  6. relTol      .. accept threshold, relative to the in-plane
 %                           group-velocity magnitude (default = 1e-3, the
 %                           same test findRoots applies)
 % OUTPUT: 1. vLim      .. limiting velocity at the refined root
 %         2. thetaRoot .. refined theta* [rad]
 %         3. pol       .. [3×1] polarisation at the refined root
 %         4. residual  .. |vg2| at the refined root
 %         5. ok        .. accept test passed. FALSE is the
 %                         root-DISAPPEARANCE detector: a limiting root
 %                         can vanish outright under a small change in
 %                         the elastic constants, and then the minimum of
 %                         |vg2| inside the bracket stops being near zero.
 %                         A caller MUST check it and fall back to a full
 %                         findRoots rescan; vLim is meaningless when false.
 %
 % Use this, not findRoots, inside a finite-difference derivative.
 % findRoots brackets each root from whichever coarse-grid cell islocalmin
 % flagged, which makes the returned velocity a non-smooth function of the
 % elastic constants at the 1e-4 GPa scale a central finite difference
 % perturbs them by — a tiny elasticity change can move the flagged cell
 % by one step and jump the bracket. Here the bracket is fixed by the
 % CALLER (identical for the +h and -h evaluations), which makes the value
 % smooth and the difference valid.
 %
 % Freezing theta* instead and differentiating only the explicit
 % c-dependence is NOT a valid shortcut: v_lim = v_g1(theta*(c), c) and
 % v_g1 is not stationary in theta at the root (only v_g2 is), so the
 % dtheta*/dc term is not small — it can exceed the frozen-theta term
 % several-fold.
solvers/green

Green's function solver

GreenSolver value class

% Surface dynamic Green's function G_IJ(k∥, ω) for anisotropic solids.
 % Half-space and layered geometry (Every et al. 1997, Every 2002, Maznev et al. 1999).
 %
 % Value class — copy-on-modify semantics.
 %
 % Usage:
 %   obj = GreenSolver.calc(structure, angle, velRange)
 %   obj = GreenSolver.calc(structure, angle, velRange, 3, 3)   % G_33 (surface Brillouin scattering)
 %   obj = GreenSolver.calc(structure, angle, velRange, 1, 3)   % G_13 (TGS)
 %   obj = GreenSolver.calc(structure, angle, velRange, 3, 3, 'ComputeMinus', false)
 %       % leave G_minus/G_eff (and their GFull_ forms) empty. Costs the same
 %       % either way -- G_− is read off G_+ by reciprocity, not solved.
 %       % obj.GFull(I,J,:) gives every component's G_+.
 %   obj = GreenSolver.calc(structure, 0, velAxis, 1, 3, 'Eta', 3e-3)
 %       % larger limiting-absorption damping, emulating a finite measurement
 %       % linewidth (default 3e-4). CalcCommon.greenScoreSpectrum uses this call
 %       % (with eta = SawSpectrum.ETA) and reads obj.G_eff (G13_eff).
 %   obj = GreenSolver.calc(structure, 0, velAxis, 3, 3, 'ScanMode', 'isoFreq', 'Omega0', w0)
 %       % fixed frequency, swept |k∥| (layered dispersion)
 %
 % Output:
 %   obj.G       — G_IJ(+k∥)  (complex), for the requested (forceDir,displDir)
 %   obj.G_minus — G_IJ(−k∥)  (complex) = G_JI(+k∥), by elastodynamic
 %                 reciprocity (exact for any elastic tensor, cut and layer
 %                 stack); empty if computeMinus=false
 %   obj.G_eff   — (G_+ + (-1)^n * G_−) / 2, n = number of (I,J) equal to the
 %                 propagation index (Maznev 1999, parity-signed), i.e.
 %                 (G_IJ + (-1)^n * G_JI) / 2 at +k∥ -- e.g. G13_eff =
 %                 (G_13 - G_31)/2; empty if computeMinus=false. Diagonal
 %                 pairs (I=J, e.g. G_33): G_eff = G_+ exactly. Off-diagonal
 %                 pairs (e.g. G_13, G_23): G_eff = G_+ only where k1->-k1 is a
 %                 true symmetry of that direction. A two-fold axis along the
 %                 surface normal (every cubic (001) and (110) face, isotropic,
 %                 hexagonal with c along the normal) makes this true at ALL
 %                 in-plane angles: the rotation R = diag(-1,-1,1) maps k to -k
 %                 and gives G_IJ(-k) = s_I s_J G_IJ(k), s = (-1,-1,+1), so with
 %                 reciprocity G_13 = -G_31 and G13_eff = G_13 exactly, while
 %                 G_12 = G_21 and G_23 = -G_32 give G12_eff = G23_eff = 0
 %                 identically (G_12, G_23 themselves are not zero). See
 %                 docs/reference/architecture/green.md, "±k symmetrisation".
 %   obj.GFull, obj.GFull_minus, obj.GFull_eff — the SAME three quantities for
 %                 ALL nine (forceDir,displDir) pairs at once, [3×3×nVel];
 %                 GFull_minus = GFull transposed in (I,J). GFull_minus/
 %                 GFull_eff follow the same computeMinus=false emptiness rule
 %                 as G_minus/G_eff.
 %   obj.computeMinus — whether the G_−/G_eff fields were filled
 %
 % Optional full output ([obj, ~] = GreenSolver.calc(...), intended for a SCALAR
 % velocity): asking for a second output switches it on, even when it is
 % discarded with ~ (that is the intended call), and it is large (the half-space
 % dispField is 6003 x 3 x 3 x nVel complex). Half-space -> obj.dispField/
 % .depthAxis; layered (structure.nLay>1) -> obj.k3OutLayered/
 % .polarizationULayered/.partialAmplitudeLayered/.hScaleOut instead, with no
 % dispField grid (Vis.calcGreenDispField2DLayered reconstructs the depth
 % profile from these plus the caller's Structure).
 %
 % Coordinate system:
 %   Standard convention throughout GULIWERR (Material.GetStandardDirMat, applied
 %   inside calc.m): I/J = 1 → propagation direction, 3 → surface normal.
 %   Default I=1, J=3 gives G_13 — the TGS component (horizontal force, vertical disp).
 %
 % GuliweRR v6.0

Properties

obj.G (default: zeros(1,0)) 
G_IJ(+k∥) — complex
obj.GFull 
G_IJ(+k∥) for all (I,J) — complex [3×3×nVel]
obj.GFull_eff 
parity-signed ±k mean per (I,J), as G_eff — complex [3×3×nVel]; empty if computeMinus=false
obj.GFull_minus 
G_IJ(−k∥) = GFull transposed in (I,J) — complex [3×3×nVel]; empty if computeMinus=false
obj.G_eff (default: zeros(1,0)) 
(G_+ + (-1)^n * G_−) / 2, parity-signed (see header); empty if computeMinus=false
obj.G_minus (default: zeros(1,0)) 
G_IJ(−k∥) = G_JI(+k∥) (reciprocity) — complex; empty if computeMinus=false
obj.computeMinus (default: true) 
whether G_minus/G_eff/GFull_minus/GFull_eff were filled (calc()'s 'ComputeMinus')
obj.depthAxis 
depth axis for dispField, normalised units (2*pi = one wavelength) — [1 × nxx3] row vector, 0 to 30 wavelengths. Use this rather than re-deriving the axis in the caller.
obj.dispField 
displacement field profile u_ij(depth, component, force, velocity)
obj.displDir (default: 3) 
J: displacement direction (row of U)
obj.forceDir (default: 1)
