 %                               structureRotated.mater(k)) already computed by the
 %                               caller (CalcSingleLayered.calcLim needs layer 1's for
 %                               the substrate fields anyway); default: computed here
 % The band's edges only, never a score input: which layer is the "substrate"
 % for a wave changes with the wavelength, so no layer is singled out.
pk = GreenScoreLayered.candidates(G, velAxis, vLo, vHi) 
% Pole-fitted candidates of one direction's complex G13_eff on [vLo, vHi]
 % >> pk = GreenScoreLayered.candidates(G, velAxis, vLo, vHi)
 %
 % INPUT:  1. G       .. [nVel x 1] complex G13_eff at SawSpectrum.ETA
 %         2. velAxis .. [nVel x 1] uniform over the band [km/s]
 %         3-4. vLo, vHi .. band (band()); peaks must be interior to it
 % OUTPUT: pk .. struct array (emptyPeaks() fields; slope, SH and the score parts
 %               NaN until GreenScore.probeSlope / polarisation / score)
 %
 % GreenScore's peak finder (bandPeaks), width filter and pole fit (GreenScore.fitPeaks:
 % gamma floored at ETA v/2, a failed fit keeps the grid peak height), on the
 % whole band instead of the half-space [0.8 vS, vTf] cap, without fBand.
pk = GreenScoreLayered.direction(structureBase, angle, G, velAxis, vSmin, vLmax) 
% All steps for one direction: candidates -> probes -> polarisation -> score
 % >> pk = GreenScoreLayered.direction(structureBase, angle, G, velAxis, vSmin, vLmax)
 %
 % INPUT:  1. structureBase .. UNROTATED Structure (probes and the residue solve
 %                             rotate it themselves)
 %         2. angle         .. direction [deg]
 %         3-4. G, velAxis  .. complex G13_eff at SawSpectrum.ETA on a uniform axis
 %                             covering the band (this direction's rotated stack)
 %         5-6. vSmin, vLmax .. bandLimits() of this direction
 % OUTPUT: pk .. scored candidates in velocity order (emptyPeaks() fields),
 %               threshold-independent: accepted() decides at any threshold
 %
 % Cost beyond the axis solve: two probe solves of ~7 points per candidate and one
 % full-tensor solve of 3 points per candidate.
pk = GreenScoreLayered.emptyPeaks() 
% The candidate struct, no entries
 % >> pk = GreenScoreLayered.emptyPeaks()
 %
 % Fields: vel (log-parabola |G| peak, the reported velocity), polVel (fitted
 % pole), gamma (pole half-width, floored, km/s), poleOk, height, prominence,
 % base, poleHeight, relWidth, relHeight (to this direction's tallest), slope /
 % slopeFwd / slopeBwd (km/s per deg), SH, norm, gateTerm, dephaseTerm, polTerm,
 % logStrength, score.
v = GreenScoreLayered.gridAxis(vLo, vMax) 
% Uniform Green axis on the lattice SawSpectrum.VEL_MIN + n x SawSpectrum.VEL_STEP, column
 % >> v = GreenScoreLayered.gridAxis(vLo, vMax)
 %
 % INPUT:  1. vLo  .. lowest velocity the axis must reach [km/s] (the band start)
 %         2. vMax .. axis end before SawSpectrum.EDGE_TAPER [km/s]
 % OUTPUT: v .. from min(VEL_MIN, the lattice point at or below vLo) to vMax +
 %              EDGE_TAPER, 4 m/s steps
 %
 % n may be negative, so a very slow stack (0.3 vSmin below 0.3 km/s) still gets
 % its whole band. Every point is VEL_MIN + n*step with the same integer n in a
 % single angle and in a sweep, so the band of a sweep's common axis is bit for
 % bit the single angle's band (and a single angle's axis is the leading part of
 % the sweep's whenever both start at VEL_MIN).
N = GreenScoreLayered.normaliser(G, velAxis, vLo, vHi) 
% Direction-own normaliser: log10 of the median |G13_eff| over the band
 % >> N = GreenScoreLayered.normaliser(G, velAxis, vLo, vHi)
 %
 % The band is wide on purpose: keeping part of the region below the slowest
 % shear limit makes N track the stack's strongest wave across wavelengths better
 % than a half-space-like [0.8 vS, ...] band.
pk = GreenScoreLayered.polarisation(structureRotated, pk) 
% Surface polarisation SH of every candidate from the diagonal-G residues at its pole
 % >> pk = GreenScoreLayered.polarisation(structureRotated, pk)
 %
 % SH = |r22| / (|r11| + |r22| + |r33|), r_ii the residue of G_ii in the 3-point
 % model a1 + r / (x - q) through pole and pole +- FIT_SPAN (the constant absorbs
 % a neighbour's skirt). One full-tensor solve, 3 points per candidate. NaN if a
 % fit is ill-conditioned (score() then gives the polarisation term -K_POL).
thr = GreenScoreLayered.resolveThreshold(t) 
% Value of a threshold: 'low' | 'high' | numeric (the score scale)
 % >> thr = GreenScoreLayered.resolveThreshold(t)
pk = GreenScoreLayered.score(pk, N) 
% Gate, dephasing and polarisation terms and the score
 % >> pk = GreenScoreLayered.score(pk, N)
 %
 % INPUT:  1. pk .. candidates() after GreenScore.probeSlope() and polarisation()
 %         2. N  .. normaliser() of this direction
 % OUTPUT: pk with norm, gateTerm, dephaseTerm, logStrength (log10 of the gated,
 %         dephased pole prominence, |G13_eff| units), polTerm, score
 %
 % Gate tau = GATE_OWN_PERIODS / v (in grating periods per km/s): a pole of
 % half-width gamma decays as exp(-2 pi gamma t / lambda). GreenScore.dephaseFactor
 % takes the gate as GATE_PERIODS / vRef, so it is called with vRef = GATE_PERIODS
 % / tau. A slope NaN (no probe maximum) is set to the probe stencil's bound, as
 % in GreenScore.score.
SH = GreenScoreLayered.surfaceSH(v, g11, g22, g33) 
% Shear-horizontal share of a pole from 3 samples of G11, G22, G33 around it
 % >> SH = GreenScoreLayered.surfaceSH(v, g11, g22, g33)
 %
 % INPUT:  v .. [3 x 1] velocities (pole - FIT_SPAN, pole, pole + FIT_SPAN)
 %         g11, g22, g33 .. [3 x 1] complex samples
 % OUTPUT: SH = |r22| / (|r11| + |r22| + |r33|) in [0, 1] (NaN if a fit is
 %         ill-conditioned), r_ii the residue of the 3-point model a1 + r/(x - q)

GreenSpotScore value class

% GreenSpotScore — the refiner of the half-space 'full' classification: it models
 % the finite probe spot by summing G13_eff coherently over
 % a Gaussian spread of directions, then decides existence and velocity from the
 % late-time spectra of that sum. Run on request (classify('refined')), stored
 % beside the default GreenScore classification, never inside it.
 %
 % EXPERIMENTAL: checked against the measured maps published in Grabec et al. 2024 and the
 % UTGS paper (arXiv:2510.10696); the scores, their scales and the interface may change.
 %
 % For one propagation direction theta:
 %   1. directions theta + o, o = -2.5:0.5:2.5 deg (FWHM 2.5 deg, cut at 2.5 sigma),
 %      each solved once (GreenSolver, ETA = 7e-3) on its own axis 0.3 km/s + n x
 %      VEL_STEP up to 1.05 x its own longLimVel + 0.1 km/s; Q(theta + 180) = Q(theta),
 %      so directions are folded into [0, 180);
 %   2. X = sum_j w_j G13_eff(theta + o_j), complex (coherent: the spot excites all
 %      directions at once), on the shortest of the 11 axes;
 %   3. late-time |spectra| of X with the gate at GATE_DETECT (4) and GATE_POS (2)
 %      periods of vRef = sqrt(G_Hill/rho) (SawSpectrum.lateTime: pad 4 -> 2 m/s, tail
 %      taper 0.2), candidates = findpeaks below longLimVel with half-prominence
 %      width <= SawSpectrum.MAX_REL_WIDTH x velocity, kept at a reported velocity <= vTf (1 +
 %      SEARCH_HI_TOL) (vTf = fastest shear limit, GreenScore's cap);
 %   4. 'high': scoreHigh = log10(prom4 / fU1) >= THRESHOLD_HIGH, fU1 = median |X|
 %      (un-gated) over [BAND_LO vS, BAND_HI vTf];
 %      'low' : scoreLow = log10(prom4 / ref.fT12) >= THRESHOLD_LOW, fT12 = median over
 %      REF_ANGLES of each direction's largest gate-4 prominence (reference(),
 %      memoised per structure);
 %   5. velocity: the gate-2 peak whose half-prominence half-width contains the gate-4
 %      peak (nearest if several), else the gate-4 position (velDet). The early,
 %      shorter gate keeps more of the pole's own lineshape, so it places the peak
 %      where the measured map does.
 %
 % Why (docs/reference/architecture/forwardCalcs_modeClassification.md, section 3): it uses the
 % spot-averaged velocity, which sits closer to where a measured map puts the peak, and
 % a modified accept decision (the averaged spectrum rejects peaks the spot dephases).
 % Cost: twice the Green solves of a 1-deg default map (one direction every half
 % degree), so it is a refiner, not the default.
 %
 % Single angle = the same angle in any sweep, bit for bit: every direction is
 % solved alone on an axis set by its own longLimVel, so a direction's spectrum does
 % not depend on which other directions were solved.
 %
 % Value class with Constant properties and Static methods only (plus reference()'s
 % per-session memo, SawSpectrum.memo). Half-space only.
 %
 % GuliweRR v6.0

Properties

GreenSpotScore.BAND_HI (default: 1) 
fU1 band end, x vTf
GreenSpotScore.BAND_LO (default: 0.8) 
fU1 band start, x vS (as GreenScore)
GreenSpotScore.DIR_STEP (default: 0.5) 
deg -- kernel direction spacing
GreenSpotScore.ETA (default: 0.007) 
GreenSolver damping (calibrated with the rest; > SawSpectrum.ETA)
GreenSpotScore.FWHM (default: 2.5) 
deg -- Gaussian direction spread of the probe spot
GreenSpotScore.FWHM_TO_SIGMA (default: 2.3548) 
FWHM / sigma (the literal the thresholds were calibrated with)
GreenSpotScore.GATE_DETECT (default: 4) 
late-time gate of the detection spectrum, periods of vRef
GreenSpotScore.GATE_POS (default: 2) 
late-time gate of the position spectrum, periods of vRef
GreenSpotScore.LABEL_TOL (default: 0.007) 
Rayleigh label below minShearSpot x (1 + LABEL_TOL) (= ETA: the pole cannot be placed more finely than its width); minShearSpot = the spot-weighted minShear (average())
GreenSpotScore.POLE_WIN_ABS (default: 0.03) 
max(POLE_WIN_REL v, POLE_WIN_ABS) km/s -- the pairing tolerance of refinedSawAgreement
GreenSpotScore.POLE_WIN_REL (default: 0.012) 
identify: a spot peak's single-direction pole lies within
GreenSpotScore.REF_ANGLES (default: [0 15 30 45 60 75 90 105 120 135 150 165]) 
deg -- directions of the 'low' reference
GreenSpotScore.REF_CACHE_SIZE (default: 32) 
reference() results memoised per session
GreenSpotScore.SEARCH_HI_TOL (default: 0.012) 
candidates kept at vel <= vTf (1 + SEARCH_HI_TOL)
GreenSpotScore.THRESHOLD_HIGH (default: -0.125) 
'high' standard (scoreHigh)
GreenSpotScore.THRESHOLD_LOW (default: -0.55) 
'low' standard (scoreLow, structure-relative)
GreenSpotScore.TRUNC_SIGMA (default: 2.5) 
kernel cut, sigmas
GreenSpotScore.VEL_STEP (default: 0.008) 
km/s -- Green axis step of every direction

Methods

obj = GreenSpotScore() 
% GreenSpotScore — the refiner of the half-space 'full' classification: it models
 % the finite probe spot by summing G13_eff coherently over
 % a Gaussian spread of directions, then decides existence and velocity from the
 % late-time spectra of that sum. Run on request (classify('refined')), stored
 % beside the default GreenScore classification, never inside it.
 %
 % EXPERIMENTAL: checked against the measured maps published in Grabec et al. 2024 and the
 % UTGS paper (arXiv:2510.10696); the scores, their scales and the interface may change.
 %
 % For one propagation direction theta:
 %   1. directions theta + o, o = -2.5:0.5:2.5 deg (FWHM 2.5 deg, cut at 2.5 sigma),
 %      each solved once (GreenSolver, ETA = 7e-3) on its own axis 0.3 km/s + n x
 %      VEL_STEP up to 1.05 x its own longLimVel + 0.1 km/s; Q(theta + 180) = Q(theta),
 %      so directions are folded into [0, 180);
 %   2. X = sum_j w_j G13_eff(theta + o_j), complex (coherent: the spot excites all
 %      directions at once), on the shortest of the 11 axes;
 %   3. late-time |spectra| of X with the gate at GATE_DETECT (4) and GATE_POS (2)
 %      periods of vRef = sqrt(G_Hill/rho) (SawSpectrum.lateTime: pad 4 -> 2 m/s, tail
 %      taper 0.2), candidates = findpeaks below longLimVel with half-prominence
 %      width <= SawSpectrum.MAX_REL_WIDTH x velocity, kept at a reported velocity <= vTf (1 +
 %      SEARCH_HI_TOL) (vTf = fastest shear limit, GreenScore's cap);
 %   4. 'high': scoreHigh = log10(prom4 / fU1) >= THRESHOLD_HIGH, fU1 = median |X|
 %      (un-gated) over [BAND_LO vS, BAND_HI vTf];
 %      'low' : scoreLow = log10(prom4 / ref.fT12) >= THRESHOLD_LOW, fT12 = median over
 %      REF_ANGLES of each direction's largest gate-4 prominence (reference(),
 %      memoised per structure);
 %   5. velocity: the gate-2 peak whose half-prominence half-width contains the gate-4
 %      peak (nearest if several), else the gate-4 position (velDet). The early,
 %      shorter gate keeps more of the pole's own lineshape, so it places the peak
 %      where the measured map does.
 %
 % Why (docs/reference/architecture/forwardCalcs_modeClassification.md, section 3): it uses the
 % spot-averaged velocity, which sits closer to where a measured map puts the peak, and
 % a modified accept decision (the averaged spectrum rejects peaks the spot dephases).
 % Cost: twice the Green solves of a 1-deg default map (one direction every half
 % degree), so it is a refiner, not the default.
 %
 % Single angle = the same angle in any sweep, bit for bit: every direction is
 % solved alone on an axis set by its own longLimVel, so a direction's spectrum does
 % not depend on which other directions were solved.
 %
 % Value class with Constant properties and Static methods only (plus reference()'s
 % per-session memo, SawSpectrum.memo). Half-space only.
 %
 % GuliweRR v6.0
tf = GreenSpotScore.accepted(pk, threshold) 
% Which scored candidates pass a threshold
 % >> tf = GreenSpotScore.accepted(pk, threshold)
 %
 % 'low' is not a subset of 'high': the two divide by different normalisers
 % (the direction's own fU1, the structure's fT12).
 % A NaN on the compared scale is an error (GreenSpotScore:accepted:noScore):
 % scoreLow is NaN when direction() ran without the reference.
[X, v, minShearSpot] = GreenSpotScore.average(dc, angle) 
% The spot-averaged complex G13_eff of one direction, and the spot-weighted minShear
 % >> [X, v] = GreenSpotScore.average(dc, angle)
 % >> [X, v, minShearSpot] = GreenSpotScore.average(dc, angle)
 %
 % OUTPUT: X            .. [n x 1] complex, sum_j w_j G13_eff(angle + o_j), on v =
 %                         the first n points of the common axis, n = the shortest
 %                         kernel axis
 %         minShearSpot .. sum_j w_j minShear(angle + o_j) [km/s] -- the boundary the
 %                         refined Rayleigh label compares with (identify()): a bound
 %                         Rayleigh wave lies below minShear in every direction of the
 %                         spot, so its averaged peak lies below the averaged minShear,
 %                         while on a curved branch it can lie above minShear(angle)
 %
 % Summed term by term in kernel order (not as a matrix product), so the result
 % does not depend on BLAS blocking.
GreenSpotScore.clearReferenceCache() 
% Empty reference()'s per-session memo
 % >> GreenSpotScore.clearReferenceCache()
pk = GreenSpotScore.direction(X, v, vS, vTf, vL, vRef, ref) 
% Scored candidates of one direction from its spot-averaged spectrum
 % >> pk = GreenSpotScore.direction(X, v, vS, vTf, vL, vRef)
 % >> pk = GreenSpotScore.direction(X, v, vS, vTf, vL, vRef, ref)
 %
 % INPUT:  1-2. X, v      .. average() output
 %         3-5. vS, vTf, vL .. slowest / fastest shear and longitudinal limiting
 %                         velocity of this direction [km/s]
 %         6. vRef        .. sqrt(G_Hill/rho) [km/s]
 %  (opt)  7. ref         .. reference(); fills scoreLow
 % OUTPUT: pk .. struct array (0..N, one per gate-4 candidate): vel (reported:
 %               gate-2-linked), velDet (gate-4), posLinked, prominence, relWidth,
 %               height (gate 4), fU1, scoreHigh, scoreLow; identity fields
 %               (modeNo, matchScore, confident, type) and accepted empty
 %               until identify() / accepted()
D = GreenSpotScore.directionSet(angles) 
% Every folded direction the kernels of the given angles need, unique, column
 % >> D = GreenSpotScore.directionSet(angles)
pk = GreenSpotScore.emptyPeaks() 
% The candidate struct with no rows (direction()'s field set)
 % >> pk = GreenSpotScore.emptyPeaks()
[x, key] = GreenSpotScore.foldDirection(x) 
% Directions folded into [0, 180) deg (Q(theta + 180) = Q(theta)) and their keys
 % >> [x, key] = GreenSpotScore.foldDirection(x)
 %
 % OUTPUT: x   .. folded, rounded to 1e-6 deg
 %         key .. int64 round(x x 1e6), the direction cache key
pk = GreenSpotScore.identify(pk, ritz, minShearSpot, poleVels) 
% Ritz mode identity and Rayleigh/pseudoRayleigh label of every candidate
 % >> pk = GreenSpotScore.identify(pk, ritz, minShearSpot)
 % >> pk = GreenSpotScore.identify(pk, ritz, minShearSpot, poleVels)
 %
 % INPUT:  minShearSpot .. the label boundary: the spot-weighted minShear from
 %                         average() (CalcSingleHS.calcRefined passes it)
 %  (opt)  poleVels     .. the single-direction poles of this angle: the vel of
 %                         every GreenScore candidate (greenScorePeaks), accepted
 %                         or not; default [] (no pole: the spot rule for every peak)
 %
 % Mode: the spot peak is mapped to the nearest single-direction pole within
 % max(POLE_WIN_REL v, POLE_WIN_ABS), and that pole bound by the 'green' rule
 % (ModeClassification.matchRitzToGreenPeak): the wave at this angle is what the
 % Ritz basis, solved at this angle only, represents. Why not the spot peak
 % itself: the spot average moves it off the single-direction pole by up to
 % tens of m/s, as far as the slab-mode spacing, so a rule at the spot velocity
 % binds a neighbour of the wave's mode on leaky entries. With no pole in the
 % window (rare; mostly on a strongly anisotropic material where the spot
 % spectrum forms a comb) the spot rule (ModeClassification.matchSpotPeak)
 % decides. Label: 'Rayleigh'
 % below minShearSpot x (1 + LABEL_TOL), else 'pseudoRayleigh' -- from the Green
 % velocity alone (docs/pitfalls.md MTH2 and MTH3). The spot-weighted boundary,
 % not minShear(angle): the spot average lifts a Rayleigh wave hugging minShear on a
 % curved branch above minShear(angle).
[o, w] = GreenSpotScore.kernel() 
% Direction offsets [deg] and Gaussian weights (summing to 1) of the probe spot
 % >> [o, w] = GreenSpotScore.kernel()
ref = GreenSpotScore.reference(structure, dc, useParallel) 
% The 'low' reference of a structure: fT12, its typical strongest wave
 % >> ref = GreenSpotScore.reference(structure)
 % >> ref = GreenSpotScore.reference(structure, dc, useParallel)
 %
 % INPUT:  1. structure   .. UNROTATED half-space Structure
 %  (opt)  2. dc          .. a direction cache (solveDirections) of this structure;
 %                           directions already in it are not solved again
 %                           (default [] = none)
 %  (opt)  3. useParallel .. "auto" (default) | "always" | "never" (the solves)
 % OUTPUT: ref .. struct: vRef [km/s], fT12 = median over REF_ANGLES (with
 %                at least one candidate) of each direction's largest gate-4
 %                prominence, pmaxPerAngle, angles
 %
 % One number per structure, so the 'low' labels never depend on the range
 % computed. Up to 12 x 11 = 132 direction solves (~4 s serial, < 1 s on a pool),
 % none in a 0:1:180 sweep, whose own directions cover them. Memoised per session
 % (keyed by the structure's contents; clearReferenceCache() empties
 % it); not memoised inside a pool worker.
out = GreenSpotScore.referenceCache(action, key, ref) 
% reference()'s memo: 'get' (returns [] on a miss) | 'put' | 'clear' | 'size'
 % >> ref = GreenSpotScore.referenceCache('get', key)
 %
 % SawSpectrum.memo under the name 'GreenSpotScore', REF_CACHE_SIZE entries.
key = GreenSpotScore.referenceKey(structure) 
% reference()'s memo key: the structure's physical content (SawSpectrum.structureKey)
 % >> key = GreenSpotScore.referenceKey(structure)
thr = GreenSpotScore.resolveThreshold(t) 
% Scale and value of a threshold: 'high' | 'low' | numeric (the 'high' scale)
 % >> thr = GreenSpotScore.resolveThreshold(t)
 %
 % OUTPUT: thr .. struct: scale ('high' -> scoreHigh, 'low' -> scoreLow), value
dc = GreenSpotScore.solveDirections(structureBase, D, useParallel, dc) 
% Direction cache: solve every direction of D not already in dc
 % >> dc = GreenSpotScore.solveDirections(structureBase, D)
 % >> dc = GreenSpotScore.solveDirections(structureBase, D, useParallel, dc)
 %
 % INPUT:  1. structureBase .. UNROTATED half-space Structure
 %         2. D             .. directions [deg] (folded here)
 %  (opt)  3. useParallel   .. "auto" (default) | "always" | "never" (thread pool
 %                             over the directions; the result does not depend on it)
 %  (opt)  4. dc            .. an existing cache to extend
 % OUTPUT: dc .. struct: key (int64 [nD x 1]), dir, vL, vS (minShear), n, G
 %               ([nMax x nD] complex, NaN-padded), v (the common axis, [nMax x 1])
d = GreenSpotScore.solveOne(structureBase, x) 
% One direction: its own axis and the complex G13_eff on it
 % >> d = GreenSpotScore.solveOne(structureBase, x)
 %
 % INPUT:  structureBase .. UNROTATED half-space Structure; x .. direction [deg]
 % OUTPUT: d .. struct G ([n x 1] complex G13_eff at ETA), vL (longLimVel),
 %              vS (min(shearLimVels), the direction's minShear), n
 %
 % The axis is GreenScore.gridAxis(1.05 vL, VEL_STEP): built as 0.3 + n x step,
 % so every direction's axis is the leading part of a longer one.

RitzScore value class

% RitzScore — the Ritz-only existence score: a gated Ritz modal sum that mimics
 % G13, deciding which surface waves exist when no Green's function is computed
 % (calcMode = 'ritzOnly').
 %
 % EXPERIMENTAL: checked against the measured maps published in Grabec et al. 2024 and the
 % UTGS paper (arXiv:2510.10696); the scores, their scales and the interface may change.
 %
 % For one propagation direction, with the Ritz modes (v_n) and their surface
 % displacements u_n (standard frame: x1 = propagation, x3 = surface normal):
 %     S(v) = sum_n u3_n conj(u1_n) / (v_n^2 - v^2 (1 + i ETA))
 % i.e. the modal expansion of the surface response to an in-plane force along x1,
 % read as out-of-plane displacement (the TGS component). S is evaluated on a uniform
 % velocity axis and scored with the shared late-time machinery (SawSpectrum): the
 % late-time gate (SawSpectrum.lateTime, 3 periods of vRef), the candidate search and
 % width filter (SawSpectrum.candidates), and
 %     score = log10(prominence / floor)
 % with the floor of THIS direction (ownReference(): the median late-time modal-sum
 % level over a band -- half-space [0.8 x the slowest, the fastest shear limit], layered
 % the whole band [0.3 vSmin, 1.05 vLmax]). The own-direction floor costs nothing, so a
 % single angle needs no extra Ritz solves and classifies exactly as it does inside a
 % sweep.
 %
 % Why it works: for M-orthonormal
 % eigenvectors the driven surface response of the finite Ritz domain IS this modal
 % sum; the discrete bulk modes of the finite domain dephase in time like the bulk
 % continuum of a true half-space, so the late-time gate suppresses them as it
 % suppresses bulk features of Green's function, while a surface-wave mode keeps
 % ringing. The G13-like numerator u3 conj(u1) matters more than any other choice
 % (an |u3|^2 weighting is clearly worse).
 %
 % HALF-SPACE RULE (direction(), acceptedHS()). Everything comes from the one
 % Ritz solve of the angle -- no reference directions, no neighbouring angles:
 %   1. score   .. the modal-sum score above (own floor), per candidate peak
 %   2. mode    .. the LOCAL supporting mode: the strongest modal-sum term among the
 %                 modes inside the peak's half-width (localMode). The global
 %                 argmax over all modes can name the Rayleigh mode for a pseudo-SAW peak 0.4-0.8 km/s away
 %   3. dScore  .. score + log10 F, F = GreenScore.dephaseFactor: the late-time height
 %                 lost when the TGS aperture (GreenScore.APERTURE_SIGMA) averages the
 %                 pole over neighbouring directions. Needs the supporting mode's angular
 %                 slope dv/dtheta, exact from the same solve (RitzSolver.angleSlope,
 %                 Hellmann-Feynman); pole half-width ETA v/2. A branch that moves fast
 %                 with angle does not ring in the experiment however sharp it is here
 %   4. veto    .. the supporting mode's freqDisp >= MIN_FREQDISP (non-dispersion)
 %   5. windowMass (candidates above min shear only, see belowShear below) .. log10 of the summed
 %                 near-surface weight r E1 fd of the modes within max(1.2 %, 30 m/s)
 %                 of the peak (windowWeights). A slab artefact rings from one or two
 %                 bulk-like modes; a real leaky wave is carried by several modes with
 %                 surface-concentrated energy. Depth-normalised: + log10(relDepth / 30)
 %                 (HS_WINDOW_DEPTH_REF)
 %   6. bottom  (candidates above min shear only) .. the supporting mode must not be
 %                 bottom-trapped (HS_BOTTOM10_MAX, HS_BOTTOM_RATIO_MAX): a U-shaped slab
 %                 mode of a strongly leaky pseudo-SAW
 %   'high': veto & dScore >= HS_THRESHOLD_HIGH & (below min shear | windowMass >=
 %   HS_MIN_WINDOW_MASS & not bottom-trapped). 'low': the SAME gates with dScore >= HS_THRESHOLD_LOW, so
 %   'low' is always a subset of 'high'.
 %   Velocity: the supporting mode's eigenvelocity. Label: 'Rayleigh' if it is below
 %   min shear + slabResolution (the Ritz slab's own discretisation error of the
 %   slow-shear limit), else 'pseudoRayleigh'.
 % Validation and limits: docs/reference/architecture/forwardCalcs_modeClassification.md, sections
 % 11-12. A very leaky pseudo-SAW that shows in TGS can be dropped at 'high' (Fe3Al(316),
 % 148-170 deg); a weak Rayleigh wave hugging min shear on a steep branch can be absent
 % at 'low' (Fe3Al(316), 86-116 deg).
 %
 % LAYERED RULE (directionLayered(), acceptedLayered()). Built like the half-space
 % rule and like the layered Green path (GreenScoreLayered): no layer is singled out as
 % "the substrate".
 %   band   .. the modal sum's axis, candidate search and floor all cover
 %             [0.3 vSmin, 1.05 vLmax] (GreenScoreLayered.band: slowest shear / fastest
 %             longitudinal limit over ALL layers); modes kept up to MODE_VEL_FACTOR x vLmax
 %   score  .. dScore (own floor, local supporting mode, aperture dephasing, as the
 %             half-space) + LAY_K_POL (1 - 2 SH), SH = |u2|^2 / |u|^2 of the supporting
 %             mode at the free surface (0 sagittal .. 1 Love) -- GreenScoreLayered's term
 %   gate   .. the supporting mode must be TRAPPED: bottom25 (its energy fraction in the
 %             deepest 25 % of the whole domain, density- and thickness-weighted) <=
 %             LAY_EBOT_MAX. A guided wave decays into the substrate (bottom25 ~1e-13);
 %             a slab-continuum mode, and every leaky wave, reaches the rigid bottom
 %             (>= 0.03). No freqDisp veto: guided waves in a stack are dispersive.
 %   'low' (default): score >= LAY_THRESHOLD_LOW -- sagittal-type waves; 'high': score
 %   >= LAY_THRESHOLD_HIGH -- also Love-type; the same gate, so 'low' is a subset of 'high'.
 %   Velocity: the supporting mode's eigenvelocity; 'SAW' with aboveSubstrateST (label
 %   only) against the substrate's slowest shear limit.
 % Tuned on the measured NiTi/MgO TGS map (Grabec et al. 2024), as GreenScoreLayered:
 % 'low' finds the sagittal-type waves, 'high' also most Love-type waves at the cost of
 % false positives. Against GreenScoreLayered on four strongly leaky stacks (computed
 % with GuliweRR): identical on every trapped wave. KNOWN LIMIT: leaky layered waves
 % (above the substrate shear limit and radiating into it) are never reported -- the gate
 % removes them with the slab continuum, which no other Ritz measure tested separates
 % from them; use calcMode 'full' for those.
 %
 % Cost beyond the Ritz solve, half-space: Christoffel limiting velocities (~4 ms), the
 % displacement profiles of the kept modes (~1 ms), sum, gate and candidates (~3 ms),
 % the slopes of the supporting modes (~4 ms), slab resolution (<1 ms). No other
 % direction is solved.
 % See docs/reference/architecture/forwardCalcs_modeClassification.md (the Ritz method).
 %
 % Value class with Constant properties and Static methods only.
 %
 % GuliweRR v6.0

Properties

RitzScore.ETA (default: 0.01) 
modal damping, denominator v_n^2 - v^2 (1 + i ETA). Larger than SawSpectrum.ETA: a discrete Ritz mode is an exact pole, and 1e-2 keeps it resolved on the 2 m/s axis and decayed within the FFT window
RitzScore.HS_BOTTOM10_MAX (default: 0.15) 
bottom-trapped gate (pseudo-SAWs only): reject a candidate
RitzScore.HS_BOTTOM_RATIO_MAX (default: 0.8) 
whose supporting mode has > 15 % of its |u|^2 in the deepest 10 % of the slab AND bottom10 / bottom25 > 0.8 (uniform: 0.4). A strongly leaky pseudo-SAW the rigid-bottom slab cannot radiate splits into "U-shaped" modes, surface AND bottom; the lower member wins the peak 55-65 m/s below the real branch (computed with GuliweRR). Evidence from ONE strongly anisotropic cut; values for relDepth ~30 -- bottom10 depends on relDepth
RitzScore.HS_FLOOR_LO (default: 0.8) 
floor band start (ownReference), x min shear; the band ends at the fastest shear limit
RitzScore.HS_MIN_LEG_PER_DEPTH (default: 2.3) 
basis guard: legOrder >= 2.3 relDepth. Below it the slab does not resolve the band up to vL and the rule breaks (legOrder < 2 relDepth: hundreds of slab modes near vL accepted); checkBasisHS warns
RitzScore.HS_MIN_WINDOW_MASS (default: -1.55) 
log10 window weight a candidate above min shear needs (depth-normalised, see HS_WINDOW_DEPTH_REF)
RitzScore.HS_THRESHOLD_HIGH (default: 0.16) 
'high': dScore threshold
RitzScore.HS_THRESHOLD_LOW (default: 0.7) 
'low': dScore threshold, same other gates as 'high'
RitzScore.HS_WINDOW_DEPTH_REF (default: 30) 
relDepth at which windowMass is unshifted: direction() adds log10(relDepth / 30), as the summed weight of a real leaky wave falls with the slab depth (more modes share it). Without it a 60-lambda slab drops a real leaky branch (computed with GuliweRR)
RitzScore.LAY_EBOT_MAX (default: 0.01) 
trapped gate: the supporting mode's bottom25 <= this. Guided waves 1e-22-1e-12, slab continuum and leaky waves >= 0.03 (four stacks, substrate depths 20 and 30 lambda); 3e-3 to 1e-1 classify NiTi/MgO identically
RitzScore.LAY_K_POL (default: 0.75) 
polarisation weight: score = dScore + LAY_K_POL (1 - 2 SH) (as GreenScoreLayered.K_POL; 0-1 give the same 'low' result, 0.75 orders Love waves before spurious peaks best)
RitzScore.LAY_THRESHOLD_HIGH (default: -0.7) 
'high': also Love-type waves
RitzScore.LAY_THRESHOLD_LOW (default: 0.65) 
'low' (default): sagittal-type waves. Centre of the window 0.5-0.8 that admits no Love-type wave. Lower thresholds recover the Rayleigh-cap ends but admit the lower Love arm first: the modal sum's pole half-width ETA v/2 dephases a steep arm far less than Green's does
RitzScore.LOCAL_MIN_HALF (default: 0.004) 
km/s -- smallest half-width localMode searches (2 axis steps)
RitzScore.MIN_FREQDISP (default: 0.3) 
HALF-SPACE ONLY non-dispersion veto: an accepted modal-sum peak whose supporting Ritz mode (localMode) has freqDisp below this is rejected (0.3 also best for the half-space rule: 0.5 drops real branches on a strongly anisotropic cut). Physics: a homogeneous half-space has no length scale, so a true SAW is non-dispersive (v_g = v_p); a mode of the finite-depth Ritz slab is set by the domain depth and is dispersive. The cut allows |v_p - v_g|/v_p up to ~35 %. Removes bulk-like slab modes that ring coherently near vL (e.g. Ni(110) ~5 km/s, v/vL 0.92-0.98). NOT applied to layered stacks: guided waves in a layered stack are genuinely dispersive -- the film thickness sets a length scale, so v_g ~= v_p for true modes -- and freqDisp cannot separate them from finite-domain artefacts (the layered rule uses the trapped gate LAY_EBOT_MAX instead). Cuts 0.2-0.3 similar, 0.3 best.
RitzScore.MODE_VEL_FACTOR (default: 1.2) 
modes summed: v_n <= MODE_VEL_FACTOR x longLimVel (half-space) or x the fastest longitudinal limit of any layer (layered) %% --- Half-space rule (direction, acceptedHS) -- tuned together against measured %% TGS maps: change none of them alone
RitzScore.VEL_STEP (default: 0.002) 
km/s -- Ritz score axis: 2 m/s (the analytic modal sum costs nothing per point; no padding or tail taper -- the thresholds were calibrated this way)
RitzScore.WINDOW_ABS (default: 0.03) 
window half-width max(WINDOW_REL v, WINDOW_ABS): the
RitzScore.WINDOW_REL (default: 0.012) 
window half-width max(WINDOW_REL v, WINDOW_ABS): the
RitzScore.WINDOW_TOP (default: 0.98) 
modes above WINDOW_TOP x vL carry no window weight (slab modes crowd the longitudinal limit)

Methods

obj = RitzScore() 
% RitzScore — the Ritz-only existence score: a gated Ritz modal sum that mimics
 % G13, deciding which surface waves exist when no Green's function is computed
 % (calcMode = 'ritzOnly').
 %
 % EXPERIMENTAL: checked against the measured maps published in Grabec et al. 2024 and the
 % UTGS paper (arXiv:2510.10696); the scores, their scales and the interface may change.
 %
 % For one propagation direction, with the Ritz modes (v_n) and their surface
 % displacements u_n (standard frame: x1 = propagation, x3 = surface normal):
 %     S(v) = sum_n u3_n conj(u1_n) / (v_n^2 - v^2 (1 + i ETA))
 % i.e. the modal expansion of the surface response to an in-plane force along x1,
 % read as out-of-plane displacement (the TGS component). S is evaluated on a uniform
 % velocity axis and scored with the shared late-time machinery (SawSpectrum): the
 % late-time gate (SawSpectrum.lateTime, 3 periods of vRef), the candidate search and
 % width filter (SawSpectrum.candidates), and
 %     score = log10(prominence / floor)
 % with the floor of THIS direction (ownReference(): the median late-time modal-sum
 % level over a band -- half-space [0.8 x the slowest, the fastest shear limit], layered
 % the whole band [0.3 vSmin, 1.05 vLmax]). The own-direction floor costs nothing, so a
 % single angle needs no extra Ritz solves and classifies exactly as it does inside a
 % sweep.
 %
 % Why it works: for M-orthonormal
 % eigenvectors the driven surface response of the finite Ritz domain IS this modal
 % sum; the discrete bulk modes of the finite domain dephase in time like the bulk
 % continuum of a true half-space, so the late-time gate suppresses them as it
 % suppresses bulk features of Green's function, while a surface-wave mode keeps
 % ringing. The G13-like numerator u3 conj(u1) matters more than any other choice
 % (an |u3|^2 weighting is clearly worse).
 %
 % HALF-SPACE RULE (direction(), acceptedHS()). Everything comes from the one
 % Ritz solve of the angle -- no reference directions, no neighbouring angles:
 %   1. score   .. the modal-sum score above (own floor), per candidate peak
 %   2. mode    .. the LOCAL supporting mode: the strongest modal-sum term among the
 %                 modes inside the peak's half-width (localMode). The global
 %                 argmax over all modes can name the Rayleigh mode for a pseudo-SAW peak 0.4-0.8 km/s away
 %   3. dScore  .. score + log10 F, F = GreenScore.dephaseFactor: the late-time height
 %                 lost when the TGS aperture (GreenScore.APERTURE_SIGMA) averages the
 %                 pole over neighbouring directions. Needs the supporting mode's angular
 %                 slope dv/dtheta, exact from the same solve (RitzSolver.angleSlope,
 %                 Hellmann-Feynman); pole half-width ETA v/2. A branch that moves fast
 %                 with angle does not ring in the experiment however sharp it is here
 %   4. veto    .. the supporting mode's freqDisp >= MIN_FREQDISP (non-dispersion)
 %   5. windowMass (candidates above min shear only, see belowShear below) .. log10 of the summed
 %                 near-surface weight r E1 fd of the modes within max(1.2 %, 30 m/s)
 %                 of the peak (windowWeights). A slab artefact rings from one or two
 %                 bulk-like modes; a real leaky wave is carried by several modes with
 %                 surface-concentrated energy. Depth-normalised: + log10(relDepth / 30)
 %                 (HS_WINDOW_DEPTH_REF)
 %   6. bottom  (candidates above min shear only) .. the supporting mode must not be
 %                 bottom-trapped (HS_BOTTOM10_MAX, HS_BOTTOM_RATIO_MAX): a U-shaped slab
 %                 mode of a strongly leaky pseudo-SAW
 %   'high': veto & dScore >= HS_THRESHOLD_HIGH & (below min shear | windowMass >=
 %   HS_MIN_WINDOW_MASS & not bottom-trapped). 'low': the SAME gates with dScore >= HS_THRESHOLD_LOW, so
 %   'low' is always a subset of 'high'.
 %   Velocity: the supporting mode's eigenvelocity. Label: 'Rayleigh' if it is below
 %   min shear + slabResolution (the Ritz slab's own discretisation error of the
 %   slow-shear limit), else 'pseudoRayleigh'.
 % Validation and limits: docs/reference/architecture/forwardCalcs_modeClassification.md, sections
 % 11-12. A very leaky pseudo-SAW that shows in TGS can be dropped at 'high' (Fe3Al(316),
 % 148-170 deg); a weak Rayleigh wave hugging min shear on a steep branch can be absent
 % at 'low' (Fe3Al(316), 86-116 deg).
 %
 % LAYERED RULE (directionLayered(), acceptedLayered()). Built like the half-space
 % rule and like the layered Green path (GreenScoreLayered): no layer is singled out as
 % "the substrate".
 %   band   .. the modal sum's axis, candidate search and floor all cover
 %             [0.3 vSmin, 1.05 vLmax] (GreenScoreLayered.band: slowest shear / fastest
 %             longitudinal limit over ALL layers); modes kept up to MODE_VEL_FACTOR x vLmax
 %   score  .. dScore (own floor, local supporting mode, aperture dephasing, as the
 %             half-space) + LAY_K_POL (1 - 2 SH), SH = |u2|^2 / |u|^2 of the supporting
 %             mode at the free surface (0 sagittal .. 1 Love) -- GreenScoreLayered's term
 %   gate   .. the supporting mode must be TRAPPED: bottom25 (its energy fraction in the
 %             deepest 25 % of the whole domain, density- and thickness-weighted) <=
 %             LAY_EBOT_MAX. A guided wave decays into the substrate (bottom25 ~1e-13);
 %             a slab-continuum mode, and every leaky wave, reaches the rigid bottom
 %             (>= 0.03). No freqDisp veto: guided waves in a stack are dispersive.
 %   'low' (default): score >= LAY_THRESHOLD_LOW -- sagittal-type waves; 'high': score
 %   >= LAY_THRESHOLD_HIGH -- also Love-type; the same gate, so 'low' is a subset of 'high'.
 %   Velocity: the supporting mode's eigenvelocity; 'SAW' with aboveSubstrateST (label
 %   only) against the substrate's slowest shear limit.
 % Tuned on the measured NiTi/MgO TGS map (Grabec et al. 2024), as GreenScoreLayered:
 % 'low' finds the sagittal-type waves, 'high' also most Love-type waves at the cost of
 % false positives. Against GreenScoreLayered on four strongly leaky stacks (computed
 % with GuliweRR): identical on every trapped wave. KNOWN LIMIT: leaky layered waves
 % (above the substrate shear limit and radiating into it) are never reported -- the gate
 % removes them with the slab continuum, which no other Ritz measure tested separates
 % from them; use calcMode 'full' for those.
 %
 % Cost beyond the Ritz solve, half-space: Christoffel limiting velocities (~4 ms), the
 % displacement profiles of the kept modes (~1 ms), sum, gate and candidates (~3 ms),
 % the slopes of the supporting modes (~4 ms), slab resolution (<1 ms). No other
 % direction is solved.
 % See docs/reference/architecture/forwardCalcs_modeClassification.md (the Ritz method).
 %
 % Value class with Constant properties and Static methods only.
 %
 % GuliweRR v6.0
acc = RitzScore.acceptedHS(pk, threshold) 
% Half-space acceptance of scored candidates (direction()) at a threshold
 % >> acc = RitzScore.acceptedHS(pk, threshold)
 %
 % INPUT:  1. pk        .. struct array from direction()
 %         2. threshold .. 'high' | 'low' | numeric (a dScore threshold with the
 %                         'high' gates)
 % OUTPUT: acc .. [numel(pk) x 1] logical:
 %                ~vetoed & dScore >= thr & (belowShear | windowMass >= HS_MIN_WINDOW_MASS
 %                & ~bottomTrapped)
 %                bottomTrapped = bottom10 > HS_BOTTOM10_MAX & bottom10 / bottom25 >
 %                HS_BOTTOM_RATIO_MAX (a U-shaped supporting mode, see the constants;
 %                never true when the fields are NaN)
 % The gates are the same at every threshold, so a higher threshold only ever
 % removes entries ('low' is a subset of 'high').
acc = RitzScore.acceptedLayered(pk, threshold) 
% Layered acceptance of scored candidates (directionLayered) at a threshold
 % >> acc = RitzScore.acceptedLayered(pk, threshold)
 %
 % OUTPUT: acc .. [numel(pk) x 1] logical: bottom25 <= LAY_EBOT_MAX (the supporting
 %                mode is trapped) & dScore + polTerm >= threshold. The gate is the
 %                same at every threshold, so 'low' is a subset of 'high'.
sc = RitzScore.candidateScore(pk, isLayered) 
% The score the Ritz rule thresholds, per candidate
 % >> sc = RitzScore.candidateScore(pk, isLayered)
 %
 % INPUT:  1. pk        .. candidates from direction() / directionLayered()
 %         2. isLayered .. true for a layered stack
 % OUTPUT: sc .. [numel(pk) x 1]: half-space dScore; layered dScore + polTerm
 %               (the polarisation term). acceptedHS / acceptedLayered compare
 %               it with the threshold; Vis.ritzModeScores shows it.
ok = RitzScore.checkBasisHS(calcParams) 
% Whether a half-space Ritz basis resolves the band the Ritz-only rule scores;
 % warns (once per session) if not
 % >> ok = RitzScore.checkBasisHS(calcParams)
 %
 % ok = legOrder >= HS_MIN_LEG_PER_DEPTH x relDepth. The rule's thresholds hold
 % (nearly unchanged results) for legOrder 2.3-3.5 x relDepth (relDepth 20-40) and
 % collapse below 2 x relDepth. The defaults (20, 50) and
 % the production bases (20, 50), (30, 70) pass. Warns once per session;
 % warning('on', id) re-enables it.
share = RitzScore.decompose(vn, num, v) 
% Each Ritz mode's share of the modal sum S at one velocity
 % >> share = RitzScore.decompose(vn, num, v)
 %
 % INPUT:  1. vn  .. [nKeep x 1] Ritz phase velocities [km/s]
 %         2. num .. [nKeep x 1] RitzScore.modalNumerators
 %         3. v   .. velocity [km/s]
 % OUTPUT: share .. [nKeep x 1] |term_n| / sum_m |term_m|, term_n = num_n /
 %                  (v_n^2 - v^2 (1 + i ETA)) -- the contributing modes of S at v
 %                  (the measure localMode's share uses)
dT = RitzScore.dephaseTerm(vMode, slope, vRef) 
% log10 aperture dephasing factor of supporting modes
 % >> dT = RitzScore.dephaseTerm(vMode, slope, vRef)
 %
 % log10 GreenScore.dephaseFactor(gamma, dv, vRef), with the modal sum's own pole
 % half-width gamma = ETA vMode / 2 and the velocity spread dv = |slope| x
 % GreenScore.APERTURE_SIGMA. <= 0; floored at -6.
pk = RitzScore.direction(ritz, num, E1, r, velAxis, ref, vS, vL, dKdC_cell, Ebot) 
% Scored half-space candidates of one direction (threshold-independent)
 % >> pk = RitzScore.direction(ritz, num, E1, r, velAxis, ref, vS, vL, dKdC_cell, Ebot)
 %
 % INPUT:  1. ritz       .. RitzSolverSAW, calc()'d, eigenvectors present
 %         2. num, E1, r .. [nKeep x 1] from modeProfiles
 %         3. velAxis    .. score axis (SawSpectrum.velocityAxis(1.05 vL, VEL_STEP))
 %         4. ref        .. ownReference struct (vRef, floor) plus slabRes
 %                          (slabResolution, km/s): the Rayleigh side's tolerance
 %         5. vS, vL     .. min shear and longitudinal limiting velocity [km/s]
 %         6. dKdC_cell  .. the precompute's GammaMatrix.derivsByDepth cell
 %                          (built if empty -- on every call, ~0.1 s at CalcParams(30,70);
 %                          the Calc classes pass it, ad-hoc callers should too)
 %         7. Ebot       .. [nKeep x 2] from modeProfiles (the bottom-trapped gate)
 % Both ref.slabRes and Ebot are required (RitzScore:direction:missingInput): each
 % changes the result when left out.
 % OUTPUT: pk .. struct array in velocity order, one per SawSpectrum.candidates
 %               peak, fields as ModeClassification.ritzScorePeaks: vel (modal-sum
 %               peak), modeNo / modeVel (local supporting mode, localMode), score,
 %               dScore (= score + dephaseTerm), prominence, relWidth, relHeight,
 %               share, freqDisp, slope [km/s per deg], windowMass (depth-
 %               normalised: + log10(relDepth / HS_WINDOW_DEPTH_REF)), belowShear
 %               (supporting mode below vS + ref.slabRes: the Rayleigh side; the
 %               peak itself can jitter across vS), vetoed (freqDisp <
 %               MIN_FREQDISP), bottom10 / bottom25 (the supporting mode's |u|^2
 %               fraction in the deepest 10 % / 25 % of the slab; diagnostics),
 %               accepted (false here; ModeClassification sets it)
 %
 % Everything a reclassification needs, so no eigenvector is read after this.
 % Only the supporting modes' slopes are evaluated (one dK/dtheta, one quadratic
 % form per distinct mode).
pk = RitzScore.directionLayered(ritz, num, Ebot, SH, velAxis, ref, vLo, vHi, vSsub, dKdC_cell) 
% Scored layered candidates of one direction (threshold-independent)
 % >> pk = RitzScore.directionLayered(ritz, num, Ebot, SH, velAxis, ref, vLo, vHi, vSsub, dKdC_cell)
 %
 % INPUT:  1. ritz       .. RitzSolverSAW of the layered stack, calc()'d
 %         2-4. num, Ebot, SH .. modeProfiles outputs of the kept modes
 %         5. velAxis    .. layeredAxis(vLo, vHi)
 %         6. ref        .. ownReference over [vLo, vHi] (vRef, floor)
 %         7-8. vLo, vHi .. band (GreenScoreLayered.band of this direction)
 %         9. vSsub      .. the substrate's slowest shear limit [km/s] -- a label
 %                          only (belowShear, aboveSubstrateST), never a score input
 %        10. dKdC_cell  .. the precompute's GammaMatrix.derivsByDepth cell (built
 %                          if empty -- on every call; the Calc classes pass it)
 % OUTPUT: pk .. struct array in velocity order (emptyPeaks fields): vel (modal-sum
 %               peak), modeNo / modeVel (local supporting mode), score, dScore
 %               (= score + aperture dephasing at the gate's vRef), prominence,
 %               relWidth, relHeight, share, slope, SH (supporting mode's surface
 %               |u2|^2 / |u|^2), polTerm (LAY_K_POL (1 - 2 SH)), bottom10 /
 %               bottom25 (its energy fraction in the deepest 10 % / 25 % of the
 %               domain), belowShear (modeVel < vSsub), freqDisp / windowMass NaN,
 %               vetoed false, accepted false (ModeClassification sets it)
 % Candidates are SawSpectrum.candidates' peaks of the late-time modal sum below vHi,
 % kept from vLo up.
pk = RitzScore.emptyPeaks() 
% The empty candidate struct array (ModeClassification.ritzScorePeaks field set)
 % >> pk = RitzScore.emptyPeaks()
 %
 % Both rules fill the same fields (half-space: SH, polTerm NaN; layered: freqDisp,
 % windowMass NaN), so the two paths concatenate. Also the default of
 % ModeClassification.ritzScorePeaks.
fl = RitzScore.floorAtAngle(structure, angleDeg, precompStruct) 
% Median late-time modal-sum level of one direction, recomputed from the structure
 % (an independent check of ownReference)
 % >> fl = RitzScore.floorAtAngle(structure, angleDeg, precompStruct)
v = RitzScore.layeredAxis(vLo, vHi) 
% Layered modal-sum axis: VEL_STEP lattice SawSpectrum.VEL_MIN + n x VEL_STEP, column
 % >> v = RitzScore.layeredAxis(vLo, vHi)
 %
 % From min(VEL_MIN, the lattice point at or below vLo) to vHi + 0.1 km/s +
 % SawSpectrum.EDGE_TAPER, so the search (below vHi) never reaches the tapered edge
 % and a very slow stack (vLo < 0.3 km/s) keeps its whole band. On the lattice, a
 % single angle and any sweep share the same points.
[modeNo, share] = RitzScore.localMode(vn, num, vPeak, relWidth) 
% The supporting Ritz mode of a modal-sum peak: the strongest term near it
 % >> [modeNo, share] = RitzScore.localMode(vn, num, vPeak, relWidth)
 %
 % OUTPUT: modeNo .. argmax |num_n / (v_n^2 - vPeak^2 (1 + i ETA))| over the modes
 %                   with |v_n - vPeak| <= max(relWidth vPeak / 2, LOCAL_MIN_HALF);
 %                   the mode nearest vPeak if none is that close
 %         share  .. that term's fraction of sum_n |term_n| over ALL modes
 % Why local: a strong Rayleigh pole keeps a large term far from its own velocity,
 % so the global argmax over all modes can name it for a pseudo-SAW peak hundreds of
 % m/s higher, and the veto/slope/velocity would then belong to the wrong mode.
num = RitzScore.modalNumerators(ritz, vLong) 
% Per-mode modal-sum weights u3 conj(u1) at the free surface
 % >> num = RitzScore.modalNumerators(ritz, vLong)
 %
 % INPUT:  1. ritz  .. RitzSolverSAW, calc()'d, eigenvectors still present
 %                     (i.e. BEFORE any retentionLevel pruning)
 %         2. vLong .. longitudinal limiting velocity [km/s] of the half-space,
 %                     or the fastest of any layer (layered)
 % OUTPUT: num .. [nKeep x 1] complex, one entry per mode 1..nKeep, where nKeep is
 %                the last mode with vel <= MODE_VEL_FACTOR x vLong. Modes are
 %                velocity-sorted, so num(n) belongs to ritz.vels(n)
 %
 % The surface displacement is the top row of calcDisplacement (top point of the
 % top layer = the free surface). The numerators survive pruning of the
 % eigenvectors: they are all that reclassification needs later.
[num, E1, r, Ebot, SH] = RitzScore.modeProfiles(ritz, vLong) 
% Modal-sum weights and near-surface measures of the kept modes, one displacement call
 % >> [num, E1, r] = RitzScore.modeProfiles(ritz, vLong)
 % >> [num, E1, r, Ebot, SH] = RitzScore.modeProfiles(ritz, vLong)
 %
 % INPUT:  as modalNumerators (layered: vLong = the fastest longitudinal limit of
 %         any layer)
 % OUTPUT: num .. [nKeep x 1] u3 conj(u1) at the free surface (= modalNumerators)
 %         E1  .. [nKeep x 1] fraction of the mode's |u|^2 within one wavelength of
 %                the surface (depth-integrated on the profile grid)
 %         r   .. [nKeep x 1] |u3(surface)|^2 / depth-mean |u|^2 / relDepth: the
 %                surface-normal amplitude a TGS probe reads, relative to the mode's
 %                spread over the slab
 %         Ebot .. [nKeep x 2] fraction of the mode's |u|^2 in the deepest 10 % / 25 %
 %                of the slab (0.10 / 0.25 for a uniform profile). A leaky wave
 %                the rigid-bottom slab cannot radiate can form "U-shaped" modes,
 %                with energy at the surface AND the bottom (computed with
 %                GuliweRR: Ebot10 / Ebot25 >= 0.84 on such modes, <= 0.73 on
 %                true pseudo-SAWs)
 %         SH  .. [nKeep x 1] |u2|^2 / |u|^2 at the free surface (0 sagittal .. 1
 %                shear-horizontal)
 % Half-space: plain |u|^2 on layer 1's uniform grid. Layered (nLay > 1): every
 % layer's profile (calcDisplacement: the same point count per layer, layer 1 the
 % deepest) weighted by its density and its own trapezoid depth step -- the layers
 % differ in thickness, so unweighted row sums would over-weight a thin film --
 % and depths measured over the whole domain: E1 = energy fraction within one
 % wavelength of the surface, r = |u3(surface)|^2 / depth-integrated rho |u|^2,
 % Ebot = energy fraction in the deepest 10 % / 25 % of the domain (the layered
 % rule's trapped gate, LAY_EBOT_MAX).
ref = RitzScore.ownReference(structure, vn, num, velAxis, fLo, fHi) 
% Floor reference of ONE direction from its already computed modal sum
 % >> ref = RitzScore.ownReference(structure, vn, num, velAxis, 0.8 * vS, vTf)   % half-space
 % >> ref = RitzScore.ownReference(structure, vn, num, velAxis, vLo, vHi)        % layered
 %
 % INPUT:  1. structure .. Structure (any orientation: only the surface layer's
 %                         Hill shear modulus and density are read)
 %         2. vn, num   .. [nKeep x 1] Ritz velocities and modalNumerators
 %         3. velAxis   .. [nVel x 1] the direction's Ritz score axis
 %         4-5. fLo, fHi .. floor band [km/s]: half-space [0.8 x min shear, the
 %                         fastest shear limit vTf]; layered the whole band
 %                         [0.3 vSmin, 1.05 vLmax] (GreenScoreLayered.band)
 % OUTPUT: ref .. struct: vRef [km/s] = sqrt(G_Hill/rho) of the surface layer (the
 %                late-time gate's and the dephasing's time scale); floor = median
 %                late-time |S| over [fLo, fHi]
 %
 % The classification's floor on the Ritz-only path (the Calc classes call it per
 % angle).
 % Why the half-space band ends at vTf: between vTf and vL the slab's modes are not
 % converged in legOrder at the production bases, so a floor taken there would sit
 % 0.15-0.2 dec too high and every score, and the best thresholds, would move with
 % legOrder / relDepth. Over [0.8 vS, vTf] the floor scales with relDepth exactly as
 % the prominences do, so the score does not depend on the basis. The layered band is GreenScoreLayered's: no
 % layer is singled out. Why the surface layer's vRef on layers too (a near-surface
 % property): a gate at the slowest shear limit of any layer classifies NiTi/MgO's
 % sagittal-type waves the same but ranks Love waves far worse against spurious
 % peaks.
thr = RitzScore.resolveThresholdHS(t) 
% Half-space dScore threshold from 'high' | 'low' | a number (see acceptedHS)
 % >> thr = RitzScore.resolveThresholdHS(t)
thr = RitzScore.resolveThresholdLayered(t) 
% Layered score threshold from 'low' | 'high' | a number (see acceptedLayered)
 % >> thr = RitzScore.resolveThresholdLayered(t)
dD = RitzScore.slabResolution(mat, vS, thetaStar, relDepth) 
% How far above the true min shear velocity the Ritz slab can place it [km/s]
 % >> dD = RitzScore.slabResolution(mat, vS, thetaStar, relDepth)
 %
 % INPUT:  1. mat       .. the half-space Material of the ROTATED structure
 %         2. vS        .. min(shearLimVels) [km/s]
 %         3. thetaStar .. its sagittal root angle (CalcCommon.limitingAndBulk
 %                         shearLimTheta of that entry)
 %         4. relDepth  .. Ritz domain depth [wavelengths]
 % OUTPUT: dD .. >= 0 [km/s]
 %
 % The slow-shear limit is a bulk wave grazing the surface, q = k_z/k -> q0. A
 % slab of depth D (rigid bottom) cannot hold a single grazing partial wave: its
 % lowest standing pattern pairs two partial waves whose k_z differ by pi/D
 % (quarter wave), i.e. a window of width w = 1/(2 D) in q. Its trace velocity is
 % at least the larger of the two partial waves' trace velocities
 %     v_tr(q) = v_phase(n(q)) sqrt(1 + q^2),  n = [1 0 q]/sqrt(1 + q^2),
 % so the slab's minimum is min over window positions [a, a + w] around q0 of
 % max(v_tr(a), v_tr(a + w)). dD = that minus vS. Ritz eigenvalues are upper
 % bounds, so the error is one-sided: a Rayleigh wave hugging vS can sit up to dD
 % above it in the slab. (Synthetic cubic, Zener ratio 20, (112) cut: modes <= 0.18 m/s
 % above vS, dD 0.21-0.30 m/s; other cuts computed: dD 0.2-0.6 m/s.)
 % thetaStar is turned into q0 by whichever of tan, -tan, cot reproduces vS
 % (LimitingVels' angle convention); 11 window positions, ~24 3x3 eigenproblems.
S = RitzScore.spectrum(vn, num, velAxis) 
% Complex Ritz modal sum on a velocity axis
 % >> S = RitzScore.spectrum(vn, num, velAxis)
 %
 % INPUT:  1. vn      .. [nKeep x 1] Ritz phase velocities [km/s]
 %         2. num     .. [nKeep x 1] RitzScore.modalNumerators
 %         3. velAxis .. [nVel x 1] km/s (SawSpectrum.velocityAxis)
 % OUTPUT: S .. [nVel x 1] complex
 %
 % A loop over modes, not an [nKeep x nVel] matrix: the same cost, without a
 % ~10-20 MB temporary per angle (which a thread pool would multiply).
wm = RitzScore.windowMass(vn, w, vPeak, vS, above) 
% log10 of the window weight around a peak, from the modes on one side of vS
 % >> wm = RitzScore.windowMass(vn, w, vPeak, vS, above)
 %
 % Sum of w over the modes within max(WINDOW_REL vPeak, WINDOW_ABS) of vPeak,
 % taking only modes at or above vS if above is true, only modes below vS
 % otherwise (a bound Rayleigh mode must not lend weight to a pseudo-SAW just
 % above vS, nor the reverse). direction() passes the candidate's side
 % (~belowShear). Floored at 1e-12 before the log.
w = RitzScore.windowWeights(vn, fd, E1, r, vS, vL) 
% Per-mode near-surface weight used by windowMass
 % >> w = RitzScore.windowWeights(vn, fd, E1, r, vS, vL)
 %
 % w = r fd below vS; r E1 fd at or above vS (a leaky mode must ALSO keep its
 % energy near the surface -- a bound mode is surface-confined anyway); 0 above
 % WINDOW_TOP x vL.

SawSpectrum value class

% SawSpectrum -- the late-time spectrum machinery the existence scores share: the
 % damping of the default existence solves (ETA), the axis lattice (VEL_MIN,
 % latticeAxis, EDGE_TAPER, AXIS_HI_X_VL), the late-time gate (lateTime, GATE_PERIODS)
 % and the peak search (peaksOf, candidates, MAX_REL_WIDTH). GreenSpotScore and
 % RitzScore read the gated spectra and the peak search; GreenScore uses the analytic
 % form of the same gate (GATE_PERIODS) and the width cap.
 %
 % EXPERIMENTAL: checked against the measured maps published in Grabec et al. 2024 and the
 % UTGS paper (arXiv:2510.10696); the scores, their scales and the interface may change.
 %
 % These are what make the scores comparable -- the same linewidth, the same axis
 % start and the same late-time cut -- so they belong to none of the methods.
 %  - ETA = 3e-3, not the GreenSolver default 3e-4: at 3e-4 a bound Rayleigh pole
 %    is ~0.5 m/s wide, narrower than the axis step, so its sampled height is grid
 %    luck and dwarfs a leaky pseudo-SAW of the same residue. A measurement-like
 %    linewidth conserves the residue and makes heights comparable.
 %  - Late-time gate (lateTime): surface-wave poles ring for tens of periods;
 %    bulk-continuum features (limiting-velocity shoulders, Van Hove edges) decay
 %    within a few. The gate starts GATE_PERIODS periods of vRef = sqrt(G_Hill/rho)
 %    of the surface layer, so no grating wavelength enters.
 %  - Zero-padding + tail taper: the gated time signal is zero-padded PAD_FACTOR x,
 %    which interpolates the late-time spectrum exactly onto a finer axis; a
 %    raised-cosine fall over the last TAIL_TAPER of the causal window stops a hard
 %    cut from rippling the spectrum into spurious candidates.
 %  - candidates: prominence, not height, since most false candidates are bumps on
 %    the flank of a stronger feature.
 % See docs/reference/architecture/forwardCalcs_modeClassification.md.
 %
 % Value class with Constant properties and Static methods only.
 %
 % GuliweRR v6.0

Properties

SawSpectrum.AXIS_HI_X_VL (default: 1.05) 
half-space axes run to AXIS_HI_X_VL x longLimVel (+ EDGE_TAPER), so a peak at the longitudinal limit is still interior
SawSpectrum.EDGE_TAPER (default: 0.1) 
km/s -- Tukey taper at both ends of the axis
SawSpectrum.ETA (default: 0.003) 
GreenSolver damping of the default existence solves
SawSpectrum.GATE_PERIODS (default: 3) 
late-time gate start, periods of vRef
SawSpectrum.MAX_REL_WIDTH (default: 0.12) 
candidate half-prominence width / velocity ceiling
SawSpectrum.PAD_FACTOR (default: 4) 
zero-padding of the gated time signal = exact band-limited interpolation of the late-time spectrum
SawSpectrum.RAMP_PERIODS (default: 0.6) 
gate turn-on (effectively abrupt, like the measured front cut)
SawSpectrum.TAIL_TAPER (default: 0.2) 
raised-cosine fall over the last 20 % of the causal time window: at 4 m/s the window (1/(2 dv) = 125 ns) ends before long-lived ringing has decayed, and a hard cut ripples the spectrum
SawSpectrum.VEL_MIN (default: 0.3) 
km/s -- velocity axis start (below any SAW seen)
SawSpectrum.VEL_STEP (default: 0.004) 
km/s -- default uniform axis step (FFT-able)

Methods

obj = SawSpectrum() 
% SawSpectrum -- the late-time spectrum machinery the existence scores share: the
 % damping of the default existence solves (ETA), the axis lattice (VEL_MIN,
 % latticeAxis, EDGE_TAPER, AXIS_HI_X_VL), the late-time gate (lateTime, GATE_PERIODS)
 % and the peak search (peaksOf, candidates, MAX_REL_WIDTH). GreenSpotScore and
 % RitzScore read the gated spectra and the peak search; GreenScore uses the analytic
 % form of the same gate (GATE_PERIODS) and the width cap.
 %
 % EXPERIMENTAL: checked against the measured maps published in Grabec et al. 2024 and the
 % UTGS paper (arXiv:2510.10696); the scores, their scales and the interface may change.
 %
 % These are what make the scores comparable -- the same linewidth, the same axis
 % start and the same late-time cut -- so they belong to none of the methods.
 %  - ETA = 3e-3, not the GreenSolver default 3e-4: at 3e-4 a bound Rayleigh pole
 %    is ~0.5 m/s wide, narrower than the axis step, so its sampled height is grid
 %    luck and dwarfs a leaky pseudo-SAW of the same residue. A measurement-like
 %    linewidth conserves the residue and makes heights comparable.
 %  - Late-time gate (lateTime): surface-wave poles ring for tens of periods;
 %    bulk-continuum features (limiting-velocity shoulders, Van Hove edges) decay
 %    within a few. The gate starts GATE_PERIODS periods of vRef = sqrt(G_Hill/rho)
 %    of the surface layer, so no grating wavelength enters.
 %  - Zero-padding + tail taper: the gated time signal is zero-padded PAD_FACTOR x,
 %    which interpolates the late-time spectrum exactly onto a finer axis; a
 %    raised-cosine fall over the last TAIL_TAPER of the causal window stops a hard
 %    cut from rippling the spectrum into spurious candidates.
 %  - candidates: prominence, not height, since most false candidates are bumps on
 %    the flank of a stronger feature.
 % See docs/reference/architecture/forwardCalcs_modeClassification.md.
 %
 % Value class with Constant properties and Static methods only.
 %
 % GuliweRR v6.0
pk = SawSpectrum.candidates(G, velAxis, vSearchMax, ref, pad, tailFrac) 
% Scored candidates (late-time peaks) of one direction's spectrum
 % >> pk = SawSpectrum.candidates(G, velAxis, vSearchMax, ref)
 % >> pk = SawSpectrum.candidates(G, velAxis, vSearchMax, ref, pad, tailFrac)
 %
 % INPUT:  1. G          .. [nVel x 1] complex spectrum (e.g. RitzScore's modal sum)
 %         2. velAxis    .. [nVel x 1] uniform km/s (SawSpectrum.velocityAxis)
 %         3. vSearchMax .. search bound [km/s]
 %         4. ref        .. struct: vRef (gate reference velocity, km/s) and floor
 %                          (the score's normaliser)
 %  (opt)  5-6. pad, tailFrac .. passed to lateTime (defaults: the Green standard,
 %                          PAD_FACTOR and TAIL_TAPER; RitzScore passes 1, 0)
 % OUTPUT: pk .. struct array (0..N), fields vel, score, prominence, height,
 %               relWidth, relHeight (height / tallest candidate here -- for
 %               display only, never part of the decision)
 %
 % Candidates are the peaks of the late-time spectrum (peaksOf).
[A, vOut] = SawSpectrum.lateTime(G, velAxis, vRef, pad, tailFrac, gatePeriods) 
% |late-time spectrum|: the first GATE_PERIODS periods of vRef removed
 % >> [A, vOut] = SawSpectrum.lateTime(G, velAxis, vRef)
 % >> [A, vOut] = SawSpectrum.lateTime(G, velAxis, vRef, pad, tailFrac)
 % >> [A, vOut] = SawSpectrum.lateTime(G, velAxis, vRef, pad, tailFrac, gatePeriods)
 %
 % INPUT:  1. G        .. [nVel x nAng] complex spectrum on the uniform velAxis
 %         2. velAxis  .. [nVel x 1] km/s
 %         3. vRef     .. reference velocity [km/s]
 %  (opt)  4. pad      .. zero-padding factor of the gated time signal (default
 %                        PAD_FACTOR): A is returned on vOut, pad x finer than
 %                        velAxis (exact band-limited interpolation -- the gated
 %                        signal has no content past half the window)
 %  (opt)  5. tailFrac .. raised-cosine fall over this fraction at the end of the
 %                        causal window (default TAIL_TAPER; 0 = hard cut)
 %  (opt)  6. gatePeriods .. gate start in periods of vRef (default GATE_PERIODS;
 %                        GreenSpotScore reads gates 2 and 4). A vector gives one
 %                        spectrum per gate from a single forward FFT (page ig of A)
 % The defaults are the Green-path standard. RitzScore passes pad = 1,
 % tailFrac = 0: its analytic spectrum is evaluated on its own 2 m/s axis, and its
 % thresholds were calibrated without padding or taper.
 % OUTPUT: A    .. [pad*nVel x nAng (x numel(gatePeriods))], NaN on the tapered axis ends
 %         vOut .. [pad*nVel x 1] km/s, the axis of A
 %
 % At fixed |k| velocity is frequency: with a formal wavelength of 1 um,
 % f [1/ns] = v [km/s] and one period at vRef lasts 1/vRef ns. FFT gives the
 % impulse response on one period 1/dv; causal content sits in the first half
 % (the retarded Green's function), negative times are discarded. The gate
 % (raised-cosine turn-on of RAMP_PERIODS) removes the early, short-lived bulk
 % contributions; surface-wave poles survive. Isolated NaNs from GreenSolver
 % are interpolated first -- one NaN would poison the whole FFT column.
v = SawSpectrum.latticeAxis(nLo, nHi, step) 
% Axis on the lattice VEL_MIN + n x step, n = nLo..nHi, column [km/s]
 % >> v = SawSpectrum.latticeAxis(nLo, nHi, step)
 %
 % The one builder of every Calc / score axis (GreenScore.gridAxis / bandAxis,
 % GreenScoreLayered.gridAxis, RitzScore.layeredAxis, SpotGreen.solveAxis, the
 % GreenSpotScore direction cache): built as VEL_MIN + n*step with integer n, not
 % with the colon operator on velocities (which fills a range from both ends), so a
 % point has the same value in a short axis and in a longer one with the same step
 % -- what makes a single angle score exactly as inside a sweep. The callers choose
 % the n range.
out = SawSpectrum.memo(cacheName, maxSize, errPrefix, action, key, value) 
% Per-session memo shared by the score references: 'get' | 'put' | 'clear' | 'size'
 % >> v = SawSpectrum.memo(cacheName, maxSize, errPrefix, 'get', key)   % [] on a miss
 % >> SawSpectrum.memo(cacheName, maxSize, errPrefix, 'put', key, value)
 %
 % INPUT:  1. cacheName .. one memo per name (e.g. 'GreenScore', 'GreenSpotScore')
 %         2. maxSize   .. entries kept; the oldest goes first
 %         3. errPrefix .. error-id prefix for an unknown action (<errPrefix>:badAction)
 %         4. action, key, value
 %
 % Keys are compared with isequal (the callers pass getByteStreamFromArray of their
 % inputs; structures through SawSpectrum.structureKey). A persistent variable: emptied by clear classes, not shared with pool
 % workers (the callers do not memoise there).
P = SawSpectrum.peaksOf(A, velAxis, vSearchMax) 
% Peaks of one late-time spectrum: below vSearchMax, width <= MAX_REL_WIDTH x velocity
 % >> P = SawSpectrum.peaksOf(A, velAxis, vSearchMax)
 %
 % INPUT:  1. A          .. [nVel x 1] |late-time spectrum| (lateTime output, NaN
 %                          on the tapered ends)
 %         2. velAxis    .. [nVel x 1] its axis [km/s]
 %         3. vSearchMax .. search bound [km/s]
 % OUTPUT: P .. struct of column vectors vel, prom, height, relWidth
 %
 % The local maxima (findpeaks, half-prominence widths, prominence floor 1e-4 x
 % the largest value) of A below vSearchMax with half-prominence width <=
 % MAX_REL_WIDTH x velocity. The search runs to vSearchMax + 0.05 km/s so that a
 % peak at the bound is still interior (docs/pitfalls.md MTH4).
key = SawSpectrum.structureKey(structure) 
% Memo key of a Structure's physical content (no names, no nMaxModes)
 % >> key = SawSpectrum.structureKey(structure)
 %
 % The byte stream, per layer, of the elastic tensor and its symmetry class, the
 % density, dirMat, and the CalcParams relDepth, legOrder and realThickness. The
 % score references (GreenScore.reference, GreenSpotScore.reference) are a function
 % of these only, so the same stack under another Structure or Material name, or
 % with another nMaxModes, hits the memo instead of re-solving 12-132 directions.
 % A byte stream, not the numbers: isequal(NaN, NaN) is false, and realThickness is
 % NaN when unset.
velAxis = SawSpectrum.velocityAxis(vMax, step) 
% Uniform velocity axis from VEL_MIN to vMax [km/s], column
 % >> velAxis = SawSpectrum.velocityAxis(vMax)
 % >> velAxis = SawSpectrum.velocityAxis(vMax, step)

DispersionBranches value class

% DispersionBranches — tracks each Ritz mode's physical identity across a fixed-angle
 % wavelength/wavevector sweep (CalcWavevecAngSweep2Layered, CalcWavevecAngSweep2Plate),
 % so a dispersion curve can be drawn as continuous branches. A separate utility, not
 % part of the surface-wave classification. See
 % docs/reference/architecture/forwardCalcs_modeClassification.md, "DispersionBranches.track".
 %
 % Value class with Static methods only.
 %
 % GuliweRR v6.0

Methods

obj = DispersionBranches() 
% DispersionBranches — tracks each Ritz mode's physical identity across a fixed-angle
 % wavelength/wavevector sweep (CalcWavevecAngSweep2Layered, CalcWavevecAngSweep2Plate),
 % so a dispersion curve can be drawn as continuous branches. A separate utility, not
 % part of the surface-wave classification. See
 % docs/reference/architecture/forwardCalcs_modeClassification.md, "DispersionBranches.track".
 %
 % Value class with Static methods only.
 %
 % GuliweRR v6.0
branchModeNoMap = DispersionBranches.track(sweepResult, angleIdx) 
% Track each Ritz mode's physical identity across a fixed-angle wavelength
 % sweep, by maximum eigenvector overlap (MAC) with the previous step
 % >> branchModeNoMap = DispersionBranches.track(sweepResult)
 % >> branchModeNoMap = DispersionBranches.track(sweepResult, angleIdx)
 %
 % INPUT:  1. sweepResult .. CalcWavevecAngSweep2Layered or
 %                           CalcWavevecAngSweep2Plate (both expose the same
 %                           sweeps{iw}.cs{angleIdx}.ritzSolver shape),
 %                           already calc()'d with retentionLevel='full'.
 %                           That is the only level keeping every Ritz
 %                           eigenvector column rather than the classified
 %                           ones alone, which matters here because a branch
 %                           can drift through classification wobble between
 %                           steps
 %  (opt) 2. angleIdx      .. which angle to track at (default 1). This stays
 %                           a FIXED-ANGLE dispersion-curve case; angleIdx
 %                           only picks which slice of a multi-angle
 %                           sweepResult to run on
 % OUTPUT: branchModeNoMap .. [nWav x nCompModes] double. Column im is one
 %                            tracked physical branch; branchModeNoMap(iw,im)
 %                            is the raw Ritz mode index at wavelength-step iw
 %                            continuing branch im's identity.
 %                            branchModeNoMap(1,:) == 1:nCompModes by
 %                            definition, and every row is a permutation of
 %                            1:nCompModes (each raw mode on exactly one branch)
 %
 % Algorithm: seed every branch at its own raw index at step 1, then at each
 % later step compute one [nCompModes x nCompModes] MAC matrix between the
 % current reference eigenvectors and that step's full eigenvector matrix in
 % a single matrix product, assign branches to candidates ONE-TO-ONE by
 % maximising the total MAC (matchpairs, a linear assignment), and chain the
 % reference forward to the matched columns.
 %
 % MAC here is MASS-WEIGHTED: |a^H sqrt(M_a M_b) b| on the unconstrained
 % Legendre coefficients, M being each step's own block-diagonal mass matrix
 % (layer factor relDepth*rho), i.e. the overlap in the physical inner product
 % integral(rho u^H u dz) (massWeightedUnit). The plain Euclidean overlap of
 % the coefficients weights every layer alike, however thin: for a film of
 % relDepth 0.001 its coefficients hold ~98 % of a SAW eigenvector's norm but
 % <1 % of its mass norm, unrelated modes then reach MAC ~0.98 with it, and
 % the assignment handed the SAW branch to a 13 km/s film mode.
 %
 % Why one-to-one: an independent per-branch argmax would let several branches
 % claim one candidate, and a candidate no branch claimed would fall off every
 % branch for the rest of the sweep. Inside a dense, near-degenerate cluster (a
 % layered stack's substrate box modes above its bulk shear velocity) that
 % happens at almost every step, so a guided mode emerging from the cluster as k
 % grows would never be plotted. Where the argmax gives distinct matches the
 % assignment is the same. Simultaneous angle-and-wavelength tracking stays out of scope:
 % MAC-chaining across a genuine 2D grid needs a real 2D continuity scheme,
 % not a choice of which 1D slice to chain along.
[vels, modeNos, seedModeNo] = DispersionBranches.trackSeededBranch(sweepResult, angleIdx) 
% Velocity of the branch seeded at the slowest accepted 'SAW' entry of the
 % first wavelength step, followed through the sweep by DispersionBranches.track
 % >> vels = DispersionBranches.trackSeededBranch(sweepResult)
 % >> [vels, modeNos, seedModeNo] = DispersionBranches.trackSeededBranch(sweepResult, angleIdx)
 %
 % INPUT:  1. sweepResult .. CalcWavevecAngSweep2Layered, calc()'d with
 %                           retentionLevel='full' (as for track)
 %  (opt) 2. angleIdx      .. which angle of the sweep (default 1)
 % OUTPUT: vels       .. [nWav x 1] Ritz velocity of the seeded branch at every
 %                       wavelength step [km/s]
 %         modeNos    .. [nWav x 1] its raw Ritz mode index at every step
 %         seedModeNo .. the seed entry's modeNo at step 1
 %
 % The seed is the accepted 'SAW' entry with the slowest bound Ritz mode (modeVel) of
 % sweepResult.sweeps{1}.significantModesMap{angleIdx}, i.e. the classification
 % under the sweep's active method and standard at the first wavelength step (the
 % Rayleigh-type wave when the steps run from thin to thick film). Errors if
 % that step has no accepted 'SAW' entry.

GreenPeaks value class

% GreenPeaks — generic peak finder for a |G| curve: the local maxima inside a mask
 % whose height exceeds a fraction of the curve's global maximum.
 %
 % Not part of the surface-wave classification (the existence scores GreenScore,
 % GreenScoreLayered, GreenSpotScore and RitzScore have their own candidate searches).
 % Used for isoFreq spectra (kParRange standing in for the velocity axis). See
 % docs/reference/architecture/forwardCalcs_modeClassification.md, "GreenPeaks.find".
 %
 % Value class with a Constant property and a Static method only.
 %
 % GuliweRR v6.0

Methods

obj = GreenPeaks() 
% GreenPeaks — generic peak finder for a |G| curve: the local maxima inside a mask
 % whose height exceeds a fraction of the curve's global maximum.
 %
 % Not part of the surface-wave classification (the existence scores GreenScore,
 % GreenScoreLayered, GreenSpotScore and RitzScore have their own candidate searches).
 % Used for isoFreq spectra (kParRange standing in for the velocity axis). See
 % docs/reference/architecture/forwardCalcs_modeClassification.md, "GreenPeaks.find".
 %
 % Value class with a Constant property and a Static method only.
 %
 % GuliweRR v6.0
peaks = GreenPeaks.find(absG, velAxis, mask, heightFrac) 
% Peak-find abs(G) within a velocity-axis mask
 % >> peaks = GreenPeaks.find(absG, velAxis, mask)
 % >> peaks = GreenPeaks.find(absG, velAxis, mask, heightFrac)
 %
 % INPUT:  1. absG       .. [nVel x 1] abs(G) on velAxis
 %         2. velAxis    .. [nVel x 1] axis (must be increasing -- required by
 %                          findpeaks(Y,X) when X is supplied)
 %         3. mask       .. logical [nVel x 1], the sub-range to search
 %  (opt)  4. heightFrac .. height floor as a fraction of max(absG) over the WHOLE
 %                          array, not just the mask (default HEIGHT_FRAC)
 % OUTPUT: peaks .. struct array with fields vel, height, prominence, width
 %                  (0..N entries)
 %
 % Uses MATLAB's findpeaks (Signal Processing Toolbox). prominence (height above
 % the higher of a peak's two bounding valleys) and width (half-prominence width,
 % 'WidthReference'='halfprom', in the units of velAxis) are reported for every
 % accepted peak but NOT filtered on: the height floor is the only gate.

SpotGreen value class

% SpotGreen — the half-space Green's function as a finite measurement sees it: the
 % coherent sum of the complex Green's function over the wavevectors a ProbeKernel
 % couples to. A picture tool (CalcAngSweepHS.calcSpotGreenMap,
 % CalcSingleHS.calcSpotGreen); it never changes a classification.
 % EXPERIMENTAL: tried on Fe3Al(316) only, not validated against measurements; the
 % interface may change.
 %
 % For one displayed direction theta and kernel nodes (offset o_j, |k|/k0 - 1 = delta_m)
 % with weights w_mj (ProbeKernel.sample):
 %
 %     X(v) = sum_j sum_m w_mj R(theta + o_j, v / (1 + delta_m))
 %
 % R(phi, v) = the one-sided Green component in phi's own propagation frame at |k| = 1.
 % Directions are solved on [0, 180) only: phi + 180 is the same solve read through
 % reciprocity, R(phi + 180) = s_I s_J G_JI(phi) = s_I s_J G_minus(phi), s = (-1, -1, 1)
 % (a half turn flips x1 and x2), so a +-k symmetric kernel's two lobes give the
 % Maznev +-k combination: the point kernel with 'G13' gives G13_eff exactly.
 %
 % The delta sum resamples each direction's spectrum at v / (1 + delta) by Keys cubic
 % convolution on the uniform solve axis (local, exact at the nodes, coefficients
 % shared by every direction): no extra Green solve for the |k| spread.
 %
 % Axes: every direction is solved on ONE structure-wide axis VEL_MIN + n x velStep
 % (from below VEL_MIN / (1 + delta_max) to above vEnd / (1 + delta_min), vEnd =
 % 1.05 x the largest bulk longitudinal velocity over in-plane directions -- an upper
 % bound of every direction's longLimVel), and X is reported on GreenScore.gridAxis(vEnd,
 % velStep). The contributions to one angle are added in a fixed order (sorted
 % direction, then +k before -k lobe, then node), so a single angle equals the same
 % angle in any sweep -- bit for bit as far as the BLAS library rounds one column of a
 % matrix product independently of the others (true on the machines tested; otherwise
 % to single-precision round-off).
 %
 % Cost and precision: the direction solves are cheap (~0.3 s for a 360-direction SRAS
 % map); the spot sum dominates. Per direction and lobe, the resampled block T is formed
 % once and multiplied by the weight columns of every (angle, node) pair that reads it --
 % and the two quadrature checks' columns -- in ONE matrix product, in single precision
 % (X is a picture stored as single anyway: |X| changes by ~1e-6 of its peak against a
 % double-precision sum; the direction solves stay double).
 %
 % Quadrature check, for free: the directions on the doubled step and the |k| nodes on
 % the doubled step are subsets of those already used; X is re-summed on each subset
 % (weights renormalised) and the largest change of |X|, relative to max |X| of that
 % angle, is reported (info.dirConvergence / relKConvergence). A large dirConvergence
 % is the "comb": the branch moves further between neighbouring directions than a
 % line is wide, so the sum shows separate poles instead of a smooth ridge.
 %
 % Static methods only. Half-space only.
 %
 % GuliweRR v6.0

Properties

SpotGreen.CONV_WARN (default: 0.05) 
warn if a quadrature check changes |X| by more than this
SpotGreen.END_FACTOR (default: 1.05) 
axis end: END_FACTOR x max bulk longitudinal velocity
SpotGreen.ETA_STEPS (default: 4)
