default |k| step at most Eta / ETA_STEPS: neighbouring nodes' poles (v x step apart) must overlap (pole full width ~ Eta x v), or the |k| sum shows them as a comb; at Eta/4 the half-density check (Eta/2) stays ~0.1 %
SpotGreen.MAX_DIRECTIONS (default: 20000) 
error above this many direction solves
SpotGreen.VEL_STEP (default: 0.004) 
km/s -- default solve and output axis step

Methods

obj = SpotGreen() 
% SpotGreen — the half-space Green's function as a finite measurement sees it: the
 % coherent sum of the complex Green's function over the wavevectors a ProbeKernel
 % couples to. A picture tool (CalcAngSweepHS.calcSpotGreenMap,
 % CalcSingleHS.calcSpotGreen); it never changes a classification.
 % EXPERIMENTAL: tried on Fe3Al(316) only, not validated against measurements; the
 % interface may change.
 %
 % For one displayed direction theta and kernel nodes (offset o_j, |k|/k0 - 1 = delta_m)
 % with weights w_mj (ProbeKernel.sample):
 %
 %     X(v) = sum_j sum_m w_mj R(theta + o_j, v / (1 + delta_m))
 %
 % R(phi, v) = the one-sided Green component in phi's own propagation frame at |k| = 1.
 % Directions are solved on [0, 180) only: phi + 180 is the same solve read through
 % reciprocity, R(phi + 180) = s_I s_J G_JI(phi) = s_I s_J G_minus(phi), s = (-1, -1, 1)
 % (a half turn flips x1 and x2), so a +-k symmetric kernel's two lobes give the
 % Maznev +-k combination: the point kernel with 'G13' gives G13_eff exactly.
 %
 % The delta sum resamples each direction's spectrum at v / (1 + delta) by Keys cubic
 % convolution on the uniform solve axis (local, exact at the nodes, coefficients
 % shared by every direction): no extra Green solve for the |k| spread.
 %
 % Axes: every direction is solved on ONE structure-wide axis VEL_MIN + n x velStep
 % (from below VEL_MIN / (1 + delta_max) to above vEnd / (1 + delta_min), vEnd =
 % 1.05 x the largest bulk longitudinal velocity over in-plane directions -- an upper
 % bound of every direction's longLimVel), and X is reported on GreenScore.gridAxis(vEnd,
 % velStep). The contributions to one angle are added in a fixed order (sorted
 % direction, then +k before -k lobe, then node), so a single angle equals the same
 % angle in any sweep -- bit for bit as far as the BLAS library rounds one column of a
 % matrix product independently of the others (true on the machines tested; otherwise
 % to single-precision round-off).
 %
 % Cost and precision: the direction solves are cheap (~0.3 s for a 360-direction SRAS
 % map); the spot sum dominates. Per direction and lobe, the resampled block T is formed
 % once and multiplied by the weight columns of every (angle, node) pair that reads it --
 % and the two quadrature checks' columns -- in ONE matrix product, in single precision
 % (X is a picture stored as single anyway: |X| changes by ~1e-6 of its peak against a
 % double-precision sum; the direction solves stay double).
 %
 % Quadrature check, for free: the directions on the doubled step and the |k| nodes on
 % the doubled step are subsets of those already used; X is re-summed on each subset
 % (weights renormalised) and the largest change of |X|, relative to max |X| of that
 % angle, is reported (info.dirConvergence / relKConvergence). A large dirConvergence
 % is the "comb": the branch moves further between neighbouring directions than a
 % line is wide, so the sum shows separate poles instead of a smooth ridge.
 %
 % Static methods only. Half-space only.
 %
 % GuliweRR v6.0
[I, J] = SpotGreen.componentIndices(component) 
% 'GIJ' -> I, J
[absX, vOut, info] = SpotGreen.compute(structureBase, angles, kernel, opts, useParallel) 
% |X| for a set of displayed directions
 % >> [absX, vOut, info] = SpotGreen.compute(structureBase, angles, kernel, opts, useParallel)
 %
 % INPUT:  1. structureBase .. UNROTATED half-space Structure
 %         2. angles        .. displayed directions [deg]
 %         3. kernel        .. ProbeKernel
 %         4. opts          .. SpotGreen.options(kernel, ...)
 %  (opt)  5. useParallel   .. "auto" (default) | "always" | "never" -- thread pool
 %                             over the direction solves (the result does not
 %                             depend on it)
 % OUTPUT: absX .. [nVel x nAngles] single, |X|
 %         vOut .. [nVel x 1] the output axis [km/s]
 %         info .. struct: kernel, the opts, vEnd, nDirections, nNodes ([nD nO]),
 %                 dirConvergence / relKConvergence ([nAngles x 1], NaN where the
 %                 kernel has a single node in that coordinate)
opts = SpotGreen.options(kernel, varargin) 
% Name-Value options of calcSpotGreenMap / calcSpotGreen, defaults filled in
 % >> opts = SpotGreen.options(kernel, 'DirStep', 0.1, 'RelKStep', 1e-3, 'Eta', 3e-3, 'VelStep', 0.004)
 %
 % Defaults: DirStep = kernel.dirStep; RelKStep = min(kernel.relKStep, Eta /
 % ETA_STEPS); Eta = SawSpectrum.ETA (the damping of the stored Green maps); VelStep
 % = VEL_STEP. An explicit RelKStep is used as given.
M = SpotGreen.resampleMatrix(vOut, delta, vS1, velStep, nS) 
% Sparse Keys-cubic resampling: rows (m-1) nOut + i give R(vOut(i) / (1 + delta(m)))
 % >> M = SpotGreen.resampleMatrix(vOut, delta, vS1, velStep, nS)
 %
 % OUTPUT: M .. sparse [nOut nD x nS], four entries per row (one where the point
 %              falls on a solve node, as for delta = 0)
vS = SpotGreen.solveAxis(vOutEnd, delta, velStep) 
% The structure-wide solve axis VEL_MIN + n x velStep covering every v / (1 + delta)
 % >> vS = SpotGreen.solveAxis(vOutEnd, delta, velStep)
dc = SpotGreen.solveDirections(structureBase, dirs, vS, eta, component, useParallel) 
% One Green solve per direction: one-sided G_IJ(+k) and G_IJ(-k) = G_JI(+k)
 % >> dc = SpotGreen.solveDirections(structureBase, dirs, vS, eta, component, useParallel)
 %
 % OUTPUT: dc .. struct: dir [nDir x 1] deg, Gp / Gm [nS x nDir] complex, vS, eta,
 %               component
 %
 % Each direction is solved alone (angle 0 on its own rotated copy -- the
 % double-rotation rule), so its spectrum does not depend on which other
 % directions were solved.
s = SpotGreen.solveOne(structureBase, x, vS, eta, I, J) 
% G_IJ(+k) and G_IJ(-k) of one direction on the solve axis
 % >> s = SpotGreen.solveOne(structureBase, x, vS, eta, I, J)
vEnd = SpotGreen.velocityEnd(structureBase) 
% 1.05 x the largest bulk longitudinal velocity over in-plane directions [km/s]
 % >> vEnd = SpotGreen.velocityEnd(structureBase)
 %
 % The longitudinal limiting velocity of a direction never exceeds the bulk
 % longitudinal velocity along it (the limiting wave's slowness projection is the
 % largest in the sagittal plane), so this bounds every direction's longLimVel.
 % One Christoffel eigenproblem per half degree; a structure property, so a
 % single angle and any sweep share it.

CalcCommon value class

% CalcCommon — shared internals for the forwardCalcs Calc* family.
 %
 % Mechanical steps only — nothing here decides WHICH combination of solves to
 % run (each Calc* class's own job) and nothing here does wave physics.
 % Stateless static class: never instantiate it. Not fronted by Calc.*, with one
 % exception: saveCalc/loadCalc are user-facing (a finished result to/from a
 % .mat file) and are launched as Calc.saveResult/Calc.loadResult.
 %
 % Static helpers rather than a shared base class. The half-space/layered/plate
 % families genuinely differ in mode taxonomy, in which Christoffel reference is
 % meaningful, and in whether a system-wide limiting velocity exists at all;
 % static calls leave each class's physics decisions visible at its own call
 % site, where inheritance would hide them in an override chain. See
 % docs/reference/architecture/forwardCalcs.md, "Why half-space and layered are separate
 % class families".
 %
 % Error-ID convention: helpers that raise errors on a caller's behalf take an
 % `errPrefix` argument (e.g. 'CalcSingleHS:calcAll') and append their own final
 % component, so the thrown identifier stays the caller's own
 % (CalcSingleHS:calcAll:badMode) and greppable from the calling class.
 %
 % GuliweRR v6.0

Methods

obj = CalcCommon() 
% CalcCommon — shared internals for the forwardCalcs Calc* family.
 %
 % Mechanical steps only — nothing here decides WHICH combination of solves to
 % run (each Calc* class's own job) and nothing here does wave physics.
 % Stateless static class: never instantiate it. Not fronted by Calc.*, with one
 % exception: saveCalc/loadCalc are user-facing (a finished result to/from a
 % .mat file) and are launched as Calc.saveResult/Calc.loadResult.
 %
 % Static helpers rather than a shared base class. The half-space/layered/plate
 % families genuinely differ in mode taxonomy, in which Christoffel reference is
 % meaningful, and in whether a system-wide limiting velocity exists at all;
 % static calls leave each class's physics decisions visible at its own call
 % site, where inheritance would hide them in an override chain. See
 % docs/reference/architecture/forwardCalcs.md, "Why half-space and layered are separate
 % class families".
 %
 % Error-ID convention: helpers that raise errors on a caller's behalf take an
 % `errPrefix` argument (e.g. 'CalcSingleHS:calcAll') and append their own final
 % component, so the thrown identifier stays the caller's own
 % (CalcSingleHS:calcAll:badMode) and greppable from the calling class.
 %
 % GuliweRR v6.0
velAxis = CalcCommon.buildAdaptiveVelAxis(candidateVels, velRange, denseHalfWidth, nDensePerPeak, nSparse) 
% Non-uniform Green velocity axis: dense near candidate velocities, sparse elsewhere
 % >> velAxis = CalcCommon.buildAdaptiveVelAxis(candidateVels, velRange, denseHalfWidth, nDensePerPeak, nSparse)
 %
 % Standalone helper, not used by the Calc classes: their axis is uniform,
 % because the existence scores need uniform sampling.
 %
 % INPUT:  1. candidateVels  .. velocities worth resolving finely (e.g. Ritz mode
 %                             velocities and the Christoffel limiting velocities)
 %         2. velRange       .. [vMin vMax], the range to cover
 %  (opt)  3. denseHalfWidth .. km/s half-width of each dense window (default 0.03)
 %  (opt)  4. nDensePerPeak  .. points per dense window (default 150)
 %  (opt)  5. nSparse        .. background-fill points across the whole range
 %                             (default 150)
 % OUTPUT: velAxis .. sorted, de-duplicated column vector: a dense linspace
 %                    window around every candidate (clamped to velRange),
 %                    unioned with one sparse linspace fill
 %
 % The defaults are chosen, not derived, and were calibrated on Fe3Al only --
 % retune per material class if needed. A bound (non-leaky) Rayleigh peak's
 % HEIGHT stays sampling-sensitive even at this density (it sits on a
 % near-singular pole); only its existence and location are well-defined,
 % so this axis is for finding WHERE features are, not for converged peak
 % heights.
 %
 % Empty candidateVels degrades to a plain uniform axis (the sparse fill
 % only). GreenSolver and GreenPeaks.find both accept a non-uniform axis.

 % >> [ids, names] = CalcCommon.classificationMethods(isLayered)

 % OUTPUT: ids   .. {'green', 'refined', 'ritz'}; layered {'green', 'ritz'}
 %                  (there is no spot score for layered stacks)
 %         names .. the matching display names ('Green', 'Refined', 'Ritz')
 % The one list the validation, the viewers' classification bar and the
 % StructureControlGUI method dropdown read.

 %
 % The limiting association needs the eigenvectors, which retention may prune, and
 % does not depend on the threshold: it runs once per calculation and every later

[csFlat, outerIdx, angleIdx] = CalcCommon.flattenSweeps(sweeps, nAngles) 
% Flatten a {nOuter x 1} cell of angle-sweep objects into one linear list
 % >> [csFlat, outerIdx, angleIdx] = CalcCommon.flattenSweeps(sweeps, nAngles)
 %
 % INPUT:  1. sweeps  .. {nOuter x 1} of CalcAngSweepLayered or
 %                      CalcIsofreqAngSweepLayered (anything exposing .cs)
 %         2. nAngles .. angles per sweep (identical for every element)
 % OUTPUT: csFlat   .. {nOuter*nAngles x 1} of the per-point objects

 %

 % it lets ParallelPolicy see the PRODUCT of both axes rather than
 % nAngles alone, which matters when neither axis alone crosses
 % AUTO_THRESHOLD but their product does. Pair with scatterToSweeps.
outCell = CalcCommon.forEachElement(inCell, useP, stepFcn) 
% Map one phase step over a cell of per-point calc objects, parallel or serial
 % >> outCell = CalcCommon.forEachElement(inCell, useP, stepFcn)
 %
 % INPUT:  1. inCell  .. {n x 1} cell of per-point Calc* objects
 %         2. useP    .. from CalcCommon.resolveParallel
 %         3. stepFcn .. function handle, outCell{i} = stepFcn(inCell{i}, i)
 % OUTPUT: outCell .. {n x 1}, same order as inCell
 %
 % The element index is passed as stepFcn's 2nd argument so a phase
 % needing per-element data (e.g. the flat 2D path's per-wavelength
 % Green velocity axis) can index into it from inside the handle,
 % rather than needing a second sliced loop variable.
 %
 % outCell is seeded from inCell and then both read and written at the
 % loop index — a plain sliced variable, which is what `parfor` requires.
sha = CalcCommon.gitCommit() 
% HEAD commit hash of the repository holding guliwerr/, '' if not found
 % >> sha = CalcCommon.gitCommit()
 %
 % Read straight from .git/HEAD (and the ref file or packed-refs it points
 % to) rather than by calling git through system(): no dependency on a git
 % binary on the path, and no shell call. In a linked worktree .git is a
 % file "gitdir: <dir>": HEAD is read from <dir>, and the refs from <dir>
 % and from the shared repository named by <dir>/commondir.
absGMap = CalcCommon.greenComponentMapFrom(csCell, component, useParallel) 
% Any Green component over a sweep, recomputed per angle on request
 % >> absGMap = CalcCommon.greenComponentMapFrom(csCell, component, useParallel)
 %
 % INPUT:  1. csCell      .. {nAngles x 1} of CalcSingleHS/CalcSingleLayered
 %                           with calcGreen() run on ONE common axis
 %         2. component   .. 'GIJ' | 'GIJeff' (CalcCommon.parseGreenComponent)
 %         3. useParallel .. as the sweep's own useParallel
 % OUTPUT: absGMap .. [nVel x nAngles] single, nothing stored
 %
 % One Green solve per angle (each element's greenComponent()), so a whole
 % sweep costs about as much as its original Green phase.
absGMap = CalcCommon.greenComponentMapOf(sweep, component) 
% Any Green component over a sweep on its greenVelAxis (not stored)
 % >> absGMap = CalcCommon.greenComponentMapOf(sweep, component)

absG = CalcCommon.greenComponentOf(cs, component) 
% |G| of any Green component on a single-angle result's greenVelAxis (not stored)
 % >> absG = CalcCommon.greenComponentOf(cs, component)

 % The body of CalcSingleHS/CalcSingleLayered.greenComponent: 'G13eff' returns the
 % stored greenAbsG13Eff, any other component is one solve on the same axis and

absG = CalcCommon.greenComponentSpectrum(structure, velAxis, component, fname) 
% |G| of any one Green component, recomputed on request (never stored)
 % >> absG = CalcCommon.greenComponentSpectrum(structure, velAxis, component, fname)
 %
 % INPUT:  1. structure .. ALREADY-ROTATED Structure for this angle
 %         2. velAxis   .. phase-velocity axis [km/s], normally the result's
 %                        own greenVelAxis
 %         3. component .. 'GIJ' (one-sided G_IJ(+k), I = force direction,
 %                        J = displacement direction, 1 = propagation, 3 =
 %                        surface normal -- e.g. 'G13' = G13_+, 'G33') or
 %                        'GIJeff' (+-k symmetrised, e.g. 'G13eff')
 %         4. fname     .. caller name for error messages
 % OUTPUT: absG .. [nVel x 1] single, matches velAxis
 %
 % Same damping (SawSpectrum.ETA) and the same single solve as
 % greenScoreSpectrum, so any component compares like for like with the
 % stored |G13_eff|. Angle argument 0 (the double-rotation trap).
greenAbsG13EffMap = CalcCommon.greenG13EffMapFrom(csCell) 
% The sweep's |G13_eff| map, assembled from a cell of single-point objects
 % >> greenAbsG13EffMap = CalcCommon.greenG13EffMapFrom(csCell)
 %
 % INPUT:  csCell .. {nAngles x 1} of CalcSingleHS/CalcSingleLayered, each
 %                   with greenAbsG13Eff filled on ONE common axis (calcGreen())
 % OUTPUT: greenAbsG13EffMap .. [nVel x nAngles] single; [] if any element
 %                   lacks greenAbsG13Eff
 %
 % Assembly only — no Green solve here. FULL MODE ONLY: callers guard this
 % behind a calcMode check.
g = CalcCommon.greenScoreSpectrum(structure, velAxis, fname) 
% The one Green solve of a 'full' angle: G13_eff at SawSpectrum.ETA
 % >> g = CalcCommon.greenScoreSpectrum(structure, velAxis, fname)
 %
 % INPUT:  1. structure .. ALREADY-ROTATED Structure for this angle
 %         2. velAxis   .. phase-velocity axis [km/s]; in the Calc pipeline a
 %                        uniform axis from SawSpectrum.VEL_MIN
 %         3. fname     .. caller name for validateattributes messages,
 %                        e.g. 'CalcSingleHS.calcGreen'
 % OUTPUT: g .. struct with
 %              velAxis   [nVel x 1] double
 %              G13Eff    [nVel x 1] complex double: G13_eff = (G13_+ - G13_-)/2,
 %                        the existence-score input
 %              absG13Eff [nVel x 1] single: abs(G13Eff), what the viewers draw
 %
 % ONE solve serves display and classification, and G13_eff is all a Calc
 % result keeps: it is the TGS response (in-plane force, out-of-plane
 % displacement, +-k symmetrised) and costs no more than any one-sided
 % component -- reciprocity gives G13_- = G31_+ from the same +k solve
 % (docs/reference/architecture/green.md, "+-k symmetrisation"). Any other component
 % is recomputed on request (greenComponentSpectrum), never stored. The
 % damping is SawSpectrum.ETA (3e-3), not GreenSolver's default 3e-4, because the
 % score is calibrated at it; every displayed peak therefore has the
 % linewidth the classifier saw.
 %
 % The magnitude is single precision (display needs no more); the complex
 % G13Eff stays double for the score's late-time FFT.
 %
 % The angle argument is 0, NEVER the caller's obj.angle: the caller's
 % constructor has already rotated the structure and GreenSolver.calc
 % rotates internally too, so passing the angle rotates twice. See
 % docs/reference/architecture/forwardCalcs.md, "Rotation convention: pass 0 to
 % solvers after construction".
 %
 % isoWavevec side only. CalcIsofreqSingleLayered keeps its own
 % GreenSolver.calc call: scanMode='isoFreq' with a real omega0 is a
 % physically different measurement (fixed frequency, swept |k∥|).
g = CalcCommon.greenSpectrum(structure, velAxis, fname) 
% One isoWavevec |G33| solve at GreenSolver's default damping (not the Calc pipeline)
 % >> g = CalcCommon.greenSpectrum(structure, velAxis, fname)
 %
 % INPUT:  1. structure .. ALREADY-ROTATED Structure for this angle
 %         2. velAxis   .. phase-velocity axis [km/s]
 %         3. fname     .. caller name for validateattributes messages
 % OUTPUT: g .. struct with velAxis [nVel x 1], absGFull [3 x 3 x nVel],
 %              absG33 [nVel x 1] (double)
 %
 % GreenSolver's default eta (3e-4), any axis: the isoWavevec |G33| reference
 % the isoFreq tests and validations compare against. The Calc classes' 'full'
 % mode uses greenScoreSpectrum instead.
 %
 % Angle argument 0, never the caller's angle (double-rotation trap).
 % computeMinus=false: for G33 (a diagonal pair) G_- = G_+ exactly.
cs1 = CalcCommon.greenStep(cs0, greenVelAxis, retentionLevel, greenScoreRef, greenThreshold, precomp) 
% Phase 3 body for one angle: the Green solve -> existence score -> classify -> prune
 % >> cs1 = CalcCommon.greenStep(cs0, greenVelAxis, retentionLevel, greenScoreRef, greenThreshold, precomp)
 %
 % INPUT:  1. cs0            .. one CalcSingleHS/CalcSingleLayered, Phases 1-2 done
 %         2. greenVelAxis   .. the sweep-wide common axis (every angle's own axis
 %                              is a leading part of it)
 %         3. retentionLevel .. 'full' | 'saw_only' | 'none'
 %  (opt)  4. greenScoreRef    .. the reference shared by the whole sweep (half-space
 %                              only): GreenScore.reference (computed per object if
 %                              omitted). Ignored for a layered angle:
 %                              GreenScoreLayered needs no reference
 %  (opt)  5. greenThreshold .. the 'green' standard: 'high' | 'low' | numeric
 %                              (default: the method's default)
 %  (opt)  6. precomp        .. the shared Ritz precompute (the Ritz candidates'
 %                              angular slopes; default: cs0's own, empty on a
 %                              pool worker)
 % OUTPUT: cs1 .. same object with Green, classification and retention applied
 %
 % One Green solve per angle: calcGreen stores the display magnitudes on the
 % common axis and the complex score input; calcGreenScore adds the probe solves
 % and the scores (half-space GreenScore, or GreenScoreLayered for a layered
 % angle); calcRitzScore stores the Ritz candidates from the eigenvectors
 % still in memory, so 'ritz' can be selected later at no cost. The angle is
 % stamped calcMode 'full' and classified 'green' (the sweep switches the method
 % afterwards if asked).
 %
 % ORDERING IS LOAD-BEARING: prune runs AFTER identifyModes, never
 % before — classification needs the full eigenvector set to know which
 % columns are significant. Pruning per angle inside the loop, rather
 % than in a post-loop, keeps peak RAM at ONE angle's eigenvector matrix
 % instead of every angle's at once.
tf = CalcCommon.isResultObject(x) 
% True if x is a scalar object of one of CalcCommon.resultClasses()
 % >> tf = CalcCommon.isResultObject(x)
tf = CalcCommon.isStale(cs) 
% Whether a classified Calc result (single angle, or any angle of a sweep) carries
 % another classification stamp than the current one
 % >> tf = CalcCommon.isStale(result)
 %
 % INPUT:  cs .. CalcSingleHS/CalcSingleLayered, or a sweep (its cs{:} are checked)
 % OUTPUT: tf .. true if classified (classification not empty) with a
 %               classificationVersion other than ModeClassification.CLASSIFICATION_VERSION
lim = CalcCommon.limitingAndBulk(mat) 
% Christoffel limiting velocities + cheap direct bulk velocities for ONE material
 % >> lim = CalcCommon.limitingAndBulk(mat)
 %
 % INPUT:  mat .. Material, taken from an ALREADY-ROTATED Structure
 % OUTPUT: lim .. struct with fields longLimVel [1x1], longLimPol [3x1],
 %                longLimTheta [1x1], shearLimVels [nShear x 1],
 %                shearLimPols [3 x nShear], shearLimIwt [nShear x 1],
 %                shearLimTheta [nShear x 1], vS / vTf [1x1] (slowest /
 %                fastest shear limiting velocity, whatever the branch: the
 %                Rayleigh boundary and the half-space candidate cap),
 %                bulkVels [3x1] = [vL; vT1; vT2] descending,
 %                bulkPols [3x3] (columns = corresponding polarisations)
 %
 % A LimitingVels.findRoots sagittal-plane scan (longitudinal branch
 % iwt=1, shear branches iwt=2,3 concatenated) plus one Christoffel
 % eigendecomposition along [1;0;0] for the bulk velocities.
 %
 % shearLimVels CONCATENATES two physically distinct shear branches, each
 % with a variable number of roots, so the branch label is carried
 % separately in shearLimIwt (2 or 3 per entry). Never max()/min()
 % shearLimVels to recover fast- vs slow-shear: the slow branch can supply
 % the maximum, and either branch can have no root at a given angle.
 % shearLimTheta/longLimTheta are the sagittal root angles theta*, which
 % LimitingVels.refineRoot needs as its bracket centre.
 %
 % WHICH material to pass is the caller's physics decision. CalcSingleHS
 % passes mater(nLay), the surface layer — for a homogeneous half-space
 % that is the whole medium, so the result is a genuine system-wide
 % limiting velocity. CalcSingleLayered passes mater(1), the substrate,
 % and stores the result under sub*-prefixed names: a SOFT reference
 % velocity (the threshold above which a mode is flagged leaky), NOT a
 % system-wide bound, since a layered stack has none. Swapping the two
 % indices is a physics error in either direction.
 %
 % No further rotation here (GetRotElastTensor(0)): the caller's
 % Structure already carries the propagation-angle rotation.
[calcResult, meta] = CalcCommon.loadCalc(filePath) 
% Load a Calc* result saved by saveCalc (or by a plain save())
 % >> [calcResult, meta] = CalcCommon.loadCalc(filePath)
 %
 % INPUT:  1. filePath .. .mat file
 % OUTPUT: 1. calcResult .. the saved Calc* object
 %         2. meta       .. saveCalc's metadata struct (fields empty for a
 %                          file written by a plain save(); guliwerrVersion
 %                          empty for a file saved before it was recorded)
 %
 % A plain save() file is accepted if it holds exactly one Calc* result
 % variable. Warns (CalcCommon:loadCalc:commitMismatch) when the file was
 % saved at a different git commit than the current code -- harmless unless
 % the Calc* classes changed in between. Errors if MATLAB could not rebuild
 % the object and fell back to a struct, which is what load() does when
 % the class definition has changed incompatibly since saving.
method = CalcCommon.modeSawClassMethod(mode) 
% The classification method of a calcMode when sawClassMethod is unset
 % >> method = CalcCommon.modeSawClassMethod(mode)
 %
 % OUTPUT: method .. 'ritz' for 'ritzOnly', 'green' otherwise ('full')
s = CalcCommon.orList(items) 
% Format a cellstr as a quoted "'a', 'b', or 'c'" fragment for error messages
 % >> s = CalcCommon.orList(items)
 %
 % Lets the validators above build the message from the allowed-value
 % list itself, so the two cannot drift apart.
[I, J, isEff] = CalcCommon.parseGreenComponent(component) 
% 'GIJ' / 'GIJeff' -> force index I, displacement index J, symmetrised flag
 % >> [I, J, isEff] = CalcCommon.parseGreenComponent('G13eff')   % 1, 3, true
root = CalcCommon.projectRoot() 
% The folder holding guliwerr/ (this file is guliwerr/forwardCalcs/CalcCommon.m)
 % >> root = CalcCommon.projectRoot()
ritzSolver = CalcCommon.pruneRitzTo(ritzSolver, classification, retentionLevel, errPrefix) 
% Apply the SAW-family eigenvector retention policy to a RitzSolverSAW result
 % >> ritzSolver = CalcCommon.pruneRitzTo(ritzSolver, classification, retentionLevel, errPrefix)
 %
 % INPUT:  1. ritzSolver      .. RitzSolverSAW result to prune
 %         2. classification  .. that point's ModeClassification result
 %         3. retentionLevel  .. 'full' | 'saw_only' | 'none'
 %         4. errPrefix       .. caller's error-ID prefix, e.g.
 %                              'CalcSingleHS:applyRetention'
 % OUTPUT: ritzSolver .. pruned (velocities/scores always survive)
 %
 % 'full':     keep eigVecs_con untouched.
 % 'saw_only': reduce to the columns identifyModes() classified as
 %             significant — multi-column in general (a pseudoRayleigh
 %             or SAW cluster plus limiting-mode entries), not a single
 %             sawModeNo.
 % 'none':     clear eigVecs_con entirely.
 %
 % SAW family only (CalcSingleHS/CalcSingleLayered). CalcSinglePlate keeps
 % its own two-level version: it has no 'saw_only' tier, and routing it
 % through here would make a plate silently ACCEPT 'saw_only'.
CalcCommon.refuseStale(cs, errPrefix) 
% Error <errPrefix>:stale if the result's classification is stale (see isStale)
 % >> CalcCommon.refuseStale(result, errPrefix)
useP = CalcCommon.resolveParallel(useParallelSetting, nItems) 
% Resolve a useParallel setting into an actual parallel/serial decision
 % >> useP = CalcCommon.resolveParallel(useParallelSetting, nItems)
 %
 % INPUT:  1. useParallelSetting .. "auto" | "always" | "never"
 %         2. nItems             .. number of independent iterations
 % OUTPUT: useP .. true only if the policy says yes AND a thread pool
 %                 is actually available
 %
 % The two ParallelPolicy calls must travel together: asking the policy
 % without confirming a pool exists runs `parfor` with no pool — silently
 % serial, plus a startup attempt per loop. Callers resolve this ONCE per
 % calc() and reuse the answer for every phase loop.
method = CalcCommon.resolveSawClassMethod(method, threshold, mode, isLayered, errPrefix) 
% The classification a calculation runs: an unset sawClassMethod ('') follows calcMode
 % >> method = CalcCommon.resolveSawClassMethod(method, threshold, mode, isLayered, errPrefix)
 %
 % INPUT:  1. method    .. sawClassMethod: '' (unset) | 'green' | 'refined' | 'ritz'
 %         2. threshold .. sawThreshold: [] | 'high' | 'low' | numeric
 %         3. mode      .. calcMode: 'full' | 'ritzOnly'
 %         4. isLayered .. layered stack (no 'refined')
 %         5. errPrefix .. e.g. 'CalcSingleHS:calcAll'
 % OUTPUT: method .. as char: unset -> modeSawClassMethod(mode) ('green' on 'full',
 %                  'ritz' on 'ritzOnly')
 %
 % Validates both inputs. Throws <errPrefix>:numericThresholdNeedsMethod (a number
 % is read on one method's own scale, so it needs the method set explicitly),
 % <errPrefix>:ritzOnlyNeedsRitz (any method but 'ritz' on 'ritzOnly'), besides
 % validateSawThreshold's / validateSawClassMethod's own errors.
names = CalcCommon.resultClasses() 
% Every Calc* result class saveCalc/loadCalc (and Gui.viewResult) accept
 % >> names = CalcCommon.resultClasses()
 %
 % OUTPUT: names .. {1 x n} cellstr of class names
cs1 = CalcCommon.ritzOnlyStep(cs0, retentionLevel, ritzThreshold, precomp) 
% 'ritzOnly' body for one angle: Ritz -> (Christoffel + modal sum) -> classify -> prune
 % >> cs1 = CalcCommon.ritzOnlyStep(cs0, retentionLevel, ritzThreshold, precomp)
 %
 % INPUT:  1. cs0                .. one CalcSingleHS/CalcSingleLayered
 %         2. retentionLevel     .. 'full' | 'saw_only' | 'none'
 %         3. ritzThreshold      .. 'high' | 'low' | numeric ([] = the default)
 %  (opt)  4. precomp            .. the sweep's shared Ritz precompute, handed to
 %                                  calcRitz (default: cs0's own -- empty on a
 %                                  pool worker, see warnRitzPrecomputeBuiltOnWorker)
 % OUTPUT: cs1 .. same object, calcAll('ritzOnly') done
 %
 % The Ritz-only counterpart of greenStep. No classification input is shared
 % between angles: each angle scores against its own floor, so a sweep and a
 % single angle classify identically. calcAll keeps the load-bearing order
 % (modal-sum weights and classification BEFORE pruning).
[precompStruct, built] = CalcCommon.ritzSAWPrecompute(structure, precompStruct, rawByDepthCell) 
% Build any missing field of the angle-independent RitzSolverSAW precompute
 % >> [precompStruct, built] = CalcCommon.ritzSAWPrecompute(structure, precompStruct, rawByDepthCell)
 %
 % INPUT:  1. structure      .. Structure to build from
 %  (opt)  2. precompStruct  .. partially-filled precompute (default: empty
 %                             struct, i.e. build everything)
 %  (opt)  3. rawByDepthCell .. pre-built GammaMatrix.rawByDepth cell to hand
 %                             to derivsByDepth (default: {}, let
 %                             derivsByDepth build its own)
 % OUTPUT: 1. precompStruct .. with nullConstrMat, dKdC_cell and d2KdCdD_cell
 %                             populated
 %         2. built         .. true if any field had to be built (false: the
 %                             input was complete and is returned unchanged,
 %                             at no cost)
 %
 % Every field depends only on calcParams (legOrder/relDepth) and density
 % — never on dirMat or propagation angle. That is what makes it correct,
 % not merely convenient, for a sweep class to build this ONCE from the
 % unrotated structure and share it across every angle.
 %
 % Fill-if-missing throughout, so one call serves both cases: a sweep
 % passes nothing and gets everything built; a single-point class passes
 % whatever its sweep handed it and only the gaps are filled.
 %
 % Lamb-side precompute is deliberately NOT handled here: it needs
 % RitzSolver.buildConstraintMatrix(..., 'lamb') (a different boundary
 % condition) and must NOT carry dKdC_cell at all.
 %
 % >> precompStruct = CalcCommon.ritzSAWPrecompute(structure, sweep)
 % With a CalcCommon.ritzWavelengthSweep struct as 2nd argument: the full precompute
 % of a structure that differs from the sweep's only in its layer depths
 % (wavelength), elastic constants or densities; see ritzWavelengthSweep.
sweep = CalcCommon.ritzWavelengthSweep(structure) 
% The wavelength-independent part of the RitzSolverSAW precompute of a structure
 % >> sweep = CalcCommon.ritzWavelengthSweep(structure)
 %
 % INPUT:  structure .. Structure to build from
 % OUTPUT: sweep .. struct: legOrders [1 x nLay] (the Legendre order of every layer,
 %                  which is all the other fields depend on), rawByDepthCell
 %                  (GammaMatrix.RawCellFor) and nullConstrMat
 %
 % Mirrors the Julia ritzWavelengthSweep / RitzWavelengthSweep. ApplyWavelength
 % changes only the films' relDepth, while the raw Legendre tensor and the constraint
 % null space N depend on the Legendre orders alone, so a sweep over wavelengths
 % (|k|) builds them once; at each wavelength
 % CalcCommon.ritzSAWPrecompute(structureAtWavelength, sweep) then only contracts
 % the depth factors. Elastic constants and densities may change between those
 % structures too (neither the raw tensor nor N carries them); the Legendre orders
 % may not. Julia's sweep also caches the real-gauge back-map and the Gram blocks of
 % M_con; MATLAB keeps neither (measured < 1 % of a solve here).
structRot = CalcCommon.rotateStructure(structure, angleDeg) 
% Rotate every layer's Material in-plane to a propagation angle
 % >> structRot = CalcCommon.rotateStructure(structure, angleDeg)
 %
 % INPUT:  1. structure .. Structure object, UNROTATED
 %         2. angleDeg  .. propagation angle [deg]
 % OUTPUT: structRot .. same Structure with every layer's dirMat rotated
 %
 % CalcParams is preserved per layer (ModifyLayer with calcParams0 omitted).
 %
 % Callers rotate ONCE, in their own constructor, and must then pass 0
 % (never obj.angle) to any solver called afterwards — the solvers
 % rotate internally too. See docs/reference/architecture/forwardCalcs.md, "Rotation
 % convention: pass 0 to solvers after construction".
filePath = CalcCommon.saveCalc(calcResult, filePath, note) 
% Save a finished Calc* result, with provenance metadata, to a .mat file
 % >> filePath = CalcCommon.saveCalc(calcResult, filePath, note)
 %
 % INPUT:  1. calcResult .. any CalcCommon.resultClasses() object, normally
 %                          already calc()'d / calcAll()'d
 %  (opt)  2. filePath   .. target .mat file (default = '': an auto-named
 %                          file in <project>/results/, see below);
 %                          '.mat' is appended if missing
 %  (opt)  3. note       .. free-text note stored in meta.note (default = '')
 % OUTPUT: filePath .. absolute path of the file written
 %
 % The file holds two variables: calcResult and meta (className, savedAt,
 % guliwerrVersion, gitCommit, matlabVersion, note). Load it back with
 % CalcCommon.loadCalc or straight into a viewer with Gui.viewSaved.
 %
 % Auto name: <project>/results/<class>_<structure name>_<yyyymmdd-HHMMSS>.mat,
 % <project> being the folder that holds guliwerr/ -- fixed, so it does not
 % depend on the current folder of whichever session saved it.
 %
 % Always -v7.3 (HDF5-based): no 2 GB per-variable limit, and measured no
 % larger or slower than -v7 for these objects. The Ritz precompute is
 % Transient on every Calc* class and therefore not saved (see
 % CalcSingleHS.precompStructForRitz).
 %
 % guliwerrVersion is guliwerrVersion() at save time -- the provenance that
 % survives a download without .git. gitCommit is HEAD of the repository
 % holding guliwerr/ at save time; uncommitted edits are not captured.

CalcCommon.warnRitzPrecomputeBuiltOnWorker(className) 
% Warn when a Calc* object had to build its Ritz precompute on a pool worker
 % >> CalcCommon.warnRitzPrecomputeBuiltOnWorker(className)
 %
 % INPUT: className .. class of the object, e.g. 'CalcSingleHS'
 %
 % Called by ritzPrecompute() after it had to build something; silent on the
 % client. The precompute is Transient, and a thread-pool parfor serialises each
 % object it hands to a worker, so a worker's copy arrives without it. Rebuilding
 % it there costs more than the Ritz solve itself, once per angle -- the sweeps
 % therefore pass their shared copy into calcRitz()/calcAll() explicitly. This
 % warning means some parallel step does not, i.e. a lost speed-up, not a wrong
 % result. Not switched off after the first time: it should stay loud. (A
 % worker's warning is printed on the client but does not set lastwarn; the
 % tests capture it with evalc.)

Inverse characterisation

Inv value class

% Namespace for GuliweRR's inverse-characterisation constructors.
 % All methods are static -- do not instantiate this class.
 %
 % EXPERIMENTAL: validated on synthetic data only; the interface may change.
 %
 % Usage:  measured    = Inv.measured(anglesDeg, velocities, waveTypes, velocityStd)
 %         association = Inv.association(sweep, measured, velocityTolerance, ...
 %                           angleTolerance, invertedLayerNo)
 %         sensitivity = Inv.sensitivity(structure, association, invertedLayerNo, measured, ...)
 %         inversion   = Inv.elasticity(structure, measured, association, sensitivity, ...
 %                           invertedLayerNo, ...)
 %
 % Thin varargin passthrough only -- no defaults live here, and none should be added:
 % a default duplicated here can silently drift from the real constructor's own default.
 % Keep this file in sync with MeasuredVelocities/ModeAssociation.fromSweep/
 % SensitivityAnalysis.calc/ElasticityInversion whenever any of their signatures change.
 %
 % Argument order: the two calls do NOT share it. Inv.sensitivity takes
 % (structure, association, invertedLayerNo, measured), Inv.elasticity takes
 % (structure, measured, association, sensitivity, invertedLayerNo) -- measured and
 % association swap places, and invertedLayerNo moves from third to last.
 %
 % JOINT MULTI-DATASET INVERSION: Inv.sensitivity/Inv.elasticity accept `structure`/
 % `association`/`measured` as either scalars (single dataset) or object arrays (several
 % crystallographic cuts of the SAME crystal, sharing one target elasticity); see
 % SensitivityAnalysis's and ElasticityInversion's own docblocks.
 %
 % GuliweRR v6.0

Methods

MeasuredVelocities value class

% MeasuredVelocities — the measured dataset from one TGS angular sweep, and nothing else.
 %   Immutable, calculation-free: what was measured, with what uncertainty, in what units.
 %   No mode number, no eigenvector, no calculated velocity -- that binding lives in
 %   ModeAssociation. Optionally carries independently measured pulse-echo bulk phase
 %   velocities and a single soft-fixed bulk modulus.
 %
 % EXPERIMENTAL: validated on synthetic data only; the interface may change.
 %
 % Value class: addPhaseVelocity/setBulkModulus return the modified object.
 %
 % JOINT MULTI-DATASET INVERSION: one object per dataset. For a joint inversion of several
 %   crystallographic cuts of the SAME crystal, pass an ARRAY of MeasuredVelocities (one per
 %   dataset) to SensitivityAnalysis.calc/ElasticityInversion -- see those classes' docblocks.
 %
 % GuliweRR v6.0

Properties

obj.angleDeg (default: zeros(0,1)) 
[nPoints x 1] propagation angle [deg]
obj.bulkModulus (default: NaN) 
soft-fixed bulk modulus [GPa]; NaN = not set
obj.nPoints (default: 0) 
[nPoints x 1] propagation angle [deg]
obj.phaseVelocities (default: struct) 
pulse-echo bulk phase velocities (optional channel)
obj.velocity (default: zeros(0,1)) 
[nPoints x 1] measured phase velocity [km/s]
obj.velocityStd (default: zeros(0,1)) 
[nPoints x 1] measurement std [km/s]; NaN = unknown. Metadata only: no calculation reads it (weights are the PointWeights option of SensitivityAnalysis.calc/ElasticityInversion)
obj.waveType (default: cell) 
[nPoints x 1] cellstr, from WAVE_TYPES

 %         2. velocity    .. [N x 1] or [1 x N] measured phase velocities [km/s]

 %                          every point; member of MeasuredVelocities.WAVE_TYPES.
 %                          Default = 'unknown' for every point.
 %  (opt)  4. velocityStd .. [N x 1], or a scalar applied to every point.
 %                          Default = NaN (unknown) for every point. Stored as
 %                          metadata only; it does not weight the fit.

ModeAssociation value class

% ModeAssociation — the binding table: which measured velocity point corresponds to
 %   which calculated object. For "ritzMode"/"pseudoSAW" points that object is one
 %   eigenvector of the Ritz eigenproblem; for "limiting" points it is one ROOT of the
 %   Christoffel sagittal-plane limiting-velocity problem. One entry per
 %   (measured point -> calculated counterpart) pair.
 %
 % EXPERIMENTAL: validated on synthetic data only; the interface may change.
 %
 %   Built initially from a forward sweep (fromSweep) at the initial elasticity guess;
 %   then re-established at every optimisation iteration (rebind for the Ritz classes,
 %   rebindLimiting for the limiting class) as elasticity -- and therefore mode
 %   character -- changes. Three words are used precisely throughout, because
 %   conflating them is what makes this subject hard to discuss:
 %
 %     BINDING          the one-time act, at construction, of deciding that measured
 %                      point k corresponds to a particular calculated object.
 %     ANCHOR           the stored, elasticity-INDEPENDENT description of identity that
 %                      lets that correspondence be re-established later. For a Ritz
 %                      mode it is the eigenvector (anchorEigVec_con); for a limiting
 %                      root it is the branch index plus the polarisation 3-vector
 %                      (limBranchIwt + limAnchorPol). An anchor is compared against,
 %                      never searched for.
 %     RE-IDENTIFICATION (the code calls it REBINDING) the act, repeated at every
 %                      objective evaluation, of finding which of this iterate's
 %                      calculated objects the anchor now refers to. A mode INDEX is
 %                      worthless across iterations because the spectrum reorders;
 %                      only the anchor survives.
 %
 %   CONFIDENCE is the score the winning candidate achieved at re-identification:
 %   macValue for a Ritz-tracked point, limMatchScore for a limiting one. A collapsing
 %   confidence is the observable signature of the tracking failure this whole
 %   machinery exists to prevent -- see confidence() for the one accessor that knows
 %   which signal belongs to which class.
 %
 %   Also owns the hand-build and pinning API: addPoint and setLimitingAnchor build an
 %   association point by point (tests, scripts), setPinned/pinnedPoints mark a binding
 %   as human-confirmed (see setPinned).
 %
 % Value class: rebind/rebindLimiting/addPoint/setPinned return the modified object. That is what lets ElasticityInversion re-bind into a SCRATCH
 %   copy every objective evaluation and promote it only when the cost improved, so a
 %   rejected trial step can never move an anchor.
 %
 % JOINT MULTI-DATASET INVERSION: one object per dataset (mode tracking is strictly a
 %   per-dataset concern). For a joint inversion of several crystallographic cuts of the
 %   SAME crystal, pass an ARRAY of ModeAssociation (one per dataset, each built against
 %   its OWN forward sweep at the SAME shared initial-guess elasticity) to
 %   SensitivityAnalysis.calc/ElasticityInversion -- see those classes' docblocks.
 %
 % GuliweRR v6.0

Properties

ModeAssociation.DEFAULT_REBIND_WINDOW (default: 1) 
[km/s]
ModeAssociation.LIM_BRANCH_PENALTY (default: 0.5) 
multiplier when the candidate root's branch index differs from the anchor's (a soft preference)
ModeAssociation.LIM_THETA_WINDOW (default: 0.15) 
Gaussian width for sagittal-root-angle proximity [rad]
ModeAssociation.LIM_VELOCITY_WINDOW (default: 0.15) 
Gaussian width for velocity proximity, RELATIVE to the anchored root's own velocity

Methods

obj = ModeAssociation() 
% Initialise an empty ModeAssociation
 % >> obj = ModeAssociation()
obj = obj.addPoint(angleDeg, ritzModeNo, measuredVelocity, calculatedVelocity, anchorEigVec_con, bindingClass, waveType, pinned) 
% Add one measured-point <-> calculated-object binding
 % >> obj = addPoint(obj, angleDeg, ritzModeNo, measuredVelocity, ...
 %                   calculatedVelocity, anchorEigVec_con, bindingClass, ...
 %                   waveType, pinned)
 % INPUT: (opt) bindingClass .. default "ritzMode"
 %        (opt) waveType     .. default "unknown"
 %        (opt) pinned       .. human-confirmed anchor (default false); true
 %                              when a human hand-picks the mode, e.g. off an
 %                              angular map.
c = obj.confidence() 
% The class-appropriate confidence signal for every point, as ONE vector
 % >> c = confidence(obj)
 % OUTPUT: c .. [nPoints x 1]; macValue for "ritzMode"/"pseudoSAW",
 %              limMatchScore for "limiting", NaN where never re-identified
 %
 % Exists so that reporting and plotting code does not have to know the
 % class taxonomy: reading macValue directly shows nothing at all for
 % limiting points, which are not MAC-tracked and carry macValue = NaN
 % forever.
opts = ModeAssociation.defaultTrackOpts() 
% Default re-identification thresholds shared by rebind/rebindLimiting
 % >> opts = ModeAssociation.defaultTrackOpts()
 %
 % OUTPUT: opts .. struct with fields
 %   macWarnThreshold    .. below this MAC a Ritz-class match is treated as
 %                          low-confidence: the row is zeroed and the anchor is
 %                          not updated (0.7)
 %   limMatchScoreThresh .. below this combined score a limiting-root match is
 %                          rejected outright as limRootLost (0.5): a vanished
 %                          root collapses the score far below it, a tracked one
 %                          stays near 1
 %   useGreenSteering    .. let a live Green peak steer pseudoSAW matches (false
 %                          here; ElasticityInversion sets it from
 %                          pseudoSAWRefinement)
 %   greenWindowRel      .. width of the tie-breaking Gaussian on relative
 %                          velocity distance from the live Green peak (0.02 --
 %                          ModeClassification.matchRitzToGreenPeak's own
 %                          velMatchTol, and the same exp(-(relDist/w)^2) idiom)
 %   greenFilterRel      .. HOW FAR from the live Green peak a candidate Ritz
 %                          mode may sit and still be eligible at all (0.04). A
 %                          HARD filter, set at the GROSS-ERROR scale: between a
 %                          correctly matched pSAW mode (well under 1% from the
 %                          peak) and a mis-track onto another cluster member.
 %                          Do NOT tighten it towards greenWindowRel: see rebind.
obj = ModeAssociation.fromSweep(sweep, measured, velocityTolerance, angleTolerance, invertedLayerNo) 
% Build the initial mode association from a forward sweep at the initial guess
 % >> obj = ModeAssociation.fromSweep(sweep, measured, velocityTolerance, angleTolerance, invertedLayerNo)
 %
 % INPUT: 1. sweep             .. CalcAngSweepHS/CalcAngSweepLayered result,
 %                                retentionLevel='full', calcMode='full'
 %        2. measured          .. MeasuredVelocities
 %  (opt) 3. velocityTolerance .. max |v_calc - v_meas| to accept a candidate
 %                                [km/s] (default 0.3)
 %  (opt) 4. angleTolerance    .. max |angle_meas - angle_sweep| to consider the
 %                                same angle [deg] (default 1e-6); errors if a
 %                                measured angle has no sweep angle within it
 %  (opt) 5. invertedLayerNo   .. which layer's material the LIMITING channel
 %                                refers to (default 1). For a half-space there
 %                                is only one layer, so the default is always
 %                                right there; it exists so the limiting binding
 %                                below uses the SAME material
 %                                SensitivityAnalysis/ElasticityInversion
 %                                evaluate the limiting channel with.
 %
 % Wave-type routing: see ModeAssociation.classifyWaveType.
 %
 % The two classes bind DIFFERENTLY:
 %   Ritz classes -- filter sweep.significantModesMap by the accepted
 %     ModeClassification types, take the entry minimising |vel - measured|,
 %     reject beyond velocityTolerance. Also stores the classifier's Green
 %     peak velocity as the seed of the Green steering (anchorGreenPeakVel).
 %   Limiting -- compute the Christoffel root set at that angle directly, filter
 %     by iwt == waveTypeIndex(waveType), take the nearest by velocity, reject
 %     beyond velocityTolerance. Routing these through significantModesMap
 %     instead would (a) let 'longitudinal'/'fastShear'/'slowShear' all compete
 %     against EVERY 'limiting' entry at that angle with no branch filter, and
 %     (b) apply velocityTolerance to a nearby RITZ MODE'S velocity rather than
 %     to the Christoffel root that is actually fitted downstream.
score = ModeAssociation.limitingRootScore(pt, roots) 
% Re-identification score of every candidate root against ONE point's anchor
 % >> score = ModeAssociation.limitingRootScore(pt, roots)
 %
 % INPUT: 1. pt    .. one points entry of bindingClass "limiting"
 %        2. roots .. struct array from SensitivityAnalysis.limitingRootsAt
 % OUTPUT: score .. [nRoots x 1] in [0,1]
 %
 %   score = |pol_a' pol_k| / (|pol_a| |pol_k|)          polarisation overlap
 %         * exp(-((v_k - v_a) / (LIM_VELOCITY_WINDOW*v_a))^2)
 %         * exp(-((theta_k - theta_a) / LIM_THETA_WINDOW)^2)
 %         * (iwt_k == iwt_a ? 1 : LIM_BRANCH_PENALTY)
 %
 % The polarisation overlap is the dominant term and the two Gaussians are
 % tie-breakers -- the same multiplicative projection x Gaussian x Gaussian
 % idiom as ModeClassification.matchRitzToGreenPeak/associateLimitingMode: a
 % linear penalty is too weak, and a bare projection with only an "> eps"
 % floor picks whatever is nearest when nothing is genuinely near. The branch
 % penalty is a SOFT preference, not a hard filter: near an acoustic axis the
 % T1/T2 labels are interchangeable (the descending-eigenvalue ordering that
 % defines them is arbitrary where the branches touch), and a hard filter
 % would reject the correct root exactly there.
 % abs() on the overlap because an eigenvector's overall sign is
 % arbitrary -- which means a polarisation and its exact reverse score 1.0.
 % For a limiting root that is physically correct (the same standing feature),
 % but it would be WRONG if this score were ever reused for something
 % directional such as group-velocity orientation. Do not reuse it carelessly.
n = obj.nPoints() 
% Number of bound points
 % >> n = nPoints(obj)
idx = obj.pinnedPoints() 
% Indices of human-confirmed (pinned) points, for reporting
 % >> idx = pinnedPoints(obj)
idx = obj.pointsAtAngle(angleDeg, bindingClasses) 
% Indices of points at a given angle, optionally filtered by bindingClass
 % >> idx = pointsAtAngle(obj, angleDeg, bindingClasses)
 % INPUT: (opt) bindingClasses .. cellstr subset of {"ritzMode","limiting",
 %                                "pseudoSAW"}; [] = no filter
obj = obj.rebind(angleDeg, eigVecs_con, M_con, vels, rebindVelocityWindow, updateAnchor, trackOpts) 
% Re-establish the RITZ-class mode binding at one angle from a freshly
 % solved eigenproblem
 % >> obj = rebind(obj, angleDeg, eigVecs_con, M_con, vels, rebindVelocityWindow, updateAnchor, trackOpts)
 %
 % INPUT: 1. angleDeg     .. propagation angle [deg]
 %        2. eigVecs_con  .. [nCon x nModes] M-orthonormal eigenvectors, current iterate
 %        3. M_con        .. [nCon x nCon] mass matrix, constrained basis
 %        4. vels         .. [nModes x 1] phase velocities of eigVecs_con's columns
 %  (opt)  5. rebindVelocityWindow .. only candidates within this window [km/s] of
 %                          each point's window CENTRE are considered (default 1.0)
 %  (opt)  6. updateAnchor .. if true, replace anchorEigVec_con with the newly
 %                          matched eigenvector ("chained" anchor policy); if
 %                          false, anchorEigVec_con is left untouched ("fixed")
 %                          (default false)
 %  (opt)  7. trackOpts    .. struct, see ModeAssociation.defaultTrackOpts
 %
 % M-weighted MAC: MAC_m = |alpha_m^H M_con alpha_anchor|. The inner product is
 % M-weighted, not Euclidean: because the eigenvectors are M-orthonormal,
 % alpha_m^H M_con alpha_anchor is exactly the expansion coefficient of the
 % anchor in the current eigenbasis and sum_m |coef|^2 = 1, so the MAC value is
 % a genuine fraction-of-identity. (DispersionBranches.track uses the same
 % mass weighting, with each wavelength step's own M.)
 %
 % GREEN STEERING (pseudoSAW points only, trackOpts.useGreenSteering). A leaky
 % pseudo-surface wave radiates real shear energy into the half-space, and
 % Ritz's finite, non-radiating domain represents that resonance as a CLUSTER
 % of near-degenerate modes whose eigenvector COMPOSITION is very sensitive to
 % small elastic-constant changes. The eigenVALUE is precise; the mode SHAPE,
 % and hence the MAC, is what is fragile. When a live Green peak is available
 % the velocity window is re-centred on IT rather than on the previous accepted
 % calculatedVelocity (which drags the window along with a mis-track), the MAC
 % is restricted to candidates within trackOpts.greenFilterRel of that peak,
 % with a Gaussian of width trackOpts.greenWindowRel breaking ties inside it.
 % Green never sets calculatedVelocity: it only decides WHICH mode is matched,
 % so the residual stays on the Ritz eigenvalue and the analytic Jacobian stays
 % exactly valid. The MAC alone cannot detect this failure: a chained anchor
 % follows a mis-track onto a neighbouring cluster member and then agrees with
 % it perfectly (macValue = 1).
 %
 % PINNED points take their own argmax BEFORE matchpairs runs on the rest, so
 % an unpinned point with a marginally higher score cannot displace a human's
 % confirmed binding onto its second choice.
obj = obj.rebindLimiting(angleDeg, roots, updateAnchor, trackOpts) 
% Re-establish the LIMITING-class root binding at one angle from a freshly
 % computed Christoffel root set
 % >> obj = rebindLimiting(obj, angleDeg, roots, updateAnchor, trackOpts)
 %
 % INPUT: 1. angleDeg  .. propagation angle [deg]
 %        2. roots     .. struct array from SensitivityAnalysis.limitingRootsAt
 %                       (fields iwt, vel, pol, theta), current iterate
 %  (opt) 3. updateAnchor .. replace limAnchorPol with the newly matched root's
 %                       polarisation ("chained"); limBranchIwt and limRootTheta
 %                       are updated regardless (default false)
 %  (opt) 4. trackOpts .. struct, see ModeAssociation.defaultTrackOpts
 %
 % Deliberately a sibling of rebind: same shape, same matchpairs step, same
 % pinned-priority pass, same promote-discipline contract. What differs is only
 % the score -- there is no eigenvector here, so identity is carried by the
 % polarisation 3-vector plus the branch index and the sagittal root angle
 % (limitingRootScore).
 %
 % Limiting roots can VANISH, not merely cross: over a small change of the
 % elastic constants a shear root can cease to exist, and an untracked
 % max()/min() over the shear roots then makes the calculated velocity jump
 % to another root with no warning, with a finite-difference Jacobian taken
 % straight across the discontinuity. A remedy designed only against crossings
 % would not catch it; the polarisation overlap does, because it collapses at
 % exactly that step.
obj = obj.removeDuplicatePoints() 
% Keep at most one binding per (angle, ritzModeNo) for "ritzMode"/"pseudoSAW"
 % points, and at most one per (angle, waveType) for "limiting" points -- the
 % closest-matching duplicate wins, the rest are dropped with a warning.
 % >> obj = removeDuplicatePoints(obj)
obj = obj.setGreenPeak(idx, peakVel) 
% Store a freshly measured live Green peak velocity on one point
 % >> obj = setGreenPeak(obj, idx, peakVel)
 %
 % INPUT: 1. idx     .. point index
 %        2. peakVel .. Green peak velocity at the CURRENT iterate [km/s],
 %                     or NaN to disable Green steering for this point
 %
 % Written only by ElasticityInversion's Green rescan. It NEVER sets
 % calculatedVelocity: a Green peak is a velocity, not an eigenvector, so it
 % cannot be an anchor and is not the quantity being fitted. It only steers
 % which Ritz mode the MAC match selects, which keeps the residual on the Ritz
 % eigenvalue and therefore keeps the analytic Rayleigh-quotient Jacobian
 % exactly valid.
obj = obj.setLimitingAnchor(idx, iwt, pol, thetaRoot) 
% Set one limiting point's ROOT ANCHOR by hand
 % >> obj = setLimitingAnchor(obj, idx, iwt, pol, thetaRoot)
 %
 % INPUT: 1. idx       .. point index (must be bindingClass "limiting")
 %        2. iwt       .. branch index (1 longitudinal, 2 fast shear, 3 slow shear)
 %        3. pol       .. [3x1] polarisation at the root
 %        4. thetaRoot .. sagittal root angle theta* [rad]
 %
 % The limiting-class counterpart of addPoint's anchorEigVec_con argument,
 % kept as a separate setter rather than four more positional arguments on
 % addPoint. fromSweep fills these itself; this is the hand-build path.
obj = obj.setLimitingLost(idx) 
% Flag ONE limiting point's tracked root as lost at this evaluation
 % >> obj = setLimitingLost(obj, idx)
 %
 % Written by ElasticityInversion when a perturbed evaluation of the point's
 % finite-difference Jacobian row lost the root
 % (SensitivityAnalysis.channelDerivative's ok = false): the row cannot be
 % formed, so the point is excluded from this evaluation exactly as
 % rebindLimiting excludes a root that has vanished.
obj = obj.setLimitingRefined(idx, velocity, thetaRoot) 
% Advance ONE limiting point from a successful anchored local refinement,
 % without a full re-identification
 % >> obj = setLimitingRefined(obj, idx, velocity, thetaRoot)
 %
 % The limitingRescanCadence > 1 shortcut ONLY (see
 % ElasticityInversion.trackLimitingRoots). It is legitimate to skip
 % rebindLimiting here precisely because LimitingVels.refineRoot already
 % applied findRoots' own accept test inside the anchored bracket and
 % returned ok = true: the tracked root demonstrably still exists, at a
 % theta* within a few degrees of where it was. limMatchScore is left at its
 % last re-identified value rather than being invented, since no scoring
 % happened on this evaluation.
obj = obj.setPinned(idx, tf) 
% Mark one or more points as human-confirmed (or un-confirm them)
 % >> obj = setPinned(obj, idx, tf)
 %
 % INPUT: 1. idx .. point index/indices
 %  (opt) 2. tf  .. logical (default true)
 %
 % pinned = true means: THIS POINT'S ANCHOR IS HUMAN-CONFIRMED. Never
 % replace it, and give the point priority when candidates are contested.
 % Re-identify it every iteration exactly like any other point -- that is
 % what keeps it on the mode the human chose. It does NOT mean "freeze
 % ritzModeNo": the spectrum reorders at every iterate, so mode #37 at
 % iteration 1 and mode #37 at iteration 40 are different physical modes,
 % and freezing the index would guarantee the drift this whole scheme
 % exists to prevent. Live state (ritzModeNo, calculatedVelocity,
 % macValue/limMatchScore, limRootTheta) is updated on a pinned point
 % exactly as on any other.
angles = obj.uniqueLimitingAngles() 
% Unique propagation angles among "limiting"-bound points, i.e. the angles
 % at which the inversion loop must compute a Christoffel root set.
 % >> angles = uniqueLimitingAngles(obj)
angles = obj.uniqueRitzAngles() 
% Unique propagation angles among Ritz-mode-bound points ("ritzMode"/"pseudoSAW"),
 % i.e. the angles at which the inversion loop must solve an eigenproblem.
 % >> angles = uniqueRitzAngles(obj)

SensitivityAnalysis value class

% SensitivityAnalysis — the sensitivity/Jacobian matrix G, the Gauss-Newton Hessian
 %   S = 2*G'*G, its eigendecomposition (transformMatrix T, sensitivityEigenvalues psi),
 %   and the cutoff deciding how many transformed constants are well-determined enough
 %   to optimise.
 %
 % EXPERIMENTAL: validated on synthetic data only; the interface may change.
 %
 %   Row k of G is one data point (an associated Ritz mode, a limiting bulk velocity, a
 %   pulse-echo phase velocity, or the bulk-modulus pseudo-datum). Column i is one
 %   independent elastic constant of the inverted layer, in ElastConsts.vector order.
 %   G_ki = d(v_k)/d(c_i), evaluated at the initial guess.
 %
 %   The Ritz-mode rows use the analytic Rayleigh-quotient formula
 %     G_ki = Re[ alpha_k' (N' dK/dc_i N) alpha_k ] / (8 pi^2 v_k)
 %   with GammaMatrix.layerBlockDerivs for dK/dc_i -- the same mathematical pattern as
 %   RitzSolver.calcGroupVelocity's dw2/dD = alpha' (dK/dD) alpha, differentiating w.r.t.
 %   an elastic constant instead of the domain depth.
 %
 %   The limiting/phase-velocity/bulk-modulus rows use a central finite difference on the
 %   SAME validated forward-model calls (CalcCommon.limitingAndBulk, Christoffel.matrix,
 %   ElastConsts.Voigt2Bulk) rather than a hand-derived analytic derivative -- a deliberate
 %   simplification (Christoffel's own Hellmann-Feynman derivative helpers are private
 %   and tied to a numerical-regularisation scheme not reused here). Cheap: one 3x3
 %   Christoffel eigenproblem (or small root-find) per constant, negligible next to the
 %   Ritz eigensolves.
 %
 %   JOINT MULTI-DATASET INVERSION (several crystallographic cuts of the SAME crystal,
 %   sharing one target elasticity): `structure`, `association` and `measured` in calc()
 %   are accepted as OBJECT ARRAYS, one element per dataset d = 1..nDatasets (length 1 is
 %   the ordinary single-dataset case). Every dataset must share the SAME invertedLayerNo
 %   LAYER INDEX, the SAME symmClassNo and the SAME nIndepConsts for that layer (checked);
 %   everything else per dataset (orientation, other layers, CalcParams/legOrder/relDepth)
 %   may differ freely. The per-dataset G_d blocks are vertically stacked
 %   G = [G_1; G_2; ...; G_D] BEFORE the single pooled SVD. Row order: all of dataset 1's
 %   rows (Ritz-class, then limiting, then phaseVelocities, then bulkModulus), then all of
 %   dataset 2's rows in the same sub-order, etc. -- pointWeights must be supplied in that
 %   same concatenated order. The canonical initial guess (initialVoigtVec/
 %   initialTransformedVec) is taken from DATASET 1's stored elConsts for the inverted
 %   layer; every other dataset's stored elConsts for that layer is unused by calc() (only
 %   its dirMat/rho/other layers matter), though physically it should be the same crystal.
 %
 % Value class: computed once and then read.
 %
 % GuliweRR v6.0

Properties

SensitivityAnalysis.DEFAULT_SENSITIVITY_CUTOFF (default: 0.00625) 
default of SensitivityCutoff (see the property)
obj.includePseudoSAW (default: true) 
pseudo-Rayleigh points contribute rows to G by DEFAULT: a TGS angular map can be carried by the pseudo-SAW branch alone over wide angle ranges, and excluding it throws away the only data there
obj.initialTransformedVec (default: []) 
T' * initialVoigtVec   [L x 1]
obj.initialVoigtVec (default: []) 
c at the initial guess [L x 1]
obj.nDatasets (default: 1) 
D; number of jointly-inverted datasets (crystallographic cuts) pooled into G; 1 for an ordinary single-dataset call
obj.nIndepConsts (default: 0) 
L
obj.nOptimisedConstants (default: 0) 
automatic cutoff P
obj.normalisedSensitivityRoots (default: []) 
sqrt(psi/psi(1)) [L x 1]
obj.pointWeights (default: []) 
[N_data x 1]; default ones = unweighted
obj.sensitivityCutoff (default: 0.00625) 
keep p while normalisedSensitivityRoots(p) >= sensitivityCutoff (error amplification <= 160x); an empirical choice (Grabec et al. 2024), not derived
obj.sensitivityEigenvalues (default: []) 
psi, descending [L x 1]
obj.sensitivityMatrix (default: []) 
G   [N_data x L], (km/s)/GPa
obj.transformMatrix (default: []) 
T, orthogonal [L x L]

Methods

obj = SensitivityAnalysis() 
% Initialise an empty SensitivityAnalysis
 % >> obj = SensitivityAnalysis()
obj = SensitivityAnalysis.calc(structure, association, invertedLayerNo, measured, varargin) 
% Build the sensitivity matrix and its eigendecomposition
 % >> obj = SensitivityAnalysis.calc(structure, association, invertedLayerNo, measured, ...)
 %
 % INPUT: 1. structure       .. Structure at the INITIAL GUESS; scalar for a single
 %                             dataset, or a [1 x nDatasets]/[nDatasets x 1] OBJECT
 %                             ARRAY for a JOINT multi-dataset inversion (several
 %                             crystallographic cuts of the SAME crystal) -- see the
 %                             class docblock.
 %        2. association    .. ModeAssociation (or array, one per dataset, same
 %                             length as structure), built at the same initial guess
 %        3. invertedLayerNo .. index of the layer whose elasticity is inverted;
 %                             SHARED across every dataset (same layer position)
 %        4. measured        .. MeasuredVelocities (or array, one per dataset) --
 %                             for phaseVelocities/bulkModulus
 % Name-value: PointWeights (default ones), IncludePseudoSAW (default TRUE --
 %                 see the includePseudoSAW property; pass false to exclude
 %                 pseudo-Rayleigh points from G),
 %             UseParallel ("auto"|"always"|"never", default "auto"),
 %             SensitivityCutoff (default 1/160)

ElasticityInversion handle class · extends matlab.mixin.Copyable

% ElasticityInversion — the optimisation orchestrator. Owns the Structure, the
 %   inverted-layer index, the MeasuredVelocities, the live ModeAssociation, the
 %   SensitivityAnalysis, the optimiser configuration and the results.
 %
 % EXPERIMENTAL: validated on synthetic data only; the interface may change.
 %
 %   calc() runs an lsqnonlin schedule over the leading P transformed elastic
 %   constants, holding the remaining ones frozen at their initial-guess values.
 %   Two modes (obj.stepwise): the default GROWS P from optimisedConstantRange(1) to
 %   (2), one lsqnonlin stage per value, each warm-started from the previous stage's
 %   result -- safer when the initial guess is distant/uncertain, at the cost of extra
 %   stages; stepwise=false instead runs ONE lsqnonlin call directly at the full
 %   P = optimisedConstantRange(2), which is usually enough in practice, leaving
 %   step-wise growth as the fallback for a run that fails to converge cleanly. Each
 %   objective evaluation (costAndJacobian) runs three passes per dataset:
 %     PASS 1, the Ritz/pseudo-SAW channel, per unique associated angle -- assemble the
 %       constrained stiffness by LINEAR SUPERPOSITION of precomputed blocks (K is
 %       exactly linear in the elastic constants, so no forward sweep is ever re-run),
 %       solve the eigenproblem, M-orthonormalise, and RE-IDENTIFY each associated mode
 %       by M-weighted MAC against its anchor eigenvector (optionally STEERED by a live
 %       Green's-function peak for pseudo-SAW points -- see pseudoSAWRefinement).
 %       Residual and analytic Jacobian come from the same Rayleigh-quotient pattern as
 %       the sensitivity matrix, evaluated at the CURRENT eigenvectors.
 %     PASS 2, the limiting channel, per angle carrying limiting points -- compute the
 %       labelled Christoffel root set, RE-IDENTIFY each anchored root by
 %       polarisation x velocity-proximity x theta-proximity x branch continuity
 %       (ModeAssociation.rebindLimiting), take the residual from the tracked root, and
 %       build the Jacobian column by central difference using ANCHORED LOCAL
 %       REFINEMENT from the tracked theta* rather than 2P further full sagittal scans.
 %     PASS 3, the pulse-echo phase-velocity and bulk-modulus channels -- direct
 %       evaluation plus a central difference, no association, no tracking.
 %   Then it promotes the rebound association (and, under the "chained" anchor policy,
 %   the updated anchors) to the live state ONLY when the pooled cost improved, so a
 %   rejected trial step cannot corrupt the tracking.
 %
 %   Passes 1 and 2 both honour the exclusion rule: a point whose re-identification
 %   confidence collapsed keeps its ROW but has both its residual and its Jacobian row
 %   ZEROED (never deleted -- lsqnonlin requires a constant residual length). Zeroing is
 %   equivalent to exclusion for a least-squares solve and keeps the residual and the
 %   Jacobian row consistent with each other.
 %
 % JOINT MULTI-DATASET INVERSION (several crystallographic cuts of the SAME crystal,
 %   sharing one target elasticity): `structure`, `measured` and `association` are OBJECT
 %   ARRAYS, one element per dataset d = 1..nDatasets (length 1 is the ordinary
 %   single-dataset case). All datasets share the SAME invertedLayerNo LAYER INDEX and
 %   the same `sensitivity` (built by SensitivityAnalysis.calc from the SAME dataset
 %   arrays, pooling every dataset's G_d block before ONE eigendecomposition). Each
 %   dataset keeps its OWN Structure (own dirMat/other-layers/CalcParams) and its OWN live
 %   ModeAssociation, re-bound independently every objective evaluation (mode tracking is
 %   strictly per dataset). Every dataset's residual/Jacobian rows are concatenated into
 %   ONE r/J handed to a single lsqnonlin call: one shared optimisation, not D separate ones.
 %   The cost-improvement gate is the TOTAL POOLED cost, and when it improves every
 %   dataset's rebound association is promoted TOGETHER. At the end of the run calc()
 %   writes the SAME recovered elasticity into every dataset's
 %   `structure(d).mater(invertedLayerNo).elConsts`; each dataset's OTHER layers/
 %   orientation/CalcParams are left untouched.
 %   NOT supported (same-crystal assumption): datasets disagreeing on symmClassNo/
 %   nIndepConsts for the inverted layer (the constructor and SensitivityAnalysis.calc
 %   both error). Different LAYER COUNTS elsewhere in the stack are fine, since only the
 %   shared layer INDEX must match. recalcForwardAtResult() covers DATASET 1 only.
 %
 % Handle class: carries the iteration state, mutated in place across the objective
 %   evaluations of a run.
 %
 % GuliweRR v6.0

Properties

obj.anchorPolicy (default: 'chained') 
'fixed' | 'chained'
obj.association (default: ModeAssociation) 
LIVE association(s), array one per dataset, promoted TOGETHER on improving evals (pooled cost gate)
obj.includePseudoSAW (default: true) 
pseudo-Rayleigh points contribute residual rows by DEFAULT: a TGS angular map can be carried by the pseudo-SAW branch alone over wide angle ranges, and excluding it discards the only data there. Taken from the SensitivityAnalysis (an explicit value must equal it), or G's rows and the residual's rows would describe different datasets.
obj.invertedLayerNo (default: 0) 
SAME layer index in every dataset
obj.limMatchScoreThresh (default: 0.5) 
below this combined score a limiting-root match is rejected as limRootLost -- see ModeAssociation.defaultTrackOpts
obj.limitingRescanCadence (default: 1) 
run the FULL Christoffel root scan every n-th objective evaluation, using anchored local refinement in between. 1 (default) = always rescan; higher values are safe only because a failed refinement (LimitingVels.refineRoot's ok = false) forces an immediate rescan anyway
obj.lowerBound (default: []) 
on the transformed constants, empty = none
obj.macWarnThreshold (default: 0.7) 
below this MAC a Ritz-class match is low-confidence: warn once per (point, stage), zero the row, and do NOT let it update the anchor
obj.measured (default: MeasuredVelocities) 
array, one per dataset
obj.nDatasets (default: 1) 
D; numel(structure)
obj.optimisedConstantRange (default: []) 
[first last], or a single N meaning [N N] (run at exactly N constants, one-shot-equivalent); empty => [1, sensitivity.nOptimisedConstants]
obj.pointWeights (default: []) 
[] => unweighted; one per data row, in the sensitivity's row order (default: the sensitivity's own weights)
obj.pseudoSAWGreenCadence (default: 5) 
full Green rescan every n-th PROMOTED evaluation (never on a rejected trial -- a rejected trial must not burn Green solves), plus an immediate single-angle rescan wherever steering failed (a pSAW point found no candidate inside the Green filter: modeCollision)
obj.pseudoSAWRefinement (default: 'green') 
'none' | 'green'. Default 'green': plain MAC tracking can follow a pseudo-SAW point onto a neighbouring member of the Ritz cluster that represents the leaky wave, with a MAC of 1 (see ModeAssociation.rebind). Costs nothing on a dataset with no pseudoSAW points: no such point, no Green solve.
obj.rebindVelocityWindow (default: 1) 
[km/s]
obj.sensitivity (default: SensitivityAnalysis) 
ONE pooled analysis, shared by every dataset
obj.stepwise (default: true) 
true (default): grow P from range(1) to range(2), one lsqnonlin stage per value, warm-started from the previous stage's result (the safer choice for a distant/uncertain initial guess). false: ONE lsqnonlin call directly at P = range(2), all free constants optimised at once from the initial guess. range(1) is unused when stepwise=false.
obj.structure (default: Structure) 
current elasticity (updated at the end of calc(), not per-eval); [1 x nDatasets] array for a joint multi-dataset inversion

Methods

obj = ElasticityInversion(structure, measured, association, sensitivity, invertedLayerNo, varargin) 
% Construct the inversion orchestrator
 % >> obj = ElasticityInversion(structure, measured, association, sensitivity, ...
 %              invertedLayerNo, Name, Value, ...)
 %
 % INPUT: 1. structure       .. Structure at the INITIAL GUESS; scalar for a single
 %                             dataset, or a [1 x nDatasets] OBJECT ARRAY for a JOINT
 %                             multi-dataset inversion (several crystallographic cuts
 %                             of the SAME crystal) -- see the class docblock.
 %        2. measured        .. MeasuredVelocities (or array, one per dataset)
 %        3. association     .. ModeAssociation (or array, one per dataset), built at
 %                             the same initial guess
 %        4. sensitivity     .. SensitivityAnalysis, built at the same initial guess
 %                             from the SAME dataset arrays (always scalar: one pooled
 %                             analysis shared by every dataset)
 %        5. invertedLayerNo .. index of the layer whose elasticity is inverted;
 %                             SHARED across every dataset (same layer position)
 % Name-Value: AnchorPolicy ('fixed'|'chained', default 'chained'),
 %             RebindVelocityWindow (default 1.0 km/s),
 %             IncludePseudoSAW (default: the sensitivity's; an explicit value
 %                 must equal it -- see the property),
 %             PointWeights (default: the sensitivity's, i.e. unweighted unless it
 %                 was built with weights; one per data row of the sensitivity, in
 %                 its row order -- see SensitivityAnalysis),
 %             ToleranceLevel (default 2),
 %             LowerBound/UpperBound (on the transformed constants, one per
 %                 independent constant, default [] = none),
 %             OptimisedConstantRange ([first last], or a single N meaning [N N];
 %                 default [1, sensitivity.nOptimisedConstants]),
 %             Stepwise (default true: grow P stage by stage; false: one
 %                 lsqnonlin call at P = range(2) -- see the stepwise property),
 %             MacWarnThreshold (default 0.7),
 %             LimMatchScoreThresh (default 0.5),
 %             LimitingRescanCadence (default 1),
 %             PseudoSAWRefinement ('none'|'green', default 'green'),
 %             PseudoSAWGreenCadence (default 5)
obj = obj.calc() 
% Run the lsqnonlin schedule, step-wise (obj.stepwise = true) or in one go
 % >> obj = calc(obj)
 % Populates obj.result and updates obj.structure to the inverted elasticity.
 % obj is a handle -- the return value is the same object, for chaining only.
 % Repeatable: every call starts from the constructor's structure and
 % association (the initial guess and its binding), not from a previous run's
 % promoted anchors, so a second call reproduces the first.
sweep = obj.recalcForwardAtResult(angles, vMax, calcMode, retentionLevel) 
% Fresh forward sweep at the inverted elasticity, for display/checking
 % >> sweep = recalcForwardAtResult(obj, angles, vMax, calcMode, retentionLevel)
 % Deliberately separate and opt-in -- costs a full sweep, never called inside calc().
 % For a joint inversion this recalculates DATASET 1 only; for any other dataset,
 % call Calc.angSweepHS/angSweepLayered directly on obj.structure(d)/obj.association(d).
visual

Visualisation

Vis value class

% Namespace for GuliweRR's plotting and visualisation functions.
 % All methods are static -- do not instantiate this class.
 %
 % Usage:  Vis.plotModalShape(ritzSolver, modeNo)
 %         Vis.plotAngularMap(sweepResult)

Methods

obj = Vis() 
% Namespace for GuliweRR's plotting and visualisation functions.
 % All methods are static -- do not instantiate this class.
 %
 % Usage:  Vis.plotModalShape(ritzSolver, modeNo)
 %         Vis.plotAngularMap(sweepResult)
cMap = Vis.ColMapGrayOrange() 
% Grayscale-through-orange colormap, sequential -- the Green's-function map default
 % >> cMap = Vis.ColMapGrayOrange()
 %
 % OUTPUT: cMap .. 150x3 RGB colormap, dark grey at the low end through light
 %                 grey, then light-to-saturated orange at the high end
 %
 % A hardcoded lookup table, not generated from a formula. It is the
 % colormap of the UTGS paper's Green-map figures (arXiv:2510.10696), resampled from 3668 to
 % 150 rows: the grey part runs to row 94, so the white point sits at ~62 % of the scale.
cMap = Vis.ColormapRWB() 
% Red-White-Blue diverging colormap, for signed data (e.g. displacement fields)
 % >> cMap = Vis.ColormapRWB()
 %
 % OUTPUT: cMap .. 201x3 RGB colormap, red at low end, blue at high end, true
 %                 white at the centre
 %
 % Formula courtesy of IA Veres. The centre entry is 1, giving a true white
 % zero-crossing — the physically meaningful choice for signed modal-shape
 % fields. A 0 there renders pure black instead.
cMap = Vis.ColormapRYG() 
% Red-Yellow-Green sequential colormap, for a confidence/quality scalar in [0,1]
 % where the VALUE, not just the ordering, has a meaning: 0 = bad, 1 = good.
 % >> cMap = Vis.ColormapRYG()
 %
 % OUTPUT: cMap .. 101x3 RGB colormap, red at 0, yellow at 0.5, green at 1
 %
 % Deliberately not a perceptually-uniform sequential map like parula: parula puts
 % its brightest, most eye-catching colour (yellow) at the HIGH end, which reads as
 % "flagged"/"warning" for a quality score where high = good, not as reassurance.
 % Traffic-light semantics instead -- e.g. ModeAssociation's per-point MAC value,
 % where red should mean "look at this" and green "this tracked cleanly".
dispField = Vis.calcDispField2D(ritzSolver, modeNo, meshSpacing) 
% Signed 2D (depth x lateral) displacement field for one Ritz mode
