 % >> dispField = Vis.calcDispField2D(ritzSolver, modeNo, meshSpacing)
 %
 % INPUT:  1. ritzSolver  .. RitzSolver (or subclass) object, eigVecs_con/
 %                           nullConstrMat populated for the requested mode
 %                           (retentionLevel='full'; there is no
 %                           recompute-on-demand for a pruned RitzSolver)
 %         2. modeNo      .. which mode (column of eigVecs_con) to evaluate
 %  (opt)  3. meshSpacing .. depth/lateral mesh spacing (default 0.01, matching
 %                           RitzSolver.calcDisplacement's cached-basis default)
 % OUTPUT: dispField .. {3 x nLay} cell array, dispField{iDir,iLay} = real signed
 %                      2D field [nMeshDepth x nMeshLateral], normalised by one
 %                      global max across every direction and layer together.
 %                      That max is over POSITIVE values only, not max(abs(...)),
 %                      so a negative-dominant mode can fall outside [-1,1].
 %
 % Built on RitzSolver.calcDisplacement; this function only slices that output
 % into per-layer depth profiles. The lateral factor, outer product and
 % normalisation are the @Vis-private visDispField2D helper.
dispField = Vis.calcGreenDispField2D(greenObj, forceDir, meshSpacing) 
% Signed 2D (depth x lateral) displacement field for one Green's-function
 % response (one velocity, one force direction) -- the Green's-response
 % analogue of calcDispField2D, feeding ModalShapeViewerGUI's Green's-response
 % source.
 % >> dispField = Vis.calcGreenDispField2D(greenObj, forceDir, meshSpacing)
 %
 % INPUT:  1. greenObj    .. GreenSolver object with .dispField populated: call
 %                           GreenSolver.calc(structure, angle, velSelected, ...)
 %                           with nargout>1 first. Full output is half-space
 %                           only. velSelected should be a SCALAR velocity (a
 %                           1-element velRange) — dispField is built over every
 %                           point of whatever velRange is given, so a single
 %                           value keeps the expensive full-partial-wave solve
 %                           to the one velocity being displayed
 %         2. forceDir    .. which force direction's response to show (1-3)
 %  (opt)  3. meshSpacing .. lateral mesh spacing (default 0.01)
 % OUTPUT: dispField .. {3 x 1} cell (half-space only, so nLay=1 always),
 %                      dispField{iDir} = real signed 2D field
 %                      [nMeshDepth x nMeshLateral], normalised by the global max
 %                      across all 3 directions — the same shape and
 %                      normalisation convention as calcDispField2D's {3 x nLay}
 %                      output, so plotGreenModalShape mirrors plotModalShape.
 %                      Row 1 = deepest, row end = the free surface, RE-ORIENTED
 %                      from GreenSolver.calc's own storage (index 1 = surface)
 %                      to match calcDispField2D's row convention
 %
 % No physical depth-axis reconstruction is attempted: GreenSolver.calc never
 % stores its xx3 axis, and plotModalShape only uses a normalised row-index
 % fraction for its y-axis anyway.
dispField = Vis.calcGreenDispField2DLayered(greenObj, structure, forceDir, meshSpacing) 
% Signed 2D (depth x lateral) displacement field for a LAYERED Green's-
 % function response (one velocity, one force direction) -- the layered
 % counterpart of calcGreenDispField2D, built from GreenSolver.calc's
 % k3OutLayered/polarizationULayered/partialAmplitudeLayered/hScaleOut
 % instead of its half-space-only .dispField.
 % >> dispField = Vis.calcGreenDispField2DLayered(greenObj, structure, forceDir, meshSpacing)
 %
 % INPUT:  1. greenObj    .. GreenSolver object from GreenSolver.calc(structure,
 %                           angle, velSelected, ...) called with nargout>1 on
 %                           a LAYERED structure (nLay>=2). velSelected should
 %                           be a SCALAR velocity: as in calcGreenDispField2D,
 %                           only the FIRST stored velocity point is read
 %         2. structure   .. the SAME Structure passed to GreenSolver.calc,
 %                           which keeps no copy of it. Only
 %                           .calcParams(:).relDepth is read
 %         3. forceDir    .. which force direction's response to show (1-3)
 %  (opt)  4. meshSpacing .. depth/lateral mesh spacing (default 0.01)
 % OUTPUT: dispField .. {3 x nLay} cell, dispField{iDir,iLay} = real signed
 %                      2D field [nMeshDepth x nMeshLateral], Structure order
 %                      (1 = substrate ... nLay = outermost film), normalised
 %                      by ONE global max across every direction and layer --
 %                      the same convention as calcDispField2D, so
 %                      plotGreenModalShapeLayered can mirror plotModalShape.
 %                      The substrate comes back at its FULL relDepth(1)
 %                      extent; substrateCutoff cropping is the caller's job
 %
 % RECONSTRUCTION: every layer's solved amplitude vector is referenced to the
 % SAME global x3 as the substrate (x3=0 at the substrate interface,
 % x3=-H_total at the free surface; Every 2002 convention) -- buildBCMatrix
 % never re-references a film's phase to its own local boundaries, only to
 % whichever interface plane a boundary-condition row enforces. Per layer
 % (nWaves = 6 film, 3 substrate):
 %
 %   u(x3,ci,cj) = sum_n A(n,cj) * U(ci,n) * exp(i*k3(n)*x3)
 %
 % ci = displacement component, cj = force direction. x3 maps linearly per
 % layer onto RitzSolver's ti in [-1,1] (ti=-1 deep, ti=+1 shallow), the
 % row-1-deepest ordering visDispField2D expects. Derivation:
 % docs/reference/architecture/green.md, "Layered extension". At the free surface the
 % reconstruction reproduces the solver's surface G_IJ, and a film of the
 % substrate's own material gives the plain half-space field to round-off.
 %
 % SUBSTRATE DISPLAY EXTENT: GreenSolver's substrate is analytically
 % semi-infinite, so structure.calcParams(1).relDepth plays no part in its
 % physics. It is reused here purely as a finite display extent -- the same
 % substrate truncation a companion RitzSolver on this Structure uses, so at
 % matching meshSpacing both solvers share an identical ti grid per layer,
 % index for index, and a direct Ritz-vs-Green shape comparison is meaningful.
 %
 % EVANESCENT-OVERFLOW LIMITATION: exp(i*k3*x3) for a single partial wave can
 % overflow to Inf for a thick film, a deep substrate extent, or a layer far
 % from x3=0, even though the true summed field is finite (Inf times a tiny
 % amplitude is not the small residual it should be). Not fixed here -- see
 % docs/reference/architecture/green.md, "Conditioning".
frac = Vis.calcModalShapeRowHeights(relDepths, minFrac) 
% Non-proportional per-layer height fractions for the modal-shape depth stack
 % >> frac = Vis.calcModalShapeRowHeights(relDepths, minFrac)
 %
 % INPUT: 1. relDepths .. [nLay x 1] every layer's relDepth, uncropped --
 %                        layer 1 = substrate, layer nLay = outermost film
 %  (opt) 2. minFrac    .. floor fraction of the total displayed height
 %                         every layer is guaranteed, before the remainder
 %                         is split proportionally to true relDepth
 %                         (default 0.3). Auto-clamped down if nLay*minFrac
 %                         would reach/exceed 1 (many thin films).
 % OUTPUT: frac .. [nLay x 1], sums to 1
 %
 % Every layer is guaranteed a floor share up front so a thin film stays visible
 % next to a much deeper substrate.
[rgbCell, ampCell] = Vis.calcPolarisationRgb(dispField) 
% Merge a displacement-field triplet into one true-colour polarisation image
 % >> [rgbCell, ampCell] = Vis.calcPolarisationRgb(dispField)
 %
 % INPUT: 1. dispField .. {3 x nLay} cell of signed real 2D fields
 %                        [nMeshDepth x nMeshLateral], one per displacement
 %                        direction (standard frame: 1 = propagation, 2 =
 %                        in-plane transverse, 3 = surface normal), as from
 %                        Vis.calcDispField2D / calcGreenDispField2D(Layered).
 %                        The layers may differ in row count (cropped substrate)
 % OUTPUT: rgbCell .. {1 x nLay}, each [nMeshDepth x nMeshLateral x 3] RGB
 %                    image in [0,1]
 %         ampCell .. {1 x nLay}, each [nMeshDepth x nMeshLateral] amplitude
 %                    |u| / max|u| in [0,1], the same normaliser for every layer
 %
 % The colour scheme of Seiner's displacement-field figures in the UTGS paper
 % (Grabec et al., rev2, Figs 3(g) and 4(b)):
 %   - hue = the DIRECTION of the displacement at that point: the unit vector
 %     p = (|u1|, |u2|, |u3|)/|u| weights one base colour per direction,
 %     hue = p1*red + p2*blue + p3*green (visPolarisationColours: red and blue
 %     are the primaries, green is darkened to 0.65 to balance its luminance).
 %     Every direction maps to one point of the positive octant of the unit
 %     sphere, which is what Vis.plotPolarisationKey draws;
 %   - brightness = the amplitude |u|, white at zero, the saturated hue at the
 %     field's global maximum: pixel = (1 - a) + a * hue.
 % Signs are dropped (|u_i|), so each component shows two maxima per
 % wavelength. The field is one snapshot (the real part at one phase), so two
 % components that are 90 deg out of phase -- u1 and u3 of a Rayleigh wave --
 % appear as separate stripes a quarter wavelength apart, not as one mixed
 % colour.
 %
 % One normaliser across all layers (max of |u|, not the positive maximum
 % used by the signed component panels), so nothing is clipped and layers stay
 % comparable.
cutoffRelDepth = Vis.calcSubstrateCutoffDepth(substrateCutoff, cutoffParam, relDepths, substrateDispMag) 
% Effective substrate relDepth to DISPLAY for plotModalShape's axis layout
 % >> cutoffRelDepth = Vis.calcSubstrateCutoffDepth(substrateCutoff, cutoffParam, relDepths, substrateDispMag)
 %
 % INPUT: 1. substrateCutoff  .. 'none' | 'decay' | 'fixedMultiple'
 %        2. cutoffParam      .. threshold fraction (decay, e.g. 0.05) or multiple
 %                               (fixedMultiple, e.g. 4); ignored for 'none'
 %        3. relDepths        .. [nLay x 1] every layer's relDepth, layer 1 = substrate,
 %                               layer nLay = outermost film (nearest the free surface)
 %  (opt) 4. substrateDispMag .. required only for 'decay': [nMeshDepth x 1]
 %                               displacement magnitude across the SUBSTRATE's own
 %                               depth mesh, index 1 = deepest (ti=-1), index end =
 %                               the substrate/first-film interface (ti=+1) -- or the
 %                               free surface itself if nLay==1
 % OUTPUT: cutoffRelDepth .. effective substrate relDepth to use for axis-height
 %                          allocation (<= relDepths(1))
s = Vis.classificationScoreScale(method, isLayered) 
% The score scale of a classification method: its standards and the display range
 % >> s = Vis.classificationScoreScale(method, isLayered)
 %
 % INPUT: 1. method    .. 'green' | 'refined' | 'ritz' (layered: 'green' | 'ritz')
 %        2. isLayered .. true for a layered stack
 % OUTPUT: s .. struct:
 %           high, low  .. the 'high' / 'low' threshold values (the classes' own
 %                         constants, read here, never copied)
 %           lowScale   .. 'score' if 'low' is compared with the entry score, like
 %                         'high' and a numeric standard; 'structure-relative' if it
 %                         is compared with a separate structure-relative score
 %                         ('green' / 'refined' half-space: lowScore / scoreLow)
 %           sizeRange  .. [lo hi] score range the marker size spans; scores
 %                         outside it are clipped
 %           name       .. display name ('Green' | 'Refined' | 'Ritz')
 %
 % Scores are log10 amplitude ratios (dB = 20 x score), comparable only within one
 % method. sizeRange starts at the looser ('high') threshold, where the weakest
 % accepted entry sits, and ends one decade (20 dB) above the stricter 'low'
 % threshold where both are on the score scale, else 1.5 decades (30 dB) above
 % 'high'. So the strong entries of a typical map spread over the size range
 % instead of saturating it.
 %
 % Shared by Vis.plotClassificationDiagnostic / plotClassificationCurve (marker
 % size) and the viewers' classification bar (standard values, dB labels).
[uCoord, wCoord, ptsXYZ] = Vis.extractPlanarCut(X, Y, Z, theta, phi, planeNormal, inPlaneRef) 
% Extract the planar cut of a (theta,phi)-parametrised 3D surface
 % >> [uCoord, wCoord, ptsXYZ] = extractPlanarCut(X, Y, Z, theta, phi, planeNormal, inPlaneRef)
 %
 % INPUT: 1-3. X,Y,Z     .. [nPhi x nTheta] surface coordinates for ONE
 %                          sheet/wave-type (call once per sheet for a
 %                          multi-sheet surface, e.g. plotVelSurface's or
 %                          plotGroupVelSurface's 3 wave types -- typically
 %                          hOut(k).XData/.YData/.ZData directly)
 %        4-5. theta,phi .. the parameter grids used to build X,Y,Z (plain
 %                          vectors, e.g. linspace(0,pi,nTheta)/
 %                          linspace(0,2*pi,nPhi) -- the SAME nTheta/nPhi
 %                          passed to plotVelSurface/plotGroupVelSurface)
 %        6. planeNormal .. 3-vector, normal of the cutting plane (through
 %                          the origin) -- e.g. [0;0;1] for the sagittal
 %                          plane in the standard convention (axis 3 =
 %                          surface normal) used throughout this project,
 %                          including plotVelSurface/plotGroupVelSurface.
 %  (opt) 7. inPlaneRef  .. 3-vector, one of the two in-plane basis
 %                          directions to project onto (default: an
 %                          arbitrary vector perpendicular to planeNormal)
 % OUTPUT: uCoord, wCoord .. 2D in-plane coordinates of the cut curve --
 %                           possibly several disconnected segments,
 %                           NaN-separated (matches MATLAB's own plot/
 %                           contour convention for broken curves; SAW/
 %                           pseudoSAW cuts in particular need not be
 %                           continuous)
 %         ptsXYZ         .. [N x 3] the same points in original 3D
 %                           coordinates, same NaN-row separation
 %
 % The full-sphere sweep is needed first because a vector-valued surface's
 % plotted point is not simply the wave normal scaled by a scalar — group
 % velocity can point off-plane.
 %
 % Method: the surface point at (theta,phi) lies in the cutting plane
 % exactly where f(theta,phi) = dot([X,Y,Z](theta,phi), planeNormal) = 0.
 % Finds that zero contour with MATLAB's own contourc over the (theta,phi)
 % parameter domain, then bilinearly interpolates X,Y,Z at each contour
 % vertex to recover the exact 3D crossing point and projects onto two
 % orthonormal in-plane basis vectors.
[absG, componentLabel, velAxis] = Vis.greenComponentData(result, component) 
% |G| data, TeX label and velocity axis of one Green component of a Calc result
 % >> [absG, componentLabel] = Vis.greenComponentData(result)            % |G13_eff|
 % >> [absG, componentLabel] = Vis.greenComponentData(result, 'G33')     % recomputed
 % >> [absG, componentLabel, velAxis] = Vis.greenComponentData(result, 'spot')
 %
 % INPUT: 1. result    .. CalcSingleHS/CalcSingleLayered (a curve) or
 %                        CalcAngSweepHS/CalcAngSweepLayered (a map) result,
 %                        calc()'d with calcMode='full'
 %  (opt) 2. component .. 'G13eff' (default) | 'GIJ' | 'GIJeff', I = force and
 %                        J = displacement direction (1 = propagation, 3 = normal)
 %                          'G13eff' -- |(G13_+ - G13_-)/2| = |(G13_+ - G31_+)/2|,
 %                                      the +-k symmetrised TGS response: the
 %                                      ONLY component a result stores, and the
 %                                      one the existence score reads
 %                          'GIJ'    -- one-sided |G_IJ(+k)|, e.g. 'G13' (G13_+),
 %                                      'G33'; 'GIJeff' -- +-k symmetrised
 %                        Anything but 'G13eff' is RECOMPUTED on the result's own
 %                        axis and damping (greenComponent()/greenComponentMap():
 %                        one Green solve per angle) and not stored.
 %                          'spot'   -- the stored spot-averaged |G| (a half-space
 %                                      result's calcSpotGreen()/calcSpotGreenMap()
 %                                      run with a ProbeKernel), on its own axis
 % OUTPUT: absG           .. [nVel x 1] (single result) or [nVel x nAngles] (sweep),
 %                           on velAxis, single precision
 %         componentLabel .. TeX label: 'G_{13}^{eff}', 'G_{13}^{+}', 'G_{33}',
 %                           'G_{13}^{spot}', ...
 %         velAxis        .. [nVel x 1] km/s: result.greenVelAxis, or
 %                           result.spotGreenVelAxis for 'spot'
 %
 % Errors clearly when the data is missing: calcMode was not 'full'
 % (Vis:greenComponentData:noGreenData), or the stored G13_eff is empty
 % (Vis:greenComponentData:noG13EffData); 'spot' without the stored spot data
 % (:noSpotGreenData) or on a layered result (:noSpotSupport).
 %
 % Public rather than @Vis-private because the viewers place their own overlays
 % on these curves/maps and must read the same component the plot shows.
[xEdges, yEdges, CPadded] = Vis.pcolorCenteredGrid(x, y, C) 
% Cell-edge coordinates + padded C so pcolor(...)+shading('flat') draws
 % each C(i,j) cell CENTERED on (x(j), y(i))
 % >> [xEdges, yEdges, CPadded] = Vis.pcolorCenteredGrid(x, y, C)
 %
 % pcolor's native behaviour treats (x(j), y(i)) as a cell's LOWER-LEFT
 % corner, not its centre, and 'flat' shading additionally drops the last
 % row/column of C -- so C(:,j) ends up drawn from x(j) to x(j+1) rather
 % than centred on x(j) (docs/pitfalls.md). This function converts sample
 % coordinates to cell edges instead, so the caller's own pcolor(xEdges,
 % yEdges, CPadded) call draws each sample centred where it belongs.
 %
 % INPUT: 1. x .. [1xN] or [Nx1] sample coordinates (need not be evenly
 %                spaced)
 %        2. y .. [1xM] or [Mx1] sample coordinates
 %        3. C .. [MxN] colour data (double or single), C(i,j) belongs to
 %                (x(j), y(i)); CPadded keeps its class
 % OUTPUT: xEdges  .. [1x(N+1)] cell-edge x-coordinates
 %         yEdges  .. [(M+1)x1] cell-edge y-coordinates
 %         CPadded .. [(M+1)x(N+1)] -- last row/column duplicated from the
 %                    last real row/column; 'flat' shading never draws it,
 %                    it exists only to satisfy pcolor's size requirement
 %
 % Interior edges are midpoints between consecutive samples; the two open ends
 % are linearly extrapolated by their nearest interior half-gap. Under
 % non-uniform spacing this brackets each sample by half its gap to each
 % neighbour, giving unequal margins where the gaps differ — equal margins on
 % both sides is not achievable without overlapping cells or leaving gaps.
 %
 % x/y need only be MONOTONIC, not increasing: CalcIsofreqAngSweepLayered's
 % kParRange (= omega0 ./ vAxis) is a naturally decreasing axis.
[typeNames, typeColors, parityNames, parityColors] = Vis.plateModeTypeStyle() 
% Display names and colours for the four Lamb/plate mode types and their two parity classes
 % >> [typeNames, typeColors, parityNames, parityColors] = Vis.plateModeTypeStyle()
 %
 % OUTPUT: typeNames    .. {1 x 4} cell of names, indexed by
 %                         RitzSolverLamb.modesType (1-4)
 %         typeColors   .. [4 x 3] RGB, same indexing
 %         parityNames  .. {1 x 4} cell, the parity class of each type
 %                         ('Asymm-class' for 1 and 3, 'Symm-class' for 2 and 4) --
 %                         the labels classifyBranches shows where only the parity is exact
 %         parityColors .. [4 x 3] RGB, same indexing: the midpoint of the class's two
 %                         type colours (Asymm-class teal between blue and green,
 %                         Symm-class crimson between red and purple), so a parity-only
 %                         branch reads as "this family, Lamb/TH undecided"
 %
 % Indexed BY modesType directly -- typeColors(modesType, :) -- so a caller
 % never has to re-derive the mapping.
 %
 % Public rather than @Vis-private because the mapping is a presentation
 % convention shared with callers OUTSIDE visual/: a GUI showing "this mode is
 % Symm" beside a plot drawn here must use the same names and colours.
[hSaw, hAx] = Vis.plotAngularMap(sweepResult, plotType, hAx) 
% Plot SAW velocity vs. angle -- Cartesian (velocity) or polar (slowness)
 % >> [hSaw, hAx] = plotAngularMap(sweepResult, plotType, hAx)
 %
 % INPUT: 1. sweepResult .. CalcAngSweepHS or CalcAngSweepLayered result object,
 %                          already calc()'d -- .angles/.sawVelMap/.sawTypeMap
 %                          read directly (populated under both 'full' and
 %                          'ritzOnly' calcMode -- mode classification itself
 %                          doesn't need Green)
 %  (opt) 2. plotType    .. 'cartesian' (default, velocity vs angle) | 'polar'
 %                          (SLOWNESS = 1/velocity vs angle, NOT velocity — the
 %                          standard slowness-surface representation, and a real
 %                          behavioural difference between the two modes, not
 %                          just a coordinate-system swap)
 %  (opt) 3. hAx         .. target axes, created fresh if omitted — axes() for
 %                          cartesian, polaraxes() for polar. A caller passing
 %                          one in must supply the matching TYPE
 % OUTPUT: hSaw .. plot object handle for the SAW-velocity series: every SAW-type
 %                 point across every angle, flattened from the ragged
 %                 sawVelMap{iAngle} cell into one series
 %         hAx  .. the axes used
 %
 % Each angle's sawVelMap entry may be empty or hold several velocities, so the
 % flattening below never assumes one point per angle.
 %
 % Limiting-velocity reference lines are deliberately NOT drawn here —
 % plotClassificationDiagnostic is where they belong.
[hOut, hAx] = Vis.plotAssociationDiagnostic(association, hAx, overlayMode) 
% Plot every associated point's propagation angle vs. calculated velocity,
 % coloured by its achieved MAC value -- a mode-tracking debugging view, since
 % a collapsing MAC is the observable signature of a binding going bad. Points
 % with modeCollision=true are ringed in red.
 % >> [hOut, hAx] = Vis.plotAssociationDiagnostic(association, hAx, overlayMode)
 %
 % INPUT:  1. association .. ModeAssociation, after at least one rebind()
 %                          (e.g. inversion.association after calc())
 %  (opt)  2. hAx          .. axes handle (new figure if omitted)
 %  (opt)  3. overlayMode  .. false (default): standalone plot with the
 %                          Vis.ColormapRYG() colormap, a colorbar, labels and
 %                          title. true: markers ONLY, coloured by explicit
 %                          per-point RGB so the axes' colormap/CLim are left
 %                          untouched -- for overlaying on a background that owns
 %                          them, e.g. Vis.plotGreenMap, whose angle/velocity
 %                          axes this matches exactly
 % OUTPUT: hOut .. struct of the scatter handles actually drawn
 %         hAx  .. axes handle
 %
 % 'limiting'-class points are routed through Christoffel, not MAC-tracked
 % (sidestepping the mode-identity problem entirely), so their macValue is
 % always NaN -- and scatter silently DROPS NaN-coloured points. They are drawn
 % as a separate, labelled, colour-neutral series instead.
[hPoints, hAx, hBg, hLim] = Vis.plotClassificationCurve(singleResult, colorBy, showGreenBackground, refLevel, hAx, component) 
% Comparative mode-classification diagnostic for a SINGLE propagation
 % angle: classified points marked on the Green's-function curve, with a
 % Christoffel limiting-velocity reference line -- 1D analogue of
 % plotClassificationDiagnostic.m for CalcSingleHS/CalcSingleLayered
 % results (no angle axis to sweep).
 % >> [hPoints, hAx, hBg, hLim] = plotClassificationCurve(singleResult, colorBy, showGreenBackground, refLevel, hAx, component)
 %
 % INPUT: 1. singleResult         .. CalcSingleHS or CalcSingleLayered result
 %                                  object, calcAll()'d. Reads
 %                                  .classification.significantModes directly
 %                                  and never recomputes a parallel score
 %  (opt) 2. colorBy             .. 'type' (default) | 'score' | 'confidence',
 %                                  as in plotClassificationDiagnostic
 %  (opt) 3. showGreenBackground .. draw the response curve first (default true;
 %                                  skipped with a warning if the chosen curve's
 %                                  data is missing, e.g. a Green component on a
 %                                  'ritzOnly' result)
 %  (opt) 4. refLevel            .. how much classification detail to show
 %                                  (default 'full') -- the same 3 tiers
 %                                  plotClassificationDiagnostic documents:
 %                                  'sawOnly' | 'sawLimit' | 'full'
 %  (opt) 5. hAx                 .. target axes (created fresh if omitted)
 %  (opt) 6. component           .. response curve drawn and projected onto:
 %                                  'G13eff' (|G13_eff| -- the component the Green
 %                                  score reads, so points sit on the curve they
 %                                  were decided on; the default when the result
 %                                  has Green data) | any 'GIJ'/'GIJeff',
 %                                  recomputed on request (see
 %                                  Vis.greenComponentData) | 'ritzSpectrum'
 %                                  (plotRitzSpectrumCurve, |S(v)|; the default on
 %                                  a 'ritzOnly' result)
 % OUTPUT: hPoints .. colorBy='type': array of scatter handles, one per type
 %                    present (legend shown); 'score'/'confidence': a single
 %                    scatter handle (its own colour bar: UserData.colorbar)
 %         hAx     .. axes used
 %         hBg     .. the background curve's line handle, or [] if not drawn
 %         hLim    .. struct('long', <xline handle or []>, 'shear', <array of
 %                    xline handles or []>). Only ONE is ever populated: long
 %                    for HS, shear for layered
 %
 % Y-AXIS: velocity occupies the x-axis here (the swept-away dimension is
 % angle, not velocity), so each classified mode is marked at its own velocity
 % and projected onto the log-normalised Green curve's y-value there by
 % interpolation, reading as a conventional peak-picking plot. Falls back to a
 % fixed y=0 baseline when the chosen curve's data is missing, so classified points
 % stay visible rather than being suppressed for want of a curve to project onto.
 %
 % MARKER SIZE and GRADE: as plotClassificationDiagnostic -- size by score over the
 % method's range (Vis.classificationScoreScale), 'weak' entries open markers.
 %
 % 'score'/'confidence' colouring is `gray` unflipped — black low, white high —
 % on the points' own range and colour bar, with a dark outline, as in
 % plotClassificationDiagnostic.
[hPoints, hAx, hBg, hLim] = Vis.plotClassificationDiagnostic(sweepResult, colorBy, showGreenBackground, refLevel, hAx, component) 
% Comparative mode-classification diagnostic: classified points over the
 % Green map, with a Christoffel limiting-velocity reference line
 % >> [hPoints, hAx, hBg, hLim] = plotClassificationDiagnostic(sweepResult, colorBy, showGreenBackground, refLevel, hAx, component)
 %
 % INPUT: 1. sweepResult         .. CalcAngSweepHS or CalcAngSweepLayered result
 %                                  object, calc()'d. Reads .significantModesMap
 %                                  directly — the already-classified struct
 %                                  array per angle — and never recomputes a
 %                                  parallel score
 %  (opt) 2. colorBy             .. 'type' (default, categorical) | 'score'
 %                                  (continuous: the entry's score in dB, on its
 %                                  method's scale) | 'confidence' (continuous:
 %                                  abs(modeVel-greenPeakVel), the bound Ritz
 %                                  mode's distance from its Green peak; NaN
 %                                  where no Green match was made)
 %  (opt) 3. showGreenBackground .. draw the response background first (default
 %                                  true; skipped with a warning if the chosen
 %                                  background's data is missing, e.g. a Green
 %                                  component on a 'ritzOnly' result)
 %  (opt) 4. refLevel            .. how much classification detail to show
 %                                  (default 'full'), in 3 cumulative tiers.
 %                                  'other'-type points are never shown at any
 %                                  tier — this is a classification read-out, not
 %                                  a catalogue of every candidate considered.
 %                                    'sawOnly'  -- SAW-family points only
 %                                                  (Rayleigh/pseudoRayleigh/SAW),
 %                                                  no reference line
 %                                    'sawLimit' -- + ONE Christoffel reference
 %                                                  line, by family: longitudinal
 %                                                  for HS (Rayleigh is already
 %                                                  subsonic w.r.t. shear, so the
 %                                                  longitudinal bound is the
 %                                                  informative one pseudoRayleigh
 %                                                  approaches), substrate shear
 %                                                  for layered (the bound above
 %                                                  which the wave leaks into the
 %                                                  substrate)
 %                                    'full'     -- + 'limiting'-type points, the
 %                                                  Ritz modes sitting AT a
 %                                                  bulk-wave limiting velocity.
 %                                                  For layered results this is
 %                                                  what shows the substrate-shear
 %                                                  boundary as discrete data
 %  (opt) 5. hAx                 .. target axes (created fresh if omitted)
 %  (opt) 6. component           .. response background: 'G13eff' (|G13_eff|,
 %                                  what the Green score read; the default when
 %                                  the result has Green maps) | any
 %                                  'GIJ'/'GIJeff', recomputed on request
 %                                  (Vis.greenComponentData) | 'spot' (the stored
 %                                  calcSpotGreenMap result) | 'ritzSpectrum'
 %                                  (plotRitzSpectrumMap, |S(v)| from the stored
 %                                  modal numerators; the default on a 'ritzOnly'
 %                                  result, which has no Green map)
 % OUTPUT: hPoints .. colorBy='type': array of scatter handles, one per type
 %                    present (legend shown); 'score'/'confidence': a single
 %                    scatter handle (its own colour bar: UserData.colorbar)
 %         hAx     .. axes used
 %         hBg     .. the background's pcolor handle, or [] if not drawn
 %         hLim    .. struct('long', <handle or []>, 'shear', <handle or []>).
 %                    Only ONE is ever populated — long for HS, shear for layered
 %                    — returned as a struct so a caller can toggle either
 %                    without knowing which family sweepResult belongs to
 %
 % MARKER SIZE is scaled by each point's score: linear in the score (a log10
 % amplitude ratio, so in dB) over its method's range,
 % Vis.classificationScoreScale(...).sizeRange, clipped at both ends. Points with no
 % score (none on a current result apart from 'limiting') get a fixed size;
 % 'limiting' points a smaller fixed one.
 %
 % GRADE: entries graded 'weak' (accepted at the active, looser standard but not
 % at the method's 'low' one) are drawn as open markers, 'strong' ones filled.
 %
 % 'score'/'confidence' colouring is plain `gray`, which already maps low data to
 % black and high to white — NOT flipud(gray) — over the points' own range, with
 % its own colour bar right of the background's: the background keeps its
 % ColMapGrayOrange colormap and limits (explicit RGB points,
 % visClassificationScatterByScore). Every point has a dark outline, so white
 % points read against a white or bright background too.
 %
 % HS vs layered by class (visIsLayered); the per-angle limiting velocities are
 % read duck-typed -- see docs/reference/architecture/visual.md, "Duck-typing across
 % HS/Layered result classes".
[hLines, hAx, hBg] = Vis.plotDispersionCurves(sweepResult, curveType, angleIdx, showRitzBranches, showGreenBackground, scaleMode, climRange, hAx) 
% Plot fixed-angle dispersion curves for a CalcWavevecAngSweep2Layered
 % wavelength sweep -- one connected line per tracked physical branch,
 % optionally over a Green's-|G| background
 % >> [hLines, hAx, hBg] = Vis.plotDispersionCurves(sweepResult, curveType, angleIdx, showRitzBranches, showGreenBackground, scaleMode, climRange, hAx)
 %
 % INPUT: 1. sweepResult .. CalcWavevecAngSweep2Layered, already calc()'d,
 %                          with retentionLevel='full' --
 %                          DispersionBranches.track's own
 %                          requirement (this function calls it directly
 %                          and lets its errors propagate)
 %  (opt) 2. curveType    .. 'wavelength-freq' (default) | 'wavelength-vphase'
 %                          | 'k-freq' | 'k-vphase' | 'freq-vphase' | 'freq-vgroup'
 %  (opt) 3. angleIdx     .. which angle to plot the dispersion curve at
 %                          (default 1). sweepResult may carry more than one
 %                          angle, but this is always a SINGLE fixed-angle
 %                          curve set, never several at once
 %  (opt) 4. showRitzBranches .. draw the tracked branch lines (default true).
 %                          The lines are fully computed and returned in
 %                          hLines either way — false only sets Visible='off'
 %                          — so a caller can flip them back on without a
 %                          redraw, the same convention the GUI viewers'
 %                          per-type checkboxes use
 %  (opt) 5. showGreenBackground .. draw a Green's |G13_eff| heatmap behind the
 %                          lines (default false, unlike
 %                          plotClassificationDiagnostic's default-true:
 %                          Green data here needs calcMode='full' on the
 %                          WHOLE wavelength sweep, and only 2 of the 6
 %                          curveTypes can show it). Requires curveType
 %                          'wavelength-vphase' or 'k-vphase' (velocity
 %                          y-axis) and ERRORS otherwise, as it does when
 %                          sweepResult was calc()'d with calcMode='ritzOnly'
 %                          — an explicit opt-in cannot be silently ignored.
 %                          The 4 frequency-based curveTypes are excluded
 %                          because freq = vel/wavelength rescales the y-axis
 %                          per wavelength column, making the background a
 %                          non-rectangular grid. Both flags false is valid:
 %                          an empty, labelled axes
 %  (opt) 6. scaleMode    .. 'log' (default) | 'linear' -- same meaning as
 %                          plotGreenMap's; used only with the background
 %  (opt) 7. climRange    .. 2-element [lo hi], default [-4, -1], matching
 %                          plotGreenMap (the same |G13_eff| quantity, with
 %                          wavelength standing in for angle) rather than
 %                          plotDispersionMap's [-5, -2], which is tuned for
 %                          the isoFreq angle x kParRange family. Used only
 %                          with the background
 %  (opt) 8. hAx          .. target axes (created fresh if omitted)
 % OUTPUT: hLines .. [1 x nCompModes] line handles, one per tracked branch, each
 %                   tagged UserData.branchIdx for the GUI's click-to-select
 %                   convention (ButtonDownFcn is set by the caller, not here)
 %         hAx    .. axes used
 %         hBg    .. background pcolor handle, or [] if showGreenBackground was false
 %
 % Branch continuity comes from DispersionBranches.track.
 % Separate from plotDispersionMap.m rather than a case dispatched there:
 % plotDispersionMap's CalcWavevecAngSweep2Layered branch is a multi-angle 2D
 % scatter, this is a single-fixed-angle curve view — connected lines, a
 % structurally different rendering.
 %
 % Branches classified SAW/limiting at the FIRST wavelength step are drawn
 % highlighted (thicker, coloured), every other raw Ritz branch thin grey.
 % Highlighting by physical significance rather than by symmetric/antisymmetric
 % parity: a layered film-on-substrate structure has no such parity.
 %
 % freq = vel/wavelength, k = 2*pi/wavelength.
 %
 % GREEN BACKGROUND: |G13_eff| (greenAbsG13EffMap, the component the existence
 % score reads and the only one a result stores). Each sweeps{iw}
 % carries its own (greenVelAxis, greenAbsG13EffMap) under calcMode='full'. The axes normally coincide (the
 % substrate limiting velocities and vMax do not change with wavelength), but
 % nothing guarantees it, so plotWavevecGreenBackground interpolates every
 % column onto one shared velocity axis first, leaving NaN outside a
 % wavelength's own range, which pcolor draws blank.
[hLines, hAx, hChristLines] = Vis.plotDispersionCurvesPlate(sweepResult, curveType, angleIdx, colorMode, confidenceThresh, showChristoffelBounds, hAx) 
% Plot fixed-angle dispersion curves for a CalcWavevecAngSweep2Plate
 % wavevector sweep -- one connected line per tracked physical branch,
 % optionally with Christoffel bulk/limiting-velocity reference lines.
 % >> [hLines, hAx, hChristLines] = Vis.plotDispersionCurvesPlate(sweepResult, curveType, angleIdx, colorMode, confidenceThresh, showChristoffelBounds, hAx)
 %
 % Separate from Vis.plotDispersionCurves (the CalcWavevecAngSweep2Layered
 % analogue) rather than a class-dispatch branch inside it, the two cases
 % differing in more than cosmetics:
 %   - the swept axis is CalcWavevecAngSweep2Plate's own `wavVecs` directly, a
 %     real wavevector, not a wavelength converted to k; wavelength here is
 %     DERIVED as 2*pi./wavVecs, the reverse of the Layered function.
 %   - there is no "significant SAW/Sezawa mode" concept to highlight — every
 %     Plate mode is a genuine physical branch — so highlighting uses
 %     classifyBranches' mode-type labels, with its confidence-driven fallback
 %     to plain branch identity, instead of significantModesMap.
 %   - there is no Green's-function background: no analogue exists for a free
 %     plate, so there is no showGreenBackground parameter at all.
 %   - Christoffel bulk/limiting velocities stay meaningful without Green data,
 %     and appear here as optional reference lines.
 %
 % INPUT: 1. sweepResult .. CalcWavevecAngSweep2Plate, already calc()'d, with
 %                          retentionLevel='full' — classifyBranches' own
 %                          requirement, whose errors propagate from here
 %  (opt) 2. curveType    .. 'wavelength-freq' (default) | 'wavelength-vphase'
 %                          | 'k-freq' | 'k-vphase' | 'freq-vphase' |
 %                          'freq-vgroup' — the same 6 names
 %                          plotDispersionCurves uses. 'k' here IS
 %                          sweepResult.wavVecs, 'wavelength' = 2*pi./wavVecs,
 %                          and 'freq' is sweepResult.freqsPhysicalMap3D,
 %                          already in whatever real units realThickness/wavVecs
 %                          were given in and not re-derived here
 %  (opt) 3. angleIdx     .. which angle to plot at (default 1)
 %  (opt) 4. colorMode    .. 'type' (default) | 'branch'. 'type' colours each
 %                          branch by mode-type (Asymm/Symm/TH-Asymm/TH-Symm)
 %                          where classifyBranches trusts the label, by its
 %                          parity class (Vis.plateModeTypeStyle's parityColors,
 %                          dashed) where only the parity is exact, evaluated
 %                          at this curve's FIRST wavevector step — a
 %                          per-branch, not per-point decision, so a branch
 %                          whose confidence drops later keeps its first-step
 %                          colour. 'branch' colours by branch identity
 %                          (MATLAB `lines`, cyclic beyond 7), ignoring
 %                          classification: the escape hatch for a stack where
 %                          the labels are not trusted
 %  (opt) 5. confidenceThresh .. passed to classifyBranches (default 0.3, with
 %                          the same unvalidated-default caveat)
 %  (opt) 6. showChristoffelBounds .. default true when curveType has a velocity
 %                          y-axis, false otherwise — a phase-velocity bound has
 %                          no meaning on a frequency or group-velocity axis, so
 %                          it is silently not drawn rather than an error, this
 %                          being an additive overlay and not an explicit data
 %                          request. Draws the bottom-layer and, if different,
 %                          top-layer longitudinal and shear Christoffel
 %                          limiting velocities as horizontal lines, rotated to
 %                          sweepResult.angles(angleIdx). Both surfaces are
 %                          shown because at high kd an asymmetric stack's
 %                          surface modes decouple toward each surface's OWN
 %                          Rayleigh-adjacent velocity independently
 %  (opt) 7. hAx          .. target axes (created fresh if omitted)
 % OUTPUT: hLines       .. [1 x nCompModes] line handles, one per tracked branch,
 %                         each tagged UserData.branchIdx for click-to-select
 %         hAx          .. axes used
 %         hChristLines .. Christoffel reference-line handles (yline objects),
 %                         or empty gobjects(1,0) if not drawn
[hOut, hAx] = Vis.plotDispersionMap(sweepResult, omegaIdx, scaleMode, climRange, plotType, hAx) 
% Plot a 2D (or 1D) dispersion map -- angle x wavelength/kParRange/omega0
 % >> [hOut, hAx] = plotDispersionMap(sweepResult, omegaIdx, scaleMode, climRange, plotType, hAx)
 %
 % INPUT: 1. sweepResult .. one of four different result classes, dispatched
 %                          by isa() since their underlying data shapes are
 %                          genuinely different, not just differently-named
 %                          properties on the same shape (unlike
 %                          plotAngularMap/plotGreenMap's HS-vs-Layered
 %                          duck-typing):
 %           - CalcWavevecAngSweep2Layered (isoWavevec 2D: angle x wavelength)
 %             -> scatter of classified SAW points (angle, wavelength),
 %                coloured by LOCAL RANK within each point's own cluster.
 %                sawVelMap2D{iw,ia} is already velocity-sorted ascending, so
 %                rank is position in that array and no new sort is needed.
 %                These are ranks, NOT tracked branches — for connected branch
 %                lines use Vis.plotDispersionCurves. No Green magnitude here,
 %                so scaleMode/climRange/plotType are ignored: always a
 %                Cartesian scatter.
 %           - CalcFreqAngSweep2Layered (isoFreq 2D: angle x omega0, each
 %             omega0 its own angle x kParRange Green |G| heatmap slice, not
 %             eagerly aggregated) -> plots ONE selected omega0 slice, see
 %             omegaIdx
 %           - CalcIsofreqAngSweepLayered (isoFreq, one omega0: angle x
 %             kParRange) -> Green |G| heatmap, the same ColMapGrayOrange
 %             treatment plotGreenMap uses
 %           - CalcIsofreqSingleLayered (isoFreq, one angle, one omega0:
 %             kParRange only) -> 1D line, absG vs kParRange. No angle axis at
 %             all, so plotType is ignored: always a Cartesian line.
 %  (opt) 2. omegaIdx    .. which omega0 slice to plot -- ONLY used when
 %                          sweepResult is a CalcFreqAngSweep2Layered
 %                          (default 1); ignored otherwise
 %  (opt) 3. scaleMode    .. 'log' (default) | 'linear', as in plotGreenMap.
 %                          Applies to the three Green-magnitude cases;
 %                          ignored for CalcWavevecAngSweep2Layered, which has
 %                          no Green magnitude to scale. 'log' plots
 %                          log10(|G_33| / max|G_33|), normalised to this
 %                          plot's own peak so the display range is
 %                          material- and units-independent; 'linear' gives
 %                          plain |G_33|, which buries the peak's sharpness
 %                          against a much broader background
 %  (opt) 4. climRange    .. 2-element [lo hi]. Colour-axis limits for the 2D
 %                          heatmap cases (log10-normalised units when
 %                          scaleMode='log'), default [-5, -2]; ylim for the 1D
 %                          CalcIsofreqSingleLayered line, default [-5, 0] (the
 %                          normalised peak sits at 0, so the heatmap default
 %                          would cut it off). Ignored for 'linear' unless
 %                          given explicitly
 %  (opt) 5. plotType     .. 'cartesian' (default, angle x kParRange axes) |
 %                          'polar' (angle as azimuth, kParRange as radius).
 %                          Affects the two 2D heatmap cases only, delegating
 %                          to plotPolarHeatmap.m. sweepResult.angles is
 %                          plotted exactly as given, not mirrored to a full
 %                          circle
 %  (opt) 6. hAx          .. target axes (created fresh if omitted)
 % OUTPUT: hOut .. scatter/pcolor/line handle, whichever case applied
 %         hAx  .. axes used
 %
 % kParRange is plotted as given, not converted to a velocity axis.
 %
 % The climRange default here ([-5,-2]) is tuned independently of
 % plotGreenMap's ([-4,-1]) — they cover different scan types.
[hOut, hAx] = Vis.plotGreenCurve(singleResult, scaleMode, hAx, component) 
% Plot the Green's-function velocity curve -- |G| vs. velocity, for a
 % SINGLE propagation angle -- 1D analogue of plotGreenMap.m for
 % CalcSingleHS/CalcSingleLayered results (no angle axis to sweep).
 % >> [hOut, hAx] = plotGreenCurve(singleResult, scaleMode, hAx, component)
 %
 % INPUT: 1. singleResult .. CalcSingleHS or CalcSingleLayered result object,
 %                          calcAll()'d with calcMode='full' — the Green fields
 %                          stay empty under 'ritzOnly', which errors clearly
 %                          here. .greenVelAxis and the component's curve are
 %                          read directly, both classes naming them the same.
 %                          A sweep's maps are these same curves stacked per angle
 %  (opt) 2. scaleMode    .. 'log' (default) | 'linear' -- the same
 %                          normalisation plotGreenMap uses: 'log' plots
 %                          log10(|G| / max|G|), normalised to this curve's
 %                          own peak
 %  (opt) 3. hAx          .. target axes (created fresh if omitted)
 %  (opt) 4. component    .. 'G13eff' (default, the stored |G13_eff| -- the TGS
 %                          response the existence score reads) |
 %                          any 'GIJ' / 'GIJeff', e.g. 'G13' (one-sided G13_+),
 %                          'G33': recomputed for this call, not stored -- see
 %                          Vis.greenComponentData
 % OUTPUT: hOut .. line handle
 %         hAx  .. axes used
 %
 % No climRange/plotType/colorbar parameters: those are plotGreenMap's
 % heatmap-specific concerns, and a single-angle curve has no second axis to
 % spend colour on.
[hOut, hAx, hCb] = Vis.plotGreenMap(sweepResult, scaleMode, climRange, plotType, hAx, component) 
% Plot the Green's-function angular map -- |G| heatmap, angle x velocity
 % >> [hOut, hAx, hCb] = plotGreenMap(sweepResult, scaleMode, climRange, plotType, hAx, component)
 %
 % INPUT: 1. sweepResult .. CalcAngSweepHS or CalcAngSweepLayered result object,
 %                          calc()'d with calcMode='full' — the Green maps stay
 %                          empty under 'ritzOnly', which errors clearly here.
 %                          .angles/.greenVelAxis and the component's map are
 %                          read directly, both classes naming them the same
 %  (opt) 2. scaleMode    .. 'log' (default) | 'linear'. 'log' plots
 %                          log10(|G| / max|G|), normalised to the map's own
 %                          peak so the display range is material- and
 %                          units-independent and shows how sharp the SAW peak
 %                          is relative to the background rather than its
 %                          absolute magnitude
 %  (opt) 3. climRange    .. 2-element [lo hi] colour-axis limits. For 'log' this
 %                          is in log10(normalised) units, default [-4, -1], i.e.
 %                          1e-4x to 1e-1x of the peak; auto for 'linear' unless
 %                          given explicitly
 %  (opt) 4. plotType     .. 'cartesian' (default, angle x velocity axes) |
 %                          'polar' (angle as azimuth, velocity as radius,
 %                          delegating to plotPolarHeatmap.m).
 %                          sweepResult.angles is plotted exactly as given, not
 %                          mirrored to a full circle — a 0-180 deg sweep draws
 %                          a half-disc wedge
 %  (opt) 5. hAx          .. target axes (created fresh if omitted). ALWAYS a
 %                          plain axes(), never polaraxes(), for both plotTypes
 %  (opt) 6. component    .. 'G13eff' (default, the stored |G13_eff| map -- the
 %                          TGS response the existence score reads)
 %                          | any 'GIJ' / 'GIJeff', e.g. 'G13' (one-sided G13_+),
 %                          'G33': recomputed for this call (one Green solve per
 %                          angle), not stored -- see Vis.greenComponentData
 %                          | 'spot': the stored spot-averaged map (half-space result,
 %                          sweepResult.calcSpotGreenMap(kernel) first), drawn on
 %                          its own spotGreenVelAxis
 % OUTPUT: hOut .. pcolor object handle
 %         hAx  .. the axes used
 %         hCb  .. colorbar handle
 %
 % Every component is drawn on the same angles x greenVelAxis grid at the same
 % damping (SawSpectrum.ETA = 3e-3): the stored |G13_eff|, and any other
 % component recomputed on request (Vis.greenComponentData).
 % The axis starts at 0.3 km/s (SawSpectrum.VEL_MIN), so the lowest part is
 % usually empty; set ylim, or use the viewers' range fields.
 %
 % The Cartesian branch draws each sample CENTRED on its (angle, velocity) value
 % via Vis.pcolorCenteredGrid; plain pcolor(angles, vel, C) would draw angle(j)'s
 % column from angle(j) to angle(j+1) instead — see docs/pitfalls.md.
 %
 % Default colormap ColMapGrayOrange. CData holds real, finite numbers (zeros
 % floored to eps before log10); out-of-range values are clipped for DISPLAY
 % only, via clim().
[hOut, hAxCell] = Vis.plotGreenModalShape(greenObj, forceDir, cutoffParam, dirs, meshSpacing, hFig, hAxCellIn) 
% Plot a Green's-function response's signed 2D (depth x lateral) displacement
 % field -- Green's-response analogue of plotModalShape, for one already-
 % selected velocity and force direction.
 % >> [hOut, hAxCell] = plotGreenModalShape(greenObj, forceDir, cutoffParam, dirs, meshSpacing, hFig, hAxCellIn)
 %
 % INPUT: 1. greenObj    .. GreenSolver object, .dispField populated -- see
 %                          calcGreenDispField2D's own docblock (call
 %                          GreenSolver.calc with nargout>1 and a SCALAR
 %                          velocity first; half-space only)
 %        2. forceDir    .. which force direction's response to show (1-3)
 %  (opt) 3. cutoffParam .. decay threshold fraction, default 0.02: the display
 %                          truncates once the depth-profile magnitude drops
 %                          below this fraction of its value AT the surface.
 %                          Always a plain decay cutoff, unlike
 %                          plotModalShape's substrateCutoff — a Green's
 %                          response is ALWAYS a half-space, so there is no
 %                          film-stack choice to make and
 %                          calcSubstrateCutoffDepth's nLay>=2 guard does not
 %                          fit. The crop is applied here directly, but the
 %                          decay SCAN is the same visDecayCutoffIdx helper
 %  (opt) 4. dirs         .. which displacement directions to draw (default
 %                           1:3), or 'rgb' for the merged polarisation view
 %                           -- as plotModalShape's dirs
 %  (opt) 5. meshSpacing  .. lateral mesh spacing (default 0.01)
 %  (opt) 6. hFig         .. target figure (a classic figure() created fresh if
 %                           omitted, as in plotModalShape). IGNORED when
 %                           hAxCellIn is given
 %  (opt) 7. hAxCellIn    .. pre-built {numel(dirs) x 1} cell of axes/uiaxes to
 %                           render into -- same contract as plotModalShape's
 %                           hAxCellIn: skips hFig/clf/Position entirely, and
 %                           cla()'s and renders into each given handle.
 %                           With dirs='rgb': a {1 x 1} cell, no key drawn
 % OUTPUT: hOut    .. array of pcolor handles [numel(dirs) x 1] (dirs='rgb':
 %                    one image handle)
 %         hAxCell .. {numel(dirs) x 1} cell of axes handles ({1 x 1})
 %
 % Visual language matches plotModalShape (ColormapRWB, [-1,1] clim, thin black
 % frame, axis off, direction titles) so a Ritz modal shape and a Green's
 % response can sit side by side — both render each panel through the same
 % visModalShapePanel helper. Always exactly one "layer": Green's full output is
 % half-space only, so there is no multi-layer stacking to allocate.
[hOut, hAxCell] = Vis.plotGreenModalShapeLayered(greenObj, structure, forceDir, substrateCutoff, cutoffParam, dirs, meshSpacing, hFig, hAxCellIn) 
% Plot a LAYERED Green's-function response's signed 2D (depth x lateral)
 % displacement field, for one already-selected velocity and force
 % direction. Layered counterpart of the half-space-only plotGreenModalShape;
 % its multi-layer axis stacking mirrors plotModalShape.m instead.
 % >> [hOut, hAxCell] = plotGreenModalShapeLayered(greenObj, structure, forceDir, substrateCutoff, cutoffParam, dirs, meshSpacing, hFig, hAxCellIn)
 %
 % INPUT: 1. greenObj        .. GreenSolver object from GreenSolver.calc called
 %                              with nargout>1 and a SCALAR velocity on a
 %                              LAYERED structure -- see
 %                              calcGreenDispField2DLayered
 %        2. structure       .. the SAME Structure passed to GreenSolver.calc
 %        3. forceDir        .. which force direction's response to show (1-3)
 %  (opt) 4. substrateCutoff .. 'none' | 'decay' (default) | 'fixedMultiple' |
 %                              'boosted' -- see calcSubstrateCutoffDepth.m.
 %                              Defaults to 'decay', not plotModalShape's
 %                              'none': GreenSolver's substrate is analytically
 %                              semi-infinite, so its display extent is not a
 %                              physical "true scale" the way a Ritz substrate
 %                              truncation is, and shown in full it is usually
 %                              a mostly-empty panel
 %  (opt) 5. cutoffParam     .. threshold fraction (decay, default 0.05) or
 %                              multiple (fixedMultiple, default 4); ignored
 %                              for 'none'/'boosted'
 %  (opt) 6. dirs            .. which displacement directions to draw (default
 %                              1:3), or 'rgb' for the merged polarisation view
 %                              -- as plotModalShape's dirs
 %  (opt) 7. meshSpacing     .. depth/lateral mesh spacing passed to
 %                              calcGreenDispField2DLayered (default 0.01)
 %  (opt) 8. hFig            .. target figure (a classic figure() created fresh
 %                              if omitted). IGNORED when hAxCellIn is given
 %  (opt) 9. hAxCellIn       .. pre-built {numel(dirs) x nLay} cell of
 %                              axes/uiaxes to render into -- same contract as
 %                              plotModalShape's hAxCellIn ({1 x nLay} with
 %                              dirs='rgb')
 % OUTPUT: hOut    .. array of pcolor object handles [numel(dirs) x nLay]
 %                    (dirs='rgb': image handles [1 x nLay])
 %         hAxCell .. {numel(dirs) x nLay} cell of axes handles ({1 x nLay})
 %
 % Layer stacking as in plotModalShape.m: layer 1 (substrate) at the BOTTOM of
 % the figure, layer nLay (outermost film) at the TOP. Default colormap
 % ColormapRWB.
[hOut, hAx] = Vis.plotGroupVelSurface(mater, nTheta, nPhi, hFig) 
% 3D group-velocity ("ray") surface -- the direction of energy/signal
 % propagation, which in general deviates from the wave-normal direction
 % >> [hOut, hAx] = plotGroupVelSurface(mater, nTheta, nPhi, hFig)
 %
 % INPUT: 1. mater  .. Material object (elastic constants + density +
 %                     dirMat orientation) -- same convention as
 %                     plotVelSurface
 %  (opt) 2. nTheta .. polar-angle mesh resolution (default 61)
 %  (opt) 3. nPhi   .. azimuthal mesh resolution (default 121)
 %  (opt) 4. hFig   .. target figure (created fresh if omitted)
 % OUTPUT: hOut .. 1x3 array of surf handles [qL, qT1, qT2]
 %         hAx  .. 1x3 array of axes handles
 %
 % Same (theta,phi) full-sphere sweep and frame convention as plotVelSurface
 % (axis 3 = surface normal). Unlike plotVelSurface, the surface point at
 % (theta,phi) is NOT dirVec scaled by a scalar: it is the actual group-velocity
 % VECTOR Christoffel.groupVelVectorsForDir returns, which can point in a
 % genuinely different direction from the wave normal that produced it. A
 % self-intersecting, "multiply-folded" surface is therefore real physics, not a
 % plotting bug (Every et al. 2013, Wave Motion 50, Sec. 2.3).
 %
 % For a 2D planar cut (e.g. the sagittal plane), pass this function's returned
 % surf handles' XData/YData/ZData to extractPlanarCut.m. Computing group
 % velocity over the WHOLE sphere and then filtering is necessary here, unlike
 % plotVelSurface, where restricting the sweep to one plane from the start
 % already gives the right answer.
[hOut, hAx] = Vis.plotInversionFit(measured, associationFinal, associationInitial, hAx) 
% Plot every stage of the inversion pipeline against propagation angle, on
 % one axes: which measured points were associated vs. dropped, their
 % initial-guess calculated velocity (optional), and their final/recovered
 % calculated velocity.
 % >> [hOut, hAx] = Vis.plotInversionFit(measured, associationFinal, associationInitial, hAx)
 %
 % INPUT:  1. measured           .. MeasuredVelocities, the FULL dataset -- including
 %                                 any point ModeAssociation.fromSweep dropped (the
 %                                 "N of M measured point(s) had no matching mode
 %                                 within tolerance" warning)
 %         2. associationFinal   .. ModeAssociation to plot as "recovered", e.g.
 %                                 inversion.association AFTER inversion.calc()
 %  (opt)  3. associationInitial .. an EARLIER ModeAssociation snapshot of the same
 %                                 points (e.g. the variable Inv.association(...)
 %                                 returned BEFORE calc() ran) to overlay as the
 %                                 initial-guess fit. [] or omitted to skip that series.
 %                                 Works because ModeAssociation is a value class: the
 %                                 variable you built the ElasticityInversion FROM is
 %                                 never touched by calc() -- only the instance's own
 %                                 copy (inversion.association) is
 %  (opt)  4. hAx                .. axes handle (new figure if omitted)
 % OUTPUT: hOut .. struct of the scatter handles actually drawn (fields depend on
 %                 which binding classes and series are present)
 %         hAx  .. axes handle
 %
 % A measured point counts as "associated" if associationFinal contains a point at
 % the same angle with the same measuredVelocity (both are copied verbatim from
 % `measured` by ModeAssociation.fromSweep and never altered by rebind, so this match
 % is exact, not a tolerance guess). Points failing that match were dropped -- either
 % no candidate of the right wave type existed, or none was within velocityTolerance.
[hOut, hAxCell] = Vis.plotModalShape(ritzSolver, modeNo, substrateCutoff, cutoffParam, dirs, meshSpacing, hFig, hAxCellIn) 
% Plot a Ritz mode's signed 2D (depth x lateral) displacement field
 % >> [hOut, hAxCell] = plotModalShape(ritzSolver, modeNo, substrateCutoff, cutoffParam, dirs, meshSpacing, hFig, hAxCellIn)
 %
 % INPUT: 1. ritzSolver      .. RitzSolver (or subclass) object, eigVecs_con/
 %                              nullConstrMat already populated for modeNo. There
 %                              is no retention-aware recompute-on-demand: this
 %                              errors if eigVecs_con lacks the column, rather
 %                              than silently doing the wrong thing
 %        2. modeNo          .. which mode to plot
 %  (opt) 3. substrateCutoff .. 'none' (default, true-scale) | 'decay' |
 %                              'fixedMultiple' -- see calcSubstrateCutoffDepth.m
 %                              -- | 'boosted': no cropping at all (the full
 %                              substrate depth is always rendered), but row
 %                              heights are NOT plain-proportional to relDepth
 %                              either -- see calcModalShapeRowHeights.m. Use
 %                              this when a thin film next to a deep substrate
 %                              needs to stay visible without losing any of
 %                              the substrate's own displayed data.
 %  (opt) 4. cutoffParam     .. threshold fraction (decay, default 0.05) or
 %                              multiple (fixedMultiple, default 4); ignored
 %                              for 'none'/'boosted'
 %  (opt) 5. dirs            .. which displacement directions to draw (default
 %                              1:3), or 'rgb': one merged panel per layer,
 %                              coloured by polarisation direction with
 %                              brightness = |u| (Vis.calcPolarisationRgb), plus
 %                              the colour key (Vis.plotPolarisationKey) when
 %                              drawing into a fresh figure
 %  (opt) 6. meshSpacing     .. depth/lateral mesh spacing passed to
 %                              calcDispField2D (default 0.01 — 201x201 points
 %                              spanning the FULL relDepth range of the deepest
 %                              layer). A substrateCutoff/ylim zoom into a
 %                              shallow sliver of that range only sees the points
 %                              falling inside it, so a zoomed-in caller usually
 %                              wants this finer than the default
 %  (opt) 7. hFig            .. target figure (a classic figure() created fresh if
 %                              omitted — classic figure/axes, not uifigure/
 %                              uiaxes, this being general-purpose plotting
 %                              output rather than a GUI window). IGNORED when
 %                              hAxCellIn is given
 %  (opt) 8. hAxCellIn       .. pre-built {numel(dirs) x nLay} cell of axes/uiaxes
 %                              to render into. A caller building a combined
 %                              uifigure needs this: classic axes() cannot be
 %                              parented inside a uifigure at all. When given,
 %                              hFig/clf are skipped and each axes is cla()'d and
 %                              rendered into directly, with no Position
 %                              assignment — sizing and placement stay the
 %                              caller's responsibility. With dirs='rgb': a
 %                              {1 x nLay} cell, and no key is drawn
 % OUTPUT: hOut    .. array of pcolor object handles [numel(dirs) x nLay]
 %                    (dirs='rgb': image handles [1 x nLay])
 %         hAxCell .. {numel(dirs) x nLay} cell of axes handles ({1 x nLay})
 %
 % Layer stacking: layer 1 (substrate) at the BOTTOM of the figure (deepest),
 % layer nLay (outermost film, nearest the free surface) at the TOP — matching
 % RitzSolver.calcDisplacement's own row order, where ilay loops 1:nLay filling
 % rows bottom-up and row "end" of the LAST layer is the true free surface.
 % Columns are the requested directions. Default colormap ColormapRWB.
[hOut, hAx] = Vis.plotPolarHeatmap(angles, radius, C, hAx) 
% Draw a 2D angle x radius heatmap in polar form, on ordinary Cartesian axes
 % >> [hOut, hAx] = plotPolarHeatmap(angles, radius, C, hAx)
 %
 % INPUT: 1. angles .. [nAngles x 1] angles [deg] -- plotted exactly as
 %                     given, NOT mirrored/extended to a full circle. Pass
 %                     a 0-360 deg array directly if a full disc is wanted;
 %                     a 0-180 deg array draws a half-disc wedge.
 %        2. radius  .. [nRadius x 1] radial axis (e.g. velocity, kParRange)
 %        3. C       .. [nRadius x nAngles] colour data -- already scaled
 %                     (log-normalised or not); this function does no
 %                     scaling itself, it only draws
 %  (opt) 4. hAx      .. target axes (created fresh if omitted) -- MUST be
 %                     plain axes(), not polaraxes(): the latter cannot host
 %                     pcolor/surf in any current MATLAB release (see
 %                     docs/reference/architecture/visual.md).
 % OUTPUT: hOut .. pcolor handle
 %         hAx  .. axes used (axis 'equal' and 'off' -- the hand-drawn grid
 %                 replaces the default x/y ticks)
 %
 % Colour scaling (log/linear), colormap, clim and colorbar are left to the
 % CALLER: this replaces only the pcolor + axis-decoration half of a Cartesian
 % call, so both display modes get identical colour treatment.
 %
 % Generic over any (angle, radius, colour) map, not Green-function-specific.
[hPatch, hAx] = Vis.plotPolarisationKey(hAx, nSub) 
% Colour key of the polarisation-RGB displacement view: the positive octant of
 % the unit sphere of displacement directions
 % >> [hPatch, hAx] = Vis.plotPolarisationKey(hAx, nSub)
 %
 % INPUT: (opt) 1. hAx  .. target axes/uiaxes (a new classic figure and axes if
 %                         omitted or empty); cla()'d first
 %        (opt) 2. nSub .. subdivisions per octant edge (default 40)
 % OUTPUT: hPatch .. the patch object of the octant
 %         hAx    .. the axes drawn into
 %
 % Each point of the octant is a unit direction p = (p1, p2, p3), p_i >= 0,
 % coloured p1*red + p2*blue + p3*green at full amplitude (the base colours of
 % visPolarisationColours) -- exactly the hue Vis.calcPolarisationRgb gives a
 % displacement in that direction. The octant
 % is viewed along (1,1,1), oriented as in the UTGS paper's key: u3
 % (out-of-plane, green) up, u2 (in-plane transverse, blue) lower left, u1
 % (propagation, red) lower right. Lower amplitude fades the same hue towards
 % white; the key shows the saturated end only.
[hOut, hAx] = Vis.plotRitzModeCurve(singleResult, criterion, velCutoffFactor, hAx) 
% Plot every computed Ritz mode (not just ModeClassification's
 % significant-mode subset) as a velocity x criterion curve, for a SINGLE
 % propagation angle -- 1D analogue of plotRitzModeMap.m for CalcSingleHS/
 % CalcSingleLayered results (no angle axis to sweep).
 % >> [hOut, hAx] = plotRitzModeCurve(singleResult, criterion, velCutoffFactor, hAx)
 %
 % INPUT: 1. singleResult     .. CalcSingleHS or CalcSingleLayered result
 %                              object, calcAll()'d. Reads .ritzSolver directly
 %                              (vels/nCompModes/diagnostics), so it works at any
 %                              retentionLevel
 %  (opt) 2. criterion        .. 'ritzScore' (default) | 'freqDisp' |
 %                              'depthInsensitivity', the same choices plotRitzModeMap
 %                              offers (see its docblock). 'freqDisp' on a
 %                              half-space also draws the veto level
 %                              (RitzScore.MIN_FREQDISP) as a dashed red line;
 %                              'ritzScore' draws the scored modes as a line with
 %                              small markers and the modes supporting no
 %                              candidate as faint grey dots on a baseline below
 %  (opt) 3. velCutoffFactor  .. upper velocity cutoff, as a multiple of
 %                              singleResult.longLimVel (default 1.10) — as in
 %                              plotRitzModeMap, but against a scalar limiting
 %                              velocity instead of a per-angle map. Pass Inf
 %                              to disable ([] = the default)
 %  (opt) 4. hAx              .. target axes (created fresh if omitted)
 % OUTPUT: hOut .. line handle; 'ritzScore': [scored, faint] (two line handles,
 %                 the faint one markers only)
 %         hAx  .. axes used
 %
 % A line rather than plotRitzModeMap's scatter, whose colour encodes the
 % criterion over a 2D (angle, velocity) domain. Points are sorted ascending
 % by velocity first: plot() draws strictly in array order, so unsorted points
 % would zig-zag between Ritz modes instead of tracing one curve across the
 % velocity axis.
[hOut, hAx] = Vis.plotRitzModeCurvePlate(singleResult, criterion, velCutoffFactor, hAx) 
% Plot every computed Ritz mode for a CalcSinglePlate result as a
 % velocity x criterion scatter -- Plate analogue of plotRitzModeCurve.m
 % (1D, single propagation angle, no angle axis to sweep).
 % >> [hOut, hAx] = Vis.plotRitzModeCurvePlate(singleResult, criterion, velCutoffFactor, hAx)
 %
 % Separate from plotRitzModeCurve rather than a class-dispatch branch inside
 % it: the only Plate mode-quality concept is mode-TYPE classification
 % (RitzSolverLamb.modesType/modeTypeConfidence), SAW's
 % diagnostics.depthInsensitivity/freqDisp having no Plate analogue, and there is
 % no Green's-function curve option at all.
 %
 % INPUT: 1. singleResult     .. CalcSinglePlate result object, calc()'d. Reads
 %                              .ritzSolver directly (vels/nCompModes/
 %                              modesType/modeTypeConfidence), so it works at
 %                              any retentionLevel
 %  (opt) 2. criterion        .. 'modeType' (default) | 'confidence'.
 %                              'modeType' puts the categorical mode type on
 %                              the y-axis (Asymm/Symm/TH-Asymm/TH-Symm, ticks
 %                              labelled accordingly): which family this mode
 %                              belongs to at this velocity. 'confidence' puts
 %                              modeTypeConfidence there, in [0,1]: how much
 %                              that label should be trusted. Marker colour
 %                              always encodes mode type either way, so a
 %                              confidence view stays linked to which family
 %                              each point belongs to
 %  (opt) 3. velCutoffFactor  .. upper velocity cutoff, as a multiple of the
 %                              structure's bottom-layer (and, if different,
 %                              top-layer) Christoffel longitudinal limiting
 %                              velocity (default 1.10). Computed fresh here,
 %                              since CalcSinglePlate has no stored longLimVel.
 %                              Pass Inf to disable ([] = the default)
 %  (opt) 4. hAx              .. target axes (created fresh if omitted)
 % OUTPUT: hOut .. scatter handle
 %         hAx  .. axes used
[hOut, hAx] = Vis.plotRitzModeMap(sweepResult, criterion, velCutoffFactor, hAx) 
% Plot every computed Ritz mode -- not just ModeClassification's
 % significant-mode subset -- as an angle x velocity scatter, shaded by a
 % mode-recognition criterion.
 % >> [hOut, hAx] = plotRitzModeMap(sweepResult, criterion, velCutoffFactor, hAx)
 %
 % INPUT: 1. sweepResult      .. CalcAngSweepHS or CalcAngSweepLayered result
 %                              object, calc()'d. Reads .cs{iAngle}.ritzSolver
 %                              directly (vels/nCompModes/diagnostics), so it
 %                              works at any retentionLevel: pruneEigVecs only
 %                              ever touches eigVecs_con
 %  (opt) 2. criterion        .. 'ritzScore' (default, as in the viewers) |
 %                              'freqDisp' | 'depthInsensitivity':
 %                                'ritzScore' -- the Ritz rule's own value: each
 %                                  mode gets the score (dB) of the Ritz candidate
 %                                  it supports (Vis.ritzModeScores); modes that
 %                                  support none are drawn faint
 %                                'freqDisp' -- the half-space veto quantity; the
 %                                  colour scale is fixed to [0 1] and shades the
 %                                  vetoed range below RitzScore.MIN_FREQDISP red
 %                                  (half-space only: not used on layered stacks,
 %                                  where the film makes true guided modes
 %                                  dispersive, so no veto is marked there)
 %                                'depthInsensitivity' -- how little a mode's
 %                                  velocity changes with the Ritz domain depth
 %                                  (log10 1/|dv/dD|): large for surface modes
 %                              freqDisp / depthInsensitivity are read straight
 %                              off ritzSolver.diagnostics, never recomputed here
 %  (opt) 3. velCutoffFactor  .. upper velocity cutoff, as a multiple of the
 %                              sweep's max longitudinal limiting velocity
 %                              (default 1.10). Drops the spurious
 %                              high-frequency Ritz modes that otherwise
 %                              dominate the velocity axis. Pass Inf to
 %                              disable ([] = the default)
 %  (opt) 4. hAx              .. target axes (created fresh if omitted)
 % OUTPUT: hOut .. scatter handle; 'ritzScore': [scored, faint] (two scatter
 %                 handles, the faint one empty-but-valid if every mode is scored)
 %         hAx  .. axes used
 %
 % Points are sorted ascending by criterion value before scattering so the
 % highest-scoring ones draw last, on top: scatter() has no explicit z-order
 % control, and a handful of bright points would otherwise be buried under a sea
 % of low-score ones.
 %
 % Black axes background with an unflipped `gray` colormap: low values render
 % dark-on-dark, high values stand out bright white (freqDisp: the vetoed range in
 % red shades, see visRitzCriterionColour).
 %
 % An exploratory diagnostic over the WHOLE Ritz spectrum, not a
 % results-presentation plot -- plotClassificationDiagnostic is the
 % classified-modes-only equivalent.
[hOut, hAx] = Vis.plotRitzModeMapPlate(sweepResult, criterion, velCutoffFactor, hAx) 
% Plot every computed Ritz mode for a CalcAngSweepPlate result as an
 % angle x velocity scatter, shaded by mode-type classification -- Plate
 % analogue of plotRitzModeMap.m.
 % >> [hOut, hAx] = Vis.plotRitzModeMapPlate(sweepResult, criterion, velCutoffFactor, hAx)
 %
 % Separate from plotRitzModeMap rather than a class-dispatch branch inside it:
 % mode-TYPE classification (RitzSolverLamb.modesType/modeTypeConfidence)
 % replaces SAW's diagnostics criteria, and there is no Green's-function map
 % option at all.
 %
 % INPUT: 1. sweepResult      .. CalcAngSweepPlate result object, calc()'d.
 %                              Reads .cs{iAngle}.ritzSolver directly
 %                              (vels/nCompModes/modesType/modeTypeConfidence),
 %                              so it works at any retentionLevel
 %  (opt) 2. criterion        .. 'modeType' (default) | 'confidence'. Both drive
 %                              marker colour directly, x/y already being
 %                              angle/velocity: 'modeType' uses the same
 %                              4-colour map plotDispersionCurvesPlate uses with
 %                              a 4-entry legend, a discrete classification
 %                              having no natural colour bar; 'confidence' uses a
 %                              continuous `gray` colormap plus colour bar, as
 %                              plotRitzModeMap does
 %  (opt) 3. velCutoffFactor  .. upper velocity cutoff, as a multiple of the
 %                              MAXIMUM over every angle of the structure's
 %                              bottom-layer (and, if different, top-layer)
 %                              Christoffel longitudinal limiting velocity
 %                              (default 1.10). Computed fresh here, since
 %                              CalcAngSweepPlate has no stored longVelMap.
 %                              Pass Inf to disable ([] = the default)
 %  (opt) 4. hAx              .. target axes (created fresh if omitted)
 % OUTPUT: hOut .. scatter handle (the real data; legend/colorbar proxies are
 %                 separate handles, not returned)
 %         hAx  .. axes used
 %
 % Points are sorted so 'modeType' draws in a fixed, deterministic order and
