 % 'confidence' draws highest-confidence last, on top.
[hOut, hAx, spec] = Vis.plotRitzSpectrumCurve(singleResult, scaleMode, hAx, showCands) 
% Plot the Ritz spectrum |S(v)| of one propagation angle -- the Ritz-side counterpart
 % of plotGreenCurve's |G13_eff|, rebuilt from the stored modal numerators (no solve)
 % >> [hOut, hAx, spec] = plotRitzSpectrumCurve(singleResult)
 % >> [hOut, hAx, spec] = plotRitzSpectrumCurve(singleResult, scaleMode, hAx, showCands)
 %
 % INPUT: 1. singleResult .. CalcSingleHS or CalcSingleLayered, calcAll()'d (either
 %                          calcMode: the Ritz candidates are stored on both)
 %  (opt) 2. scaleMode    .. 'log' (default) | 'linear' -- the normalisation of
 %                          plotGreenCurve (log10, normalised to this curve's peak)
 %  (opt) 3. hAx          .. target axes (created fresh if omitted)
 %  (opt) 4. showCands    .. false (default, as plotRitzSpectrumMap) | true: mark the
 %                          Ritz candidates -- down-triangles at the modal-sum peaks,
 %                          circles at their supporting modes
 % OUTPUT: hOut .. line handle
 %         hAx  .. axes used
 %         spec .. struct: velAxis, absS (|S| on velAxis), cands (ritzScoreCands), so a
 %                 caller can reuse the spectrum without rebuilding it
 %
 % S(v) = sum_n u3_n conj(u1_n) / (v_n^2 - v^2 (1 + i ETA)) (RitzScore.spectrum), the
 % G13-like modal sum the Ritz score is built on, on the result's Ritz score axis
 % (ritzScoreVelAxis). Errors Vis:plotRitzSpectrumCurve:noRitzSpectrum without stored
 % numerators.
[hOut, hAx, hCb] = Vis.plotRitzSpectrumMap(sweepResult, scaleMode, climRange, plotType, hAx, showCands) 
% Angle x velocity map of the Ritz spectrum |S(v)| -- the Ritz-side counterpart of
 % plotGreenMap's |G13_eff| map, rebuilt from the stored modal numerators (no solve)
 % >> [hOut, hAx, hCb] = plotRitzSpectrumMap(sweepResult)
 % >> [hOut, hAx, hCb] = plotRitzSpectrumMap(sweepResult, scaleMode, climRange, plotType, hAx, showCands)
 %
 % INPUT: 1. sweepResult .. CalcAngSweepHS or CalcAngSweepLayered, calc()'d (either
 %                         calcMode)
 %  (opt) 2. scaleMode   .. 'log' (default) | 'linear', as plotGreenMap
 %  (opt) 3. climRange   .. colour limits in the log scale (default [-4, -1], as
 %                         plotGreenMap)
 %  (opt) 4. plotType    .. 'cartesian' (default and only value; the slot keeps the
 %                         argument order of plotGreenMap, so hAx is 5th in both)
 %  (opt) 5. hAx         .. target axes (created fresh if omitted)
 %  (opt) 6. showCands   .. false (default, as plotRitzSpectrumCurve) | true: mark
 %                         every angle's Ritz candidates at their supporting modes'
 %                         velocities
 % OUTPUT: hOut .. the map's surface handle
 %         hAx  .. axes used
 %         hCb  .. colour bar
 %
 % Each angle's S(v) (RitzScore.spectrum) is evaluated on one common axis (the union
 % of the angles' Ritz score axes, RitzScore.VEL_STEP), normalised to the map's maximum
 % like plotGreenMap. The default background of a 'ritzOnly' result, which has no
 % Green map.
[hBar, hAx, hHeat] = Vis.plotSensitivitySpectrum(sensitivityAnalysis, hFig) 
% Plot the sensitivity spectrum: normalised sqrt(eigenvalue) bars (the
 % error-amplification factor of each transformed combination relative to
 % the best-determined one) plus the SIGNED transformation matrix as a
 % labelled heat-map, in the form of Grabec et al. 2024's Tables A.1-A.4.
 % >> [hBar, hAx, hHeat] = Vis.plotSensitivitySpectrum(sensitivityAnalysis, hFig)
 %
 % INPUT:  1. sensitivityAnalysis .. a computed SensitivityAnalysis object
 %  (opt)  2. hFig                .. figure handle (new figure if omitted)
 % OUTPUT: hBar  .. bar-chart handle (normalisedSensitivityRoots, log scale)
 %         hAx   .. [1x2] axes handles [barAxes, heatAxes]
 %         hHeat .. heat-map image handle (SIGNED transformMatrix)
 %
 % Bars at/above sensitivityCutoff (the optimised combinations) are shown in
 % the default colour; bars below it (frozen) are greyed out.
 %
 % The heat-map plots transformMatrix SIGNED, not abs(): the sign is what
 % tells, e.g. for cubic symmetry, a longitudinal-like "c11+c12" combination
 % (same sign on both) from a shear-like "c11-c12" one (opposite signs).
 % Diverging Vis.ColormapRWB, symmetric about zero so white always means zero,
 % as for other signed fields (e.g. displacement modal shapes).
[hOut, hAx] = Vis.plotVelCurve(mater, angleDeg, hAx) 
% Bulk-wave phase velocity vs. in-plane angle, at the fixed surface normal in mater.dirMat
 % >> [hOut, hAx] = plotVelCurve(mater, angleDeg, hAx)
 %
 % INPUT: 1. mater    .. Material object (elastic constants + density + dirMat
 %                       orientation) -- a pure bulk-wave/Christoffel quantity,
 %                       no Ritz/Green/ModeClassification dependency
 %  (opt) 2. angleDeg .. in-plane rotation angles [deg] (default 0:1:360, i.e.
 %                       a full circle at 1 deg resolution)
 %  (opt) 3. hAx       .. target axes, created fresh if omitted
 % OUTPUT: hOut .. 1x3 array of line handles [qL, qT1, qT2]
 %         hAx  .. axes used
 %
 % The 2D-curve analogue of Vis.plotVelSurface: that sweeps the full sphere,
 % this sweeps one great circle -- the in-plane rotation of mater.dirMat about
 % its fixed surface normal (dirMat row 1), the same angle convention every
 % other angular sweep in this project uses (Christoffel.phaseVelScan,
 % CalcAngSweepHS/Layered, Vis.plotAngularMap).
[hOut, hAx] = Vis.plotVelSurface(mater, surfaceType, nTheta, nPhi, hFig) 
% 3D directional bulk-wave velocity (or slowness) surfaces
 % >> [hOut, hAx] = plotVelSurface(mater, surfaceType, nTheta, nPhi, hFig)
 %
 % INPUT: 1. mater       .. Material object (elastic constants + density +
 %                          dirMat orientation) -- a pure bulk-wave/Christoffel
 %                          quantity, no Ritz/Green/ModeClassification
 %                          dependency, so this takes a Material directly
 %                          rather than a Structure/CalcSingle* wrapper
 %  (opt) 2. surfaceType .. 'velocity' (default) | 'slowness' (= 1/velocity,
 %                          computed slot-wise, i.e. per wave-type branch,
 %                          not re-sorted after inversion)
 %  (opt) 3. nTheta      .. polar-angle mesh resolution (default 61)
 %  (opt) 4. nPhi        .. azimuthal mesh resolution (default 121)
 %  (opt) 5. hFig        .. target figure (created fresh if omitted)
 % OUTPUT: hOut .. 1x3 array of surf handles [qL, qT1, qT2]
 %         hAx  .. 1x3 array of axes handles
 %
 % The tensor is taken in the standard frame used throughout this project
 % (Ritz/Christoffel/GreenSolver): axis 1 = dirMat's in-plane propagation
 % reference (dirMat row 2), axis 2 = the remaining in-plane direction (dirMat
 % row 3), axis 3 = the SURFACE NORMAL (dirMat row 1). A full spherical sweep
 % covers the whole sphere in whatever frame elTensor is expressed in, but the
 % axis LABELS below name them correctly so the plot reads physically.
 %
 % Group-velocity surfaces are Vis.plotGroupVelSurface's job: there the plotted
 % point is a genuine vector that need not lie along the wave normal.
[hOut, hAx] = Vis.plotYoungsCurve(mater, angleDeg, hAx) 
% Directional Young's modulus vs. in-plane angle, at the fixed surface normal
 % in mater.dirMat
 % >> [hOut, hAx] = plotYoungsCurve(mater, angleDeg, hAx)
 %
 % INPUT: 1. mater    .. Material object (elastic constants + density + dirMat
 %                       orientation)
 %  (opt) 2. angleDeg .. in-plane rotation angles [deg] (default 0:1:360, i.e.
 %                       a full circle at 1 deg resolution)
 %  (opt) 3. hAx       .. target axes, created fresh if omitted
 % OUTPUT: hOut .. line handle
 %         hAx  .. axes used
 %
 % The 2D-curve analogue of Vis.plotYoungsSurface (which sweeps the full
 % sphere): this sweeps one great circle -- the in-plane rotation of
 % mater.dirMat about its fixed surface normal (dirMat row 1), the same angle
 % convention Vis.plotVelCurve/Christoffel.phaseVelScan use for bulk waves.
 % mater.GetRotMat(angleDeg) already returns the 3x2 [propagation|normal]
 % format ElastConsts.Tensor2Youngs expects directly.
[hOut, hAx] = Vis.plotYoungsSurface(mater, nTheta, nPhi, hFig) 
% 3D directional Young's-modulus surface
 % >> [hOut, hAx] = plotYoungsSurface(mater, nTheta, nPhi, hFig)
 %
 % INPUT: 1. mater  .. Material object (elastic constants + density + dirMat
 %                     orientation)
 %  (opt) 2. nTheta .. polar-angle mesh resolution (default 61)
 %  (opt) 3. nPhi   .. azimuthal mesh resolution (default 121)
 %  (opt) 4. hFig   .. target figure (created fresh if omitted)
 % OUTPUT: hOut .. surf handle
 %         hAx  .. axes used
 %
 % Single-panel analogue of Vis.plotVelSurface: Young's modulus is one scalar
 % field (not three wave-type sheets), so this does not route through
 % visWaveSurfacePanels -- the plotted point is the unit direction scaled by
 % E(direction), same "radius = scalar modulus" convention as plotVelSurface's
 % velocity sheets.
 %
 % The tensor is taken in the standard frame used throughout this project
 % (Ritz/Christoffel/GreenSolver): axis 1 = dirMat's in-plane propagation
 % reference (dirMat row 2), axis 2 = the remaining in-plane direction (dirMat
 % row 3), axis 3 = the SURFACE NORMAL (dirMat row 1).
scores = Vis.ritzModeScores(singleResult) 
% Per-mode Ritz score: each Ritz mode gets the score of the Ritz candidate it supports
 % >> scores = Vis.ritzModeScores(singleResult)
 %
 % INPUT:  1. singleResult .. CalcSingleHS or CalcSingleLayered, calcAll()'d (either
 %                           calcMode: the Ritz candidates are stored on both)
 % OUTPUT: scores .. [nCompModes x 1] the Ritz rule's score (log10; x 20 for dB) of
 %                   the candidate whose supporting mode (RitzScore.localMode) is
 %                   this mode -- RitzScore.candidateScore (half-space dScore,
 %                   layered dScore + polTerm), the value the rule thresholds. The highest one where a mode
 %                   supports several candidates; NaN where it supports none.
 %
 % Every scored candidate counts, accepted or not (a vetoed or gated candidate still
 % has its score), so the view shows where the rule looked, not only what it kept.
 % The criterion 'ritzScore' of Vis.plotRitzModeMap / plotRitzModeCurve.
[plotData, dataLabel] = Vis.scaleGreenData(absG, scaleMode, componentLabel) 
% Apply the shared Green's-magnitude display scaling, with its axis label
 % >> [plotData, dataLabel] = Vis.scaleGreenData(absG, scaleMode, componentLabel)
 %
 % INPUT: 1. absG           .. |G| data of any shape (curve, map, or a
 %                             partially-NaN interpolated map -- see below)
 %        2. scaleMode      .. 'log' | 'linear'
 %        3. componentLabel .. TeX label for the quantity absG holds, used
 %                             verbatim in dataLabel, e.g. 'G_{13}^{eff}' |
 %                             'G_{33}' | 'S' (the Ritz spectrum); see
 %                             Vis.greenComponentData. Required: the label must
 %                             name the data the caller passes
 % OUTPUT: plotData  .. the values to plot/colour by, double (the Calc classes
 %                      store |G| in single precision; plotting and interpolating
 %                      are done in double)
 %         dataLabel .. matching colorbar/axis label string
 %
 % 'log' plots log10(|G| / max|G|) -- normalised to THIS data's own
 % peak first, so the display range is material- and units-independent: it
 % shows how sharp the SAW peak is relative to its own background rather
 % than its absolute magnitude. That is the sensible default everywhere it
 % is offered: a linear scale buries the Rayleigh peak's actual sharpness
 % against a much broader background. Zeros are floored to eps before
 % log10 so CData stays real and finite; out-of-range values are clipped for
 % DISPLAY only, via the caller's clim()/ylim().
 %
 % NaN handling: NaN entries (a real input -- plotDispersionCurves
 % interpolates per-wavelength Green columns onto one shared velocity axis
 % and leaves NaN outside each column's own range) are excluded from the
 % peak, and restored explicitly afterwards. Without that restore,
 % max(NaN, eps) resolves to eps in MATLAB's two-array max (NaN is treated
 % as absent whenever the other operand isn't also NaN), which would paint
 % every out-of-range cell at the floor colour instead of leaving it blank.
 %
 % Public rather than @Vis-private because GUI code outside visual/ needs the
 % same normalisation to place its own overlays on these plots — as does
 % plotClassificationCurve's y-projection, which must land exactly on the curve
 % plotGreenCurve drew.

win = Gui.viewResult(calcResult, windowMode, showNow) 
% Open the default viewer for any Calc* result, picked from its class
 % >> win = Gui.viewResult(calcResult, windowMode, showNow)
 %
 % INPUT:  1. calcResult .. any CalcCommon.resultClasses() object, already
 %                          calc()'d / calcAll()'d
 %  (opt)  2. windowMode .. passed on to viewers that take one ('combined' |
 %                          'split' | 'fullSplit', as the chosen viewer allows);
 %                          default = [] (the viewer's own default)
 %  (opt)  3. showNow    .. true (default) to make the window(s) visible; false
 %                          builds them invisible (headless/testing use)
 % OUTPUT: win .. the viewer object
 %
 % Result class -> viewer:
 %   CalcSingleHS/AngSweepHS/SingleLayered/AngSweepLayered -> Gui.combinedViewer
 %   CalcSinglePlate/AngSweepPlate                         -> Gui.combinedViewerPlate
 %   CalcIsofreqSingleLayered/IsofreqAngSweepLayered       -> Gui.isofreqCombinedViewer
 %   CalcFreqAngSweep2Layered                              -> Gui.freqAngSweep2LayeredViewer
 %   CalcWavevecAngSweep2Layered/Plate, by shape:
 %     >1 angle AND >1 wavelength/wavevector -> Gui.doubleSweepViewer(Plate)
 %     1 angle, >1 wavelength/wavevector     -> Gui.dispersionViewer(Plate)
 %     1 wavelength/wavevector               -> Gui.combinedViewer(Plate) on sweeps{1}
 %
 % The table lives here rather than in GuiCommon: choosing what a window shows
 % is a viewer decision, which GuiCommon deliberately never makes.
[win, calcResult, meta] = Gui.viewSaved(filePath, windowMode, showNow) 
% Load a saved Calc* result and open its default viewer -- no recalculation
 % >> [win, calcResult, meta] = Gui.viewSaved(filePath, windowMode, showNow)
 %
 % INPUT:  (opt) 1. filePath   .. .mat file written by Calc.saveResult (or a
 %                                plain save() of one Calc* result); omitted or
 %                                empty opens a file dialog in <project>/results/
 %         (opt) 2. windowMode .. as Gui.viewResult (default = [])
 %         (opt) 3. showNow    .. as Gui.viewResult (default = true)
 % OUTPUT: 1. win        .. the viewer object ([] if the dialog was cancelled)
 %         2. calcResult .. the loaded result
 %         3. meta       .. its save metadata (see CalcCommon.saveCalc)
 %
 % Typical use: a batch run (no display) ends with Calc.saveResult(...); the
 % file is then opened here from an interactive MATLAB session.

StructureControlGUI handle class

% StructureControlGUI -- combined structure-builder + control/launcher window
 %
 % One uifigure window: a tab per layer (Substrate + film layers, added/removed
 % dynamically via uitabgroup) for Material/CalcParams input, plus a control panel
 % that runs the forward-calc classes on the resulting Structure.
 %
 % See docs/reference/architecture/gui.md for the full design rationale.
 %
 % KNOWN LIMITATIONS:
 %   - Running a film layer standalone through the HS branch uses a hardcoded
 %     relDepth=20 truncation depth rather than an editable field.
 %
 % Constructing with an existing Structure (initialStructure) reverse-populates
 % every tab (dirMat, density, symmetry, Cij, relDepth/realThickness, legOrder)
 % from structIn.mater(k)/calcParams(k) -- see loadFromStructure/
 % populatePanelFromLayer.
 %
 % "Open viewer after Run" checkbox: on a successful Run, opens
 % Gui.combinedViewer for any Single or AngSweep result,
 % Gui.dispersionViewer for an isoWavevec 2-axis sweep,
 % Gui.isofreqCombinedViewer for an isoFreq Single or AngSweep result, or
 % Gui.freqAngSweep2LayeredViewer for an isoFreq 2-axis sweep. The
 % Combined/IsofreqCombined viewers dispatch Single vs. AngSweep internally in
 % their own bottom halves. Text and enabled state track the (layerMode, family,
 % calcType) toggles live, via refreshOpenViewerOption.
 %
 % GuliweRR v6.0

Properties

obj.advCustomThreshold 
numeric custom standard on the method's score scale
obj.advMethod 
classification method -> the Calc objects' sawClassMethod
obj.advStandard 
its standard: 'method default' | 'high' | 'low' | 'custom' -> sawThreshold
obj.calcFieldInputs 
1x3 uieditfield (text)
obj.calcFieldLabels 
1x3 uilabel
obj.lastResult 
whichever Calc* object [Run] last produced
obj.lastViewer 
viewer window [Run] last opened (openViewerCheckbox), or []
obj.maxGuiLayers (default: 5) 
soft usability cap on FILM layers (+1 for Substrate)
obj.panels (default: cell) 
{1,k}: panels{k} is a struct with .tab .isSubstrate .dirMatFields(2x3 cell) .dirMat(3x3) .libraryDropdown .hillToggle .hillSource .rhoField .symmetryDropdown .cijFields(6x6 cell, lower triangle empty) .depthField .legOrderField. A cell, NOT a "(1,:) struct"-typed property: a size/class- constrained struct property starts as a 0-field struct with no default, which then rejects the first panels(1)=structWithFields assignment as "dissimilar structures"
obj.structureModel (default: Structure) 
SetObservable drives onStructureChanged via the PostSet listener registered in the constructor -- own block because attributes like SetObservable apply to the whole properties block, not inline

 % average is on; Hill avg usable for any non-isotropic Cij (and to switch off)
 % >> updateMaterialLock(obj, k)

AngSweepViewerGUI handle class

% AngSweepViewerGUI -- angular-sweep viewer window
 %
 % One uifigure window: a map (classification diagnostic / Green's-function map /
 % Ritz-mode map / Ritz spectrum map), angle/velocity axis-range controls,
 % and click-to-select-a-mode interactivity. Built on CalcAngSweepHS/
 % CalcAngSweepLayered results and the Vis plotting functions
 % (plotClassificationDiagnostic/plotRitzModeMap/plotGreenMap/plotRitzSpectrumMap),
 % reused directly rather than re-implemented.
 %
 % CLASSIFICATION BAR (second top-bar row, a ClassificationBar): method (Green |
 % Refined | Ritz; layered Green | Ritz), standard and Reclassify for every angle of
 % the sweep -- see ClassificationBar. After a Reclassify the bar calls
 % onReclassified, which redraws the map (or the drilled-in single-angle curve)
 % with the axis ranges kept (redrawClassified) and then fires
 % OnClassificationChanged, so a host (CombinedViewerGUI, DoubleSweepViewerGUI)
 % refreshes its linked ModalShapeViewerGUI. refreshClassification is the entry
 % point for a host that reclassified the sweep elsewhere: it refreshes the bar
 % and redraws the same way. The nested single-angle viewer has no bar of its
 % own (see SingleAngleViewerGUI).
 %
 % The Ritz-mode map's criterion dropdown: 'Ritz score' (each mode coloured by
 % the score of the Ritz candidate it supports, Vis.ritzModeScores; modes
 % supporting none drawn faint), 'freqDisp' (the half-space veto quantity; the
 % colour scale marks the veto level RitzScore.MIN_FREQDISP and a click below it
 % reads "vetoed") and 'depthInsensitivity'. On a layered result freqDisp is
 % greyed in the list and the status line notes it is not used on layered stacks.
 %
 % The Ritz spectrum map (Vis.plotRitzSpectrumMap: |S(v)| from the stored modal
 % numerators, no solve) is the Ritz-side response next to the Green's-function map,
 % and the classification diagnostic's background on a 'ritzOnly' result.
 %
 % CLASSIFICATION OVERLAY: the 'Show types:' checkboxes apply to every graph
 % type. On the classification diagnostic they filter its own per-type series
 % (all on by default); on the Ritz-mode and Green's-function maps they toggle
 % the same classified points drawn on top as hollow rings
 % (GuiCommon.styleClassificationOverlay), off by default so those maps open
 % unchanged. Each of the two contexts remembers its own per-type state across
 % graph-type switches (diagnosticTypeVisible/overlayTypeVisible).
 %
 % SELECTION READOUT: a map click's angle/mode #/velocity/type is kept in
 % selectionInfo, followed by the classification readout of a classified mode
 % (method, score in dB, grade, supporting mode) and the shown criterion's value.
 % Standalone, all of it is shown on the status line; embedded (CombinedViewerGUI,
 % DoubleSweepViewerGUI) only the classification and criterion lines, since the
 % modal-shape viewer above already shows the selection (showSelectionInStatus).
 %
 % OnModeSelected below is the hand-off point ModalShapeViewerGUI attaches to;
 % see docs/reference/architecture/gui_viewers.md for the full viewer family.
 %
 % SCOPE: a single angular sweep only. The double-sweep dispersion classes
 % have their own viewers (DispersionViewerGUI, IsofreqAngSweepViewerGUI,
 % FreqAngSweep2LayeredViewerGUI).
 %
 % COLOUR SCALE: the graph types colour their data on fundamentally different
 % scales (Green / Ritz spectrum map: log10-normalised |G| / |S|; Ritz-mode map: a
 % criterion value; diagnostic: a categorical type or a score). One shared
 % cMinField/cMaxField pair stays safe across them by RE-DEFAULTING on every
 % graph-type change — onGraphTypeChanged reads the freshly-drawn axes' own CLim
 % right after drawing, before applyAxisRange re-applies it — rather than by
 % picking one fixed range that would suit only one of them.
 %
 % SINGLE-ANGLE DRILL-DOWN (viewSingleAngleBtn): toggles the map content
 % between the 2D angle x velocity view and an embedded SingleAngleViewerGUI at
 % obj.currentAngNo, the last-clicked angle or the first if none was clicked.
 % sweepResult.cs{angNo} is already a fully calcAll()'d CalcSingleHS/
 % CalcSingleLayered, so drilling down needs no recomputation. The map's
 % graphTypeDropdown/criterionDropdown stay the one set of controls while drilled
 % in: the nested viewer is built with hostControls=true (its own two dropdowns
 % hidden) and each graph type drives the curve of the same position in the
 % lists (Classification diagnostic -> curve, Green's-function map -> curve,
 % ...; the two Items lists are kept in the same order for this), through
 % SingleAngleViewerGUI.setView, so map and curve stay in step both ways; and
 % the nested viewer's OnModeSelected is forwarded through THIS object's
 % (dispatchModeSelected), so a caller that wired this object once keeps
 % working whether the user is looking at the map or at one angle. The
 % crosshair (lastCrosshairAngle/lastCrosshairVel) is re-drawn on the fresh
 % axes in both directions of the toggle rather than lost with the old ones.
 %
 % EXPORT FIGURE (button beside 'Show types:'): GuiCommon.exportAxes copies the
 % map as shown into an ordinary figure window, to save in any format. While
 % drilled in, the nested viewer's own button exports the curve.
 %
 % GuliweRR v6.0

Properties

obj.OnClassificationChanged (default: []) 
function handle @(), fired after a Reclassify (refreshClassification chain), for a linked modal-shape viewer
obj.OnModeSelected (default: []) 
function handle @(angle, modeNo, velSnapped, velRaw), for a linked modal-shape viewer
obj.classBar 
ClassificationBar -- see class docblock
obj.exportBtn 
'Export figure' of the map -- see class docblock
obj.isDrilledIn (default: false) 
true while showing an embedded SingleAngleViewerGUI instead of the map. Readable externally so a composite host (DoubleSweepViewerGUI) can tell whether xMinField/yMinField/cMinField are still valid graphics handles before touching them: they are destroyed by delete(obj.contentParent.Children) while drilled in, and nothing resets the property references, so reading .xMinField.Value then throws "Invalid or deleted object"
obj.selectionInfo (default: cell) 
{angle, mode #, velocity, type, ...} lines of the last map click, {} before any -- see SELECTION READOUT note
obj.showSelectionInStatus 
true: a map click's selectionInfo is also written to the status line. Defaults to standalone (no parentContainer) -- see class docblock's SELECTION READOUT note
obj.sweepResult 
CalcAngSweepHS or CalcAngSweepLayered, already calc()'d

AngSweepViewerGUIPlate handle class

% AngSweepViewerGUIPlate -- angular-sweep viewer window for a
 % CalcAngSweepPlate result. Plate counterpart of AngSweepViewerGUI.
 %
 % A SEPARATE class from AngSweepViewerGUI, not a class-dispatch branch inside
 % it — the same parallel-sibling convention every Plate GUI class uses.
 % Differences from AngSweepViewerGUI, all following from the plate physics:
 %   - No graphTypeDropdown at all — ABSENT, not disabled. There is exactly one
 %     map here, the Ritz-mode map: no Green's-function map (no analogue
 %     exists) and no classification diagnostic (no significant-vs-
 %     insignificant distinction to classify INTO).
 %   - criterionDropdown offers 'Mode type' | 'Confidence'
 %     (Vis.plotRitzModeMapPlate's 'modeType'/'confidence'), in place of
 %     'Ritz score'/'freqDisp'/'depthInsensitivity' (all SAW-specific).
 %   - Colour-range fields (cMinField/cMaxField) are meaningful only for
 %     criterion='confidence', a genuine continuous scale whose CLim is
 %     re-defaulted from the just-drawn axes. They are disabled, not hidden,
 %     for criterion='modeType', whose scatter points carry explicit RGB
 %     colour — a 4-way categorical legend, not a CLim-mapped scale.
 %   - No 'Show types:' per-type checkbox filter: plotRitzModeMapPlate returns
 %     ONE scatter handle for 'modeType', colour-coded via a colour matrix
 %     rather than one handle per type, so there is nothing to show/hide
 %     individually.
 %   - onMapClicked's info panel reports mode type and confidence directly off
 %     RitzSolverLamb.modesType/modeTypeConfidence, not a significantModesMap
 %     lookup.
 %
 % Single-angle drill-down (viewSingleAngleBtn) works exactly as
 % AngSweepViewerGUI's does: sweepResult.cs{angNo} is already a fully calc()'d
 % CalcSinglePlate, so drilling into an embedded SingleAngleViewerGUIPlate
 % needs no recomputation.
 %
 % GuliweRR v6.0

Properties

obj.OnModeSelected (default: []) 
function handle @(angle, modeNo, velSnapped, velRaw), for a linked modal-shape viewer
obj.sweepResult 
CalcAngSweepPlate, already calc()'d

SingleAngleViewerGUI handle class

% SingleAngleViewerGUI -- single-angle "curve" viewer window, the 1D analogue
 % of AngSweepViewerGUI for CalcSingleHS/CalcSingleLayered results, which have
 % no angle axis to sweep and so no 2D map to draw.
 %
 % One uifigure window: a curve (classification curve / Green's-function curve /
 % Ritz-mode curve / Ritz spectrum curve), a velocity/value axis-range
 % control, and click-to-select-a-mode interactivity — structurally the role
 % AngSweepViewerGUI plays for an angular sweep, driven by
 % Vis.plotClassificationCurve/plotRitzModeCurve/plotGreenCurve/
 % plotRitzSpectrumCurve instead of their *Map/*Diagnostic counterparts.
 %
 % CLASSIFICATION BAR (second top-bar row, a ClassificationBar): method, standard
 % and Reclassify for this result -- see ClassificationBar. After a Reclassify the
 % curve is redrawn (refreshClassification) and OnClassificationChanged fires, so a
 % host (CombinedViewerGUI) can refresh a linked modal-shape viewer. Hidden when
 % AngSweepViewerGUI embeds this class as its drill-down: the sweep's own bar then
 % classifies every angle at once, and reclassifying one angle alone would leave the
 % sweep's maps out of step.
 %
 % HOST CONTROLS (4th constructor argument): in that drill-down the sweep viewer's
 % own Graph type / Criterion dropdowns drive this curve (setView), so this class
 % hides its own two -- one set of controls on screen, kept in step across the
 % drill-down and back. The dropdowns still exist (setView sets them), only hidden.
 %
 % EXPORT FIGURE (button beside 'Show types:'): GuiCommon.exportAxes copies the
 % curve as shown into an ordinary figure window, to save in any format.
 %
 % The Ritz-mode curve's criterion dropdown: 'Ritz score' (each mode's score of
 % the Ritz candidate it supports, Vis.ritzModeScores; modes supporting none drawn
 % faint), 'freqDisp' (the half-space veto quantity, with the veto level
 % RitzScore.MIN_FREQDISP drawn; a click below it reads "vetoed") and
 % 'depthInsensitivity'. On a layered result freqDisp is greyed in the list and the
 % status line notes it is not used on layered stacks.
 %
 % The Ritz spectrum curve (Vis.plotRitzSpectrumCurve: |S(v)| from the stored modal
 % numerators) is the Ritz-side response next to the Green's-function curve, and the
 % classification curve's background on a 'ritzOnly' result, which has no Green data.
 %
 % CLASSIFICATION OVERLAY and SELECTION READOUT: as in AngSweepViewerGUI (see
 % its docblock). On the Ritz-mode, Green's-function and Ritz spectrum curves the
 % overlaid classified points are placed at the DISPLAYED curve's own value at
 % each mode's velocity (valueAtVelocity -- the crosshair's own lookup, which
 % reads the plotted line itself), not at plotClassificationCurve's projection, so
 % they sit on whichever curve is shown.
 %
 % The Green's-function curve (and the classification curve's background) is
 % |G13_eff|, the +-k symmetrised TGS response -- the one Green component a
 % result stores and the one the existence score reads. Other
 % components are not offered here: Vis.plotGreenCurve(singleResult, [], [],
 % 'G33') etc. recomputes any of them on request, as in AngSweepViewerGUI.
 % A 'ritzOnly' result has no Green data; the Green curve then reports the
 % plotGreenCurve error on the status line, and the classification curve is drawn
 % on the Ritz spectrum instead.
 %
 % Implements the SAME duck-typed interface AngSweepViewerGUI does (.fig,
 % .show(), .OnModeSelected, .OnClassificationChanged, .showCrosshairAt(angle,
 % vel)), so
 % CombinedViewerGUI/ModalShapeViewerGUI's wiring works against either class
 % without knowing which it has. Angle is always the fixed
 % obj.singleResult.angle here, accepted as an argument for interface symmetry.
 %
 % CROSSHAIR PERSISTENCE: the top-half mode's crosshair (last position passed
 % to showCrosshairAt, kept in lastCrosshairVel) is redrawn after every
 % graph-type/criterion change rather than lost to onGraphTypeChanged's cla()
 % -- the mechanic AngSweepViewerGUI uses across its drill-down toggle, applied
 % here to the curve redraw. modeCrosshairCheckbox (on by default) lets the
 % user switch it off, e.g. when the marker sits awkwardly on a busy curve --
 % see drawOrHideCrosshair.
 %
 % Only TWO axis-range fields (velocity, value), not three: none of this
 % class's curve types use colour as a data dimension by default. The Ritz-mode,
 % Green's-function and Ritz spectrum curves are plain 2D plots, and the
 % classification curve's default colorBy='type' uses fixed per-type swatches
 % rather than a continuous scale, so a colour-range control would control
 % nothing.
 %
 % GuliweRR v6.0

Properties

function handle @(angle, modeNo, velSnapped, velRaw), for a linked modal-shape viewer

SingleAngleViewerGUIPlate handle class

% SingleAngleViewerGUIPlate -- single-angle "curve" viewer window for a
 % CalcSinglePlate result. 1D analogue of AngSweepViewerGUIPlate, the Plate
 % counterpart of SingleAngleViewerGUI.
 %
 % A DELIBERATELY SEPARATE class from SingleAngleViewerGUI, not a
 % class-dispatch branch inside it (same "separate parallel sibling"
 % convention as ModalShapeViewerGUIPlate -- see its own docblock).
 % Differences from SingleAngleViewerGUI, all following from
 % CalcSinglePlate.m's own docblock:
 %   - No graphTypeDropdown at all -- ABSENT, not disabled
 %     (DispersionViewerGUIPlate's own precedent). There is exactly one
 %     graph type here (the Ritz-mode curve); no Green's-function curve
 %     (no analogue exists), no classification curve (no
 %     significant-vs-insignificant distinction to classify INTO -- every
 %     Ritz mode already IS a genuine physical branch).
 %   - criterionDropdown offers 'Mode type' | 'Confidence'
 %     (Vis.plotRitzModeCurvePlate's own 'modeType'/'confidence'), replacing
 %     'Ritz score'/'freqDisp'/'depthInsensitivity' (all SAW-specific, no Plate
 %     analogue).
 %   - No 'Show types:' per-type checkbox filter. plotRitzModeCurvePlate
 %     returns ONE scatter handle (colour-coded by type via a colour
 %     matrix), not one handle per type the way plotClassificationCurve
 %     does -- there is nothing to individually show/hide without splitting
 %     that function into 4 series.
 %   - onCurveClicked's info panel reports mode type + confidence
 %     (RitzSolverLamb.modesType/modeTypeConfidence) instead of a
 %     classification.significantModes lookup.
 %
 % Implements the same small duck-typed interface AngSweepViewerGUIPlate
 % does (.fig, .show(), .OnModeSelected property, .showCrosshairAt(angle,
 % vel)) so CombinedViewerGUIPlate/ModalShapeViewerGUIPlate's wiring works
 % unmodified against either class.
 %
 % GuliweRR v6.0

ModalShapeViewerGUI handle class

% ModalShapeViewerGUI -- modal-shape / Green's-response viewer window
 %
 % windowMode, the 2nd constructor argument, controls how many physical windows
 % this class opens:
 %
 %   ModalShapeViewerGUI(sweepResult)                 -- ONE window (default,
 %       'combined'): self-builds a single uifigure with the SAME {'3x','1x'}
 %       shapes-left/controls-right layout CombinedViewerGUI uses for this
 %       class's top half, then renders into a pre-built {3 x nLay} uiaxes grid
 %       (buildShapeAxesGrid) via plotModalShape/plotGreenModalShape's
 %       hAxCellIn parameter.
 %   ModalShapeViewerGUI(sweepResult, 'split')        -- TWO windows: a uifigure
 %       control panel (this class's own .fig — angle/mode stepping, source
 %       toggle, limiting-wave/SAW panel) plus a separate CLASSIC figure
 %       (.shapeFig) that plotModalShape/plotGreenModalShape render into with
 %       their classic axes() grid. Classic axes cannot be parented inside a
 %       uifigure at all, which is why this mode needs its own figure.
 %   ModalShapeViewerGUI(sweepResult, [], controlsParent, shapeParent)
 %       -- EMBEDDED: controlsParent/shapeParent are two uigridlayout regions
 %       of a caller-owned SHARED uifigure, and windowMode is ignored —
 %       embedding is the same uiaxes-grid mechanism 'combined' uses, hosted by
 %       the caller. Passing 'split' together with controlsParent is an error
 %       (ModalShapeViewerGUI:constructor:splitRequiresStandalone): splitting only makes
 %       sense when this class owns its own windows.
 %
 % obj.embedded (internal) is true whenever rendering goes through the
 % uiaxes-grid path — both real embedding and standalone 'combined' — and false
 % only for 'split' mode's classic-figure path.
 %
 % See docs/reference/architecture/gui_viewers.md for the full design rationale.
 % Standalone constructible (like AngSweepViewerGUI): takes a sweepResult directly, not a parent
 % controller object. Link two standalone windows manually via:
 %   win1 = ModalShapeViewerGUI(sweep); win2 = AngSweepViewerGUI(sweep);
 %   win2.OnModeSelected    = @(angle, modeNo, ~, rawVel) win1.goToSelection(angle, modeNo, rawVel);
 %   win1.OnSelectionChanged = @(angle, vel) win2.showCrosshairAt(angle, vel);
 % CombinedViewerGUI wires exactly this automatically.
 %
 % ANGLE STEPPING with source=Ritz modal shape follows the SAME PHYSICAL WAVE
 % (SAW/limiting-type identity), not the same raw mode index: Ritz mode
 % ordering by velocity can shuffle with angle, so "stay on this index" would
 % jump to the wrong branch. With source=Green's response it instead keeps the
 % same VELOCITY — there is no discrete mode or type to follow for an arbitrary
 % continuous velocity, so "same velocity, different angle" is the analogue.
 %
 % CONTINUOUS-VELOCITY GREEN'S MODE: a map click on AngSweepViewerGUI with
 % Green's response active has no discrete Ritz mode to snap to, so
 % goToVelocity/currentVel decouple the displayed velocity from any Ritz mode
 % index. The "Mode:" field is re-purposed as a direct velocity entry in this
 % state — a relabelled meaning, not a new widget. currentModeNo is still kept
 % pointed at the nearest Ritz mode so a switch back has a sensible starting
 % point, but never drives the Green's-response calculation.
 %
 % RETENTION-AWARE RECOMPUTE: under retentionLevel~='full',
 % cs{angNo}.ritzSolver.eigVecs_con may have been pruned to the significant
 % modes or cleared entirely. resolveDisplayRitz re-solves the missing
 % eigenvectors on demand, cached per angle, rather than freezing on stale data
 % or requiring retentionLevel='full' to browse modal shapes at all.
 %
 % "Mark current mode as SAW/limiting" is a manual QC override of the automatic
 % classification, SESSION-LOCAL ONLY: stored on this object's own overrides
 % list, never written back into the result's classification, which is
 % SetAccess=protected. See docs/reference/architecture/gui_viewers.md,
 % "Session-local classification overrides".
 %
 % LIVE CLASSIFICATION: the classified modes are read through the result handles
 % on every use (significantModesAt: cs{angNo}.classification.significantModes),
 % never copied at construction, so a Reclassify elsewhere (the classification bar
 % of the linked sweep / single-angle viewer) is seen here.
 % refreshClassification rebuilds the SAW / limiting panel for it; hosts
 % (CombinedViewerGUI, DoubleSweepViewerGUI) call it from the sweep viewer's
 % OnClassificationChanged.
 %
 % GuliweRR v6.0

Properties

ModalShapeViewerGUIPlate handle class

% ModalShapeViewerGUIPlate -- modal-shape viewer window for the Plate
 % (Lamb-wave) family. Ritz-mode-shape counterpart of ModalShapeViewerGUI.
 %
 % A DELIBERATELY SEPARATE class from ModalShapeViewerGUI, not a
 % class-dispatch branch inside it -- same "separate parallel sibling"
 % convention as every other Plate GUI class (see DispersionViewerGUIPlate's
 % own docblock). Differences from ModalShapeViewerGUI, all following
 % directly from CalcSinglePlate.m's own docblock (no Green analogue exists
 % for a free plate; every Ritz mode is a genuine physical branch, not a
 % "significant subset" of one):
 %   - No source toggle, no force-direction control, no continuous-velocity
 %     Green's-response mode -- ABSENT, not disabled (DispersionViewerGUIPlate's
 %     own precedent). Every control here drives the one and only source,
 %     the Ritz modal shape.
 %   - No significantModesMap/"SAW vs limiting" concept, so no session-local
 %     "mark as shown" override panel with per-entry Go-To buttons -- Plate
 %     has nothing analogous to classify a mode INTO (every mode already IS
 %     a genuine physical branch). Replaced by a single status label showing
 %     the CURRENTLY DISPLAYED mode's type/confidence
 %     (RitzSolverLamb.modesType/modeTypeConfidence), the same text
 %     DispersionViewerGUIPlate.redrawShape already renders.
 %   - No followPhysicalWave/classifyCurrentMode angle-stepping logic.
 %     ModalShapeViewerGUI's version specifically follows a SAW/limiting
 %     TYPE through significantModesMap across an angle step -- Plate has no
 %     such classification to follow. Angle stepping here keeps the SAME RAW
 %     MODE INDEX instead (clamped to the new angle's nCompModes) -- a plain
 %     default rather than an unrequested mode-type-tracking heuristic; if a
 %     mode's physical identity needs following across angle (mode ordering
 %     by velocity can shuffle, same as SAW), the user re-selects by mode
 %     type/confidence directly.
 %
 % Retention-aware recompute-on-demand (mirrors ModalShapeViewerGUI's own
 % resolveDisplayRitz exactly, RitzSolverLamb in place of RitzSolverSAW):
 % CalcSinglePlate's retentionLevel is 'full'/'none' only (no partial-prune
 % 'saw_only' tier to worry about column-index mismatches for) -- 'none'
 % clears eigVecs_con entirely via pruneEigVecs([]), so this class
 % recomputes and caches per angle whenever retentionLevel~='full', exactly
 % as ModalShapeViewerGUI already does.
 %
 % GuliweRR v6.0

active Ritz mode index

'combined' (default, one window) | 'split' (two windows)

CombinedViewerGUI handle class

% CombinedViewerGUI -- combination of ModalShapeViewerGUI and
 % AngSweepViewerGUI, with a windowMode toggle (2nd constructor argument) for
 % how many physical windows that combination opens:
 %
 %   CombinedViewerGUI(sweepResult)              -- ONE window (default,
 %       'combined'): modal shapes on top with their controls to the right,
 %       angular-sweep map on the bottom, in a 44%/56% split.
 %   CombinedViewerGUI(sweepResult, 'split')     -- TWO windows:
 %       ModalShapeViewerGUI standalone/'combined' (shapes and controls
 %       together) plus AngSweepViewerGUI standalone.
 %   CombinedViewerGUI(sweepResult, 'fullSplit') -- THREE windows:
 %       ModalShapeViewerGUI standalone/'split' (itself two windows, controls
 %       and shapes separately) plus AngSweepViewerGUI standalone.
 %
 % obj.fig is set only in 'combined' mode; there is no single shared figure in
 % 'split'/'fullSplit', where it stays []. Use show(), which delegates to each
 % sub-viewer and is correct in every mode, rather than touching obj.fig.
 %
 % Both underlying classes stay fully independent in every mode. This class
 % only supplies 'combined' mode with parent-container arguments and wires
 % AngSweepViewerGUI.OnModeSelected / ModalShapeViewerGUI.OnSelectionChanged
 % together — the same wiring a caller would do by hand for two standalone
 % windows, needing only the two sub-viewer handles, not how many physical
 % windows they occupy.
 %
 % SINGLE-ANGLE RESULTS: sweepResult may also be a CalcSingleHS/
 % CalcSingleLayered (calcAll()'d, not calc()'d — those have no angle
 % dimension to sweep). obj.angSweepViewer then holds a SingleAngleViewerGUI
 % instead, a 1D velocity curve rather than a 2D angle x velocity map, while
 % obj.modalShapeViewer is unaffected: ModalShapeViewerGUI accepts both result
 % families directly. The property keeps one name across both cases, external
 % code needing only the shared duck-typed interface (.fig/.show()/
 % .OnModeSelected/.OnClassificationChanged/.showCrosshairAt), never the concrete
 % class.
 %
 % RECLASSIFY: the bottom viewer's classification bar reclassifies the result; its
 % OnClassificationChanged then refreshes the modal-shape viewer
 % (ModalShapeViewerGUI.refreshClassification), which reads the classification
 % through the result handle.
 %
 % GuliweRR v6.0

Properties

obj.angSweepViewer 
AngSweepViewerGUI instance (angular-sweep sweepResult) OR SingleAngleViewerGUI instance (single-angle sweepResult) -- see class docblock's "SINGLE-ANGLE RESULTS" note
obj.modalShapeViewer 
ModalShapeViewerGUI instance
obj.windowMode 
'combined' (default) | 'split' | 'fullSplit' -- see class docblock

Methods

 %                          already calc()'d -- OR CalcSingleHS/
 %                          CalcSingleLayered, already calcAll()'d
 %                          (see class docblock's "SINGLE-ANGLE

 %  (opt) 2. windowMode  .. 'combined' (default) | 'split' | 'fullSplit'
 %                          -- see class docblock

DispersionViewerGUI handle class

% DispersionViewerGUI -- fixed-angle wavelength/frequency dispersion
 % viewer. See docs/reference/architecture/gui_viewers.md.
 %
 % Modal shapes plus controls on one side, a dispersion map on the other. ONE
 % class rather than a wired-together pair, because switching wavelength step
 % here switches WHICH CalcAngSweepLayered/ritzSolver the modal shape comes
 % from (sweepResult.sweeps{iw}), not just which mode within a fixed one.
 % ModalShapeViewerGUI's angle-oriented API does not fit that, so this class
 % renders modal shapes directly via Vis.plotModalShape, driven by its own
 % (wavelength step, branch) state.
 %
 % windowMode: 'combined' (default, ONE window, self-building the same
 % shapes-left/controls-right-over-map layout CombinedViewerGUI uses) |
 % 'split' (TWO windows, a modal-shape figure and a map figure).
 %
 % sweepResult MAY carry more than one angle: angleIdx (3rd constructor
 % argument, default 1) picks which one THIS view is fixed at. It adds no
 % angle stepper — this is always a SINGLE fixed-angle view — it only lets a
 % genuine multi-angle CalcWavevecAngSweep2Layered be sliced at a chosen
 % angle. DoubleSweepViewerGUI is what offers angle switching, by re-embedding
 % a fresh DispersionViewerGUI at a new angleIdx each time.
 %
 % EMBEDDING: parentContainer (4th constructor argument) hosts this class's
 % content inside a caller-owned uigridlayout region instead of a fresh
 % uifigure — the mechanism DoubleSweepViewerGUI uses. One region is enough,
 % unlike ModalShapeViewerGUI's two-argument controlsParent/shapeParent,
 % because this class is already a self-contained composite and builds its own
 % internal shapes/controls/map split inside whatever it is given. windowMode
 % must be 'combined' when parentContainer is given: 'split' needs two
 % independent windows, which is meaningless once this class owns none.
 %
 % CLASSIFICATION BAR (above the dispersion map, a ClassificationBar): method
 % (Green | Ritz) and standard for the WHOLE wavelength sweep -- the bar reads its
 % state off sweeps{1} and calls sweepResult.classify, which reclassifies every
 % wavelength and reassembles the maps. refreshClassification then redraws the
 % branches (Vis.plotDispersionCurves highlights the classified ones, read live
 % from significantModesMap) and the info panel's type line. The tracked branches
 % themselves do not depend on the classification.
 %
 % No Green's-response toggle (CalcWavevecAngSweep2Layered is layered-only,
 % with no half-space equivalent) and no SAW/limiting override panel. Branch
 % "prev/next" stepping needs no "follow physical wave" logic the way
 % ModalShapeViewerGUI's angle-stepping does: continuity across wavelength
 % steps is already baked into branchModeNoMap
 % (DispersionBranches.track), so stepping a branch is just
 % incrementing a column index.
 %
 % GuliweRR v6.0

Properties

obj.angleIdx 
index into sweepResult.angles this view is fixed at (default 1) -- see class docblock
obj.branchModeNoMap 
[nWav x nCompModes], DispersionBranches.track's own output
obj.classBar 
ClassificationBar -- see class docblock
obj.currentBranchIdx 
column index into branchModeNoMap (a tracked physical branch)
obj.currentWavNo 
index into sweepResult.wavelengths
obj.displayDropdown 
'components' | 'rgb' -- GuiCommon.shapeDisplayDropdown
obj.fig 
hosts the modal-shape half (shapes uiaxes grid + controls)
obj.mapFig 
hosts the dispersion-curve map + its controls; == fig in 'combined' windowMode, a separate uifigure in 'split'
obj.sweepResult 
CalcWavevecAngSweep2Layered, already calc()'d
obj.windowMode 
'combined' (default) | 'split' -- see class docblock

Methods

obj = DispersionViewerGUI(sweepResult, windowMode, angleIdx, parentContainer) 
% Construct the dispersion viewer
 % >> obj = DispersionViewerGUI(sweepResult, windowMode, angleIdx, parentContainer)
 %
 % INPUT: 1. sweepResult .. CalcWavevecAngSweep2Layered, already
 %                          calc()'d, with retentionLevel='full'
 %                          (needed for
 %                          DispersionBranches.track
 %                          -- its own missingEigVecs error surfaces
 %                          directly if this isn't satisfied)
 %  (opt) 2. windowMode  .. 'combined' (default) | 'split' -- see
 %                          class docblock. Must be 'combined' (or
 %                          omitted) when parentContainer is given.
 %  (opt) 3. angleIdx    .. which angle (default 1) -- see class
 %                          docblock's "sweepResult MAY carry more
 %                          than one angle" paragraph
 %  (opt) 4. parentContainer .. a uigridlayout region to build into
 %                          instead of a fresh own uifigure -- see
 %                          class docblock's "Embedding" paragraph
obj.applyAxisRange() 
% Apply the X/Y range fields to the map axes -- same guarded
 % parse-and-apply pattern as AngSweepViewerGUI.applyAxisRange.
 % Public (not Access=private): same reasoning as every other
 % callback in this block, so tests can call it directly.
 % >> applyAxisRange(obj)
obj.goTo(wavNo, branchIdx) 
% Jump directly to a (wavelength step, branch) selection
 % >> goTo(obj, wavNo, branchIdx)
obj.onCurveTypeChanged() 
% Also fired by showGreenBgCheckbox's own ValueChangedFcn (not just
 % curveTypeDropdown's) -- both need the exact same redraw +
 % Enable-gating response, so one shared handler covers both widgets
 % rather than duplicating the logic.
obj.onDisplayChanged() 
% Switch between the signed component panels and the polarisation-RGB
 % panel -- as ModalShapeViewerGUI.onDisplayChanged
 % >> onDisplayChanged(obj)
obj.onMapClicked(~, event) 
% Snap a map click to the nearest plotted (wavelength step,
 % branch) point -- nearest across every branch's line, in
 % axis-range-normalised distance (so wildly different x/y scales,
 % e.g. wavelength in microns vs. velocity in km/s, don't silently
 % bias the match toward whichever axis happens to span more raw
 % units) -- GuiCommon.nearestLinePoint owns that search, including
 % the "skip hidden branches" rule showRitzBranchesCheckbox needs.
 % Same isstruct/isprop IntersectionPoint handling as every other
 % viewer, via GuiCommon.clickPoint (tests pass a plain struct
 % instead of a real HitEvent object).
 % >> onMapClicked(obj, src, event)
obj.refreshClassification() 
% Redraw after the sweep's classification changed: the bar, the branch
 % highlighting (with the axis ranges kept) and the selection's type line
 % >> refreshClassification(obj)
obj.setAxisRange(axisName, lims) 
% Set one axis range from code, as if typed into its min/max fields
 % >> setAxisRange(obj, axisName, lims)    e.g. setAxisRange(obj, 'y', [NaN 7])
 %
 % axisName 'x' | 'y' (no colour axis here); lims [lo hi], a NaN
 % entry keeps that limit, [] keeps both -- see GuiCommon.setAxisRange
obj.show() 
% Make every window visible (constructed invisible so headless/
 % test use is safe) -- a no-op on the mapFig side beyond the first
 % call in 'combined' windowMode, since .fig and .mapFig are then
 % the same figure.
 % >> show(obj)

DispersionViewerGUIPlate handle class

% DispersionViewerGUIPlate -- fixed-angle wavevector/frequency dispersion
 % viewer for a free-plate (Lamb-wave) 2D sweep. See
 % docs/reference/architecture/gui_viewers.md and docs/reference/architecture/plate.md.
 %
 % A SEPARATE class from DispersionViewerGUI (the CalcWavevecAngSweep2Layered
 % analogue), not a class(sweepResult) dispatch branch inside it — the same
 % parallel-sibling split Plate and Layered/HS take everywhere their data
 % models genuinely differ. Differences, all following from the plate physics
 % (no Green's function, every mode real):
 %   - No Green's-response toggle at all: absent, not gated, since no analogue
 %     exists for a free plate.
 %   - No significantModesMap-based branch highlighting (Plate has no
 %     "significant subset" concept) — uses CalcWavevecAngSweep2Plate's
 %     classifyBranches instead, via Vis.plotDispersionCurvesPlate.
 %   - Adds a Christoffel-bounds checkbox and a colour-mode selector
 %     ('type'/'branch'), both capabilities Vis.plotDispersionCurvesPlate has
 %     and Vis.plotDispersionCurves does not.
 %   - The wavevector stepper reads sweepResult.wavVecs directly, not a real
 %     wavelength.
 %   - The modal-shape frequency label reads ritz.freqsPhysical directly, not a
 %     re-derived vel/wavelength ratio.
 %
 % Everything else — windowMode ('combined'/'split'), embedding via
 % parentContainer, the {3 x nLay} shape-axes grid, axis-range fields,
 % crosshair/click-to-select, branch stepping via branchModeNoMap — follows
 % DispersionViewerGUI's mechanisms, none of which are Layered-specific.
 %
 % GuliweRR v6.0

Properties

obj.angleIdx 
index into sweepResult.angles this view is fixed at (default 1)
obj.branchModeNoMap 
[nWav x nCompModes], DispersionBranches.track's own output
obj.currentBranchIdx 
column index into branchModeNoMap (a tracked physical branch)
obj.currentWavNo 
index into sweepResult.wavVecs
obj.displayDropdown 
'components' | 'rgb' -- GuiCommon.shapeDisplayDropdown
obj.fig 
hosts the modal-shape half (shapes uiaxes grid + controls)
obj.mapFig 
hosts the dispersion-curve map + its controls; == fig in 'combined' windowMode, a separate uifigure in 'split'
obj.sweepResult 
CalcWavevecAngSweep2Plate, already calc()'d
obj.windowMode 
'combined' (default) | 'split' -- see class docblock

DoubleSweepViewerGUI handle class

% DoubleSweepViewerGUI -- interactive viewer for a genuine DOUBLE sweep
 % (CalcWavevecAngSweep2Layered with BOTH numel(angles)>1 AND
 % numel(wavelengths)>1), switching between an angular-map view (at a
 % chosen fixed wavelength) and a dispersion-curve view (at a chosen fixed
 % angle), remembering axis-range settings on each side of the switch.
 %
 % A separate class rather than a CombinedViewerGUI mode. CombinedViewerGUI
 % already has a "map <-> single-angle curve" swap, but that lives entirely
 % inside AngSweepViewerGUI and works bottom-only, because both sides pair
 % with the SAME top (ModalShapeViewerGUI, angle-oriented). Angular-map and
 % dispersion-curve mode do not: DispersionViewerGUI exists as its own class
 % precisely because ModalShapeViewerGUI's angle-oriented API does not fit
 % wavelength-stepping, so it renders modal shapes itself. This class
 % therefore swaps a whole TOP+BOTTOM PAIR at once
 % (ModalShapeViewerGUI+AngSweepViewerGUI <-> DispersionViewerGUI's
 % self-contained shape+curve content), by the same mechanics
 % AngSweepViewerGUI's own swap uses — stable content slot, external toggle
 % button, saved-and-restored axis ranges — one level up.
 %
 % ANGULAR MODE: ModalShapeViewerGUI + AngSweepViewerGUI, wired as
 % CombinedViewerGUI wires them, pointed at sweepResult.sweeps{currentWavNo} —
 % already a complete, independently-calc()'d CalcAngSweepLayered, so
 % switching wavelength needs no recomputation, just rebuilding both viewers
 % against a different element of sweepResult.sweeps.
 %
 % DISPERSION MODE: DispersionViewerGUI embedded via its parentContainer
 % support, pointed at sweepResult with angleIdx=currentAngNo — again no
 % recomputation, just re-embedding at a different angle slice.
 %
 % RECLASSIFY: both modes carry a classification bar (AngSweepViewerGUI's,
 % DispersionViewerGUI's), and both classify the WHOLE double sweep
 % (CalcWavevecAngSweep2Layered.classify: every wavelength, 2D maps reassembled),
 % never one wavelength's slice alone: the angular sweep viewer is built with
 % classifyTarget = sweepResult. After a Reclassify in angular mode it redraws and
 % the modal-shape viewer refreshes (the CombinedViewerGUI chain); the dispersion
 % viewer redraws its own branches. A mode switch rebuilds the other side from the
 % live result, so it shows the new classification too.
 %
 % currentWavNo/currentAngNo are tracked independently and persist across mode
 % switches: entering angular mode uses whatever wavelength was last active
 % (from a previous visit or the dispersion view's wavelength stepper), and
 % entering dispersion mode uses whatever angle was last active (a previous
 % visit, a map click, or ModalShapeViewerGUI's angle-stepping).
 %
 % RAM WARNING: dispersion mode needs sweepResult calc()'d with
 % retentionLevel='full' (DispersionBranches.track's own requirement). This is
 % NOT validated at construction, since angular mode alone does not need it
 % and requiring it unconditionally would block viewing a large double sweep
 % computed cheaply for its angular side. Switching to dispersion mode without
 % it surfaces DispersionBranches.track's missingEigVecs error instead. Full
 % retention across BOTH swept axes of a real-sized double sweep is a genuinely
 % large RAM cost — see docs/pitfalls.md NUM4.
 %
 % Single window only, unlike CombinedViewerGUI's combined/split/fullSplit
 % toggle.
 %
 % GuliweRR v6.0

Properties

obj.angularSweepViewer (default: []) 
AngSweepViewerGUI, while mode=='angular' -- readable like CombinedViewerGUI.angSweepViewer, for scripted set-up
obj.currentAngNo 
index into sweepResult.angles -- used by dispersion mode
obj.currentWavNo 
index into sweepResult.wavelengths -- used by angular mode
obj.dispersionViewer (default: []) 
DispersionViewerGUI, while mode=='dispersion' (same)
obj.mode 
'angular' | 'dispersion'
obj.sweepResult 
CalcWavevecAngSweep2Layered, calc()'d, numel(angles)>1 AND numel(wavelengths)>1

Methods

% Construct the double-sweep viewer
 % >> obj = DoubleSweepViewerGUI(sweepResult)
 %
 % INPUT: 1. sweepResult .. CalcWavevecAngSweep2Layered, already
 %                          calc()'d, with numel(angles)>1 AND
 %                          numel(wavelengths)>1 (a genuine double
 %                          sweep -- use CombinedViewerGUI directly
 %                          for an angle-only sweep, or
 %                          DispersionViewerGUI directly for a
 %                          wavelength-only one)
obj.onAngularModeSelected(angle, modeNo, velRaw)

 % viewer AND tracks the clicked angle as obj.currentAngNo, so a later
 % switch to dispersion mode lands on the selected angle.
 % >> onAngularModeSelected(obj, angle, modeNo, velRaw)
obj.onAngularSelectionChanged(angle, vel)

 % map's crosshair (CombinedViewerGUI's own wiring) AND tracks the
 % angle as obj.currentAngNo, same reasoning as
 % onAngularModeSelected (angle stepping via the shape viewer's own
 % prev/next buttons counts as "selecting" an angle too).
 % >> onAngularSelectionChanged(obj, angle, vel)
obj.onSwitchMode() 
% Switch between angular-map and dispersion-curve mode, carrying over
 % the wavelength/angle active in whichever mode is being left, and
 % saving/restoring each mode's own axis-range fields.
 % >> onSwitchMode(obj)
obj.onWavChanged(newWavNo) 
% Angular-mode wavelength dropdown callback -- rebuilds against a
 % different sweeps{iWav} element (see buildAngularContent).
 % >> onWavChanged(obj, newWavNo)
 % keep the map's current ranges across the slice rebuild, typed or
 % set by setAxisRange (the fields are dead while drilled in -- the
 % last saved range is then kept instead)
obj.setAxisRange(axisName, lims) 
% Set one axis range of the CURRENT mode's view from code
 % >> setAxisRange(obj, axisName, lims)    e.g. setAxisRange(obj, 'y', [NaN 7])
 %
 % Forwarded to the angular map viewer or, in dispersion mode, to the
 % dispersion viewer (no colour axis there: 'c' errors) -- see
 % GuiCommon.setAxisRange. The other mode keeps its own saved range.
obj.show() 
% Make the window visible, maximised (constructed invisible so headless/test
 % use is safe)
 % >> show(obj)

% DoubleSweepViewerGUIPlate -- interactive viewer for a genuine DOUBLE
 % sweep (CalcWavevecAngSweep2Plate with BOTH numel(angles)>1 AND
 % numel(wavVecs)>1), switching between an angular-map view (at a chosen

IsofreqSingleLayeredViewerGUI handle class

% IsofreqSingleLayeredViewerGUI -- single-angle, single-omega0 isoFreq
 % "curve" viewer window, the isoFreq counterpart of SingleAngleViewerGUI
 % for CalcIsofreqSingleLayered results.
 %
 % Unlike SingleAngleViewerGUI (three graph types: Ritz-mode/Green's-
 % function/classification curve), this family has exactly ONE possible
 % graph -- the Green |G_33| field vs. kParRange -- since CalcIsofreqSingleLayered
 % never runs a Ritz solve at all (fixed omega0, k-swept Green's-function
 % scan only; see docs/reference/architecture/forwardCalcs.md, "isoWavevec vs.
 % isoFreq"). So there is no graphTypeDropdown/criterionDropdown at all --
 % absent, not disabled, matching the Plate viewer family's own precedent.
 % Rendering reuses Vis.plotDispersionMap directly (it already dispatches
 % on CalcIsofreqSingleLayered for its 1D line case), not a new dedicated
 % plotting function.
 %
 % Implements a renamed duck-typed interface: OnPointSelected/
 % showCrosshairAt(angle, kParVal), NOT SingleAngleViewerGUI's OnModeSelected/
 % showCrosshairAt(angle, modeNo, velSnapped, velRaw) -- kParRange is a
 % continuous, physically-meaningful axis here (CalcIsofreqSingleLayered's
 % own docblock), not a discrete Ritz mode index, so the callback signature
 % says so honestly instead of reusing a shape built for a different
 % quantity. angle is accepted only for interface symmetry with
 % IsofreqAngSweepViewerGUI (always obj.singleResult.angle here).
 %
 % See docs/reference/architecture/gui_viewers.md for the full isoFreq viewer family.
 %
 % GuliweRR v6.0

Properties

obj.OnPointSelected (default: []) 
function handle @(angle, kParVal), set by IsofreqModalShapeViewerGUI
obj.singleResult 
CalcIsofreqSingleLayered, already calc()'d

IsofreqAngSweepViewerGUI handle class

% IsofreqAngSweepViewerGUI -- isoFreq angular-sweep viewer window, the
 % isoFreq counterpart of AngSweepViewerGUI for CalcIsofreqAngSweepLayered
 % results (angle x kParRange Green |G_33| heatmap at one fixed omega0).
 %
 % Unlike AngSweepViewerGUI (three graph types: Ritz-mode/Green's-function
 % map/classification diagnostic), this family has exactly ONE possible
 % graph -- the Green |G_33| field -- since CalcIsofreqAngSweepLayered never
 % runs a Ritz solve at all (see docs/reference/architecture/forwardCalcs.md,
 % "isoWavevec vs. isoFreq"). So there is no graphTypeDropdown/
 % criterionDropdown at all -- absent, not disabled, matching the Plate
 % viewer family's own precedent. Rendering reuses Vis.plotDispersionMap
 % directly (it already dispatches on CalcIsofreqAngSweepLayered for its 2D
 % heatmap case, plotType cartesian/polar included), not a new dedicated
 % plotting function.
 %
 % In 'polar' view the map is drawn in Cartesian X/Y (pol2cart of angle and
 % kParRange): a click is converted back with cart2pol, the crosshair is placed
 % with pol2cart, and the angle/kParRange range fields do not apply (they are
 % disabled; the colour range still applies).
 %
 % Click-to-select fires OnPointSelected(angle, kParVal) directly from the
 % clicked point -- simpler than AngSweepViewerGUI's own nearest-angle-
 % then-nearest-mode search, since there is no discrete mode list to snap
 % to here (kParRange is a continuous, physically-meaningful axis --
 % CalcIsofreqSingleLayered's own docblock). Retains the "View single
 % angle" drill-down to IsofreqSingleLayeredViewerGUI, same
 % isDrilledIn/returnToSweepView mechanism AngSweepViewerGUI itself uses.
 %
 % See docs/reference/architecture/gui_viewers.md for the full isoFreq viewer family.
 %
 % GuliweRR v6.0

Properties

obj.OnPointSelected (default: []) 
function handle @(angle, kParVal), set by IsofreqModalShapeViewerGUI
obj.isDrilledIn (default: false) 
true while showing an embedded IsofreqSingleLayeredViewerGUI instead of the map -- same readable-externally reasoning as AngSweepViewerGUI.isDrilledIn
obj.sweepResult 
CalcIsofreqAngSweepLayered, already calc()'d

 %                              StructureControlGUI's Run button, typically)

IsofreqModalShapeViewerGUI handle class

% IsofreqModalShapeViewerGUI -- on-demand Green's-response depth-resolved
 % displacement field viewer for the isoFreq Layered family, the isoFreq
 % counterpart of ModalShapeViewerGUI's Green's-response source.
 %
 % CalcIsofreqSingleLayered/CalcIsofreqAngSweepLayered never run a Ritz
 % solve (see docs/reference/architecture/forwardCalcs.md, "isoWavevec vs. isoFreq"),
 % so unlike ModalShapeViewerGUI there is no Ritz modal shape to show and no
 % sourceDropdown -- Green's response is the ONLY source, so the dropdown is
 % dropped entirely (absent, not disabled), matching the Plate viewer
 % family's own precedent. Everything Ritz-specific in ModalShapeViewerGUI
 % (limModePanel, session-local SAW/limiting overrides, "follow the physical
 % wave" angle stepping, retention-aware recompute-on-demand) has no
 % isoFreq analogue and is dropped along with it.
 %
 % The underlying mechanism is reused directly: GreenSolver.calc with
 % nargout>1 (full output), then Vis.calcGreenDispField2D/
 % Vis.plotGreenModalShape -- exactly what ModalShapeViewerGUI's own
 % Green's-response mode does, with scanMode='isoFreq', the right omega0,
 % and kParRange converted to velocity via v = omega0/kParRange (the same
 % conversion CalcIsofreqSingleLayered.calc() performs). A layered structure
 % (nLay>=2, the usual isoFreq case) goes through
 % Vis.plotGreenModalShapeLayered instead, one row per layer, with the same
 % 'boosted' row heights the Ritz viewers use (see buildShapeAxesGrid). The
 % isoFreq depth scaling needs nothing extra here: GreenSolver.calc stores
 % hScaleOut = omega0/v = |k_parallel|, so every depth is already in units of
 % the selected wave's own wavelength (docs/reference/architecture/green.md).
 % Any solver error is caught onto the status line, the same try/catch
 % pattern ModalShapeViewerGUI.redrawShape uses.
 %
 % "kParRange" is a continuous, physically-meaningful axis (not a discrete
 % Ritz mode index), so this class's state is (currentAngNo, currentKParVal,
 % forceDir) rather than ModalShapeViewerGUI's (currentAngNo, currentModeNo/
 % currentVel) dual-meaning pair -- there is nothing to switch between.
 % Angle stepping keeps the SAME kParRange across the step, the direct
 % analogue of ModalShapeViewerGUI's "same velocity, different angle"
 % behaviour in its own Green's-response mode.
 %
 % ACCEPTS EITHER CalcIsofreqAngSweepLayered OR CalcIsofreqSingleLayered
 % directly, same "adapt the single-point case into a degenerate 1-angle
 % struct" pattern ModalShapeViewerGUI uses for CalcSingleHS/
 % CalcSingleLayered. IMPORTANT DIFFERENCE: CalcIsofreqSingleLayered.structure
 % is ALREADY ROTATED to its own angle (constructor: CalcCommon.rotateStructure(structure,
 % angle)), whereas CalcIsofreqAngSweepLayered.structure is the UNROTATED base
 % (each element rotates its own internal copy). GreenSolver.calc rotates its
 % `structure` argument by whatever `angle` it is given, UNCONDITIONALLY --
 % this is the rotation rule documented in docs/reference/architecture/
 % forwardCalcs.md, "Rotation convention: pass 0 to solvers after
 % construction", and the reason
 % CalcIsofreqSingleLayered.calc() itself passes angle=0, not obj.angle, to
 % GreenSolver.calc. So redrawShape below passes angle=0 when built from a
 % single-point adapter (already rotated) and the real angleVal when built
 % from a genuine sweep (unrotated), tracked via the private
 % structureAlreadyRotated flag set at construction.
 %
 % See docs/reference/architecture/gui_viewers.md for the full isoFreq viewer family.
 %
 % GuliweRR v6.0

Properties

obj.OnSelectionChanged (default: []) 
function handle @(angle, kParVal), set by the map/curve viewer
obj.displayDropdown 
'components' | 'rgb' -- GuiCommon.shapeDisplayDropdown
obj.sweepResult 
struct(angles, omega0, structure, kParRange) -- either a genuine CalcIsofreqAngSweepLayered or a degenerate 1-angle adapter built from a CalcIsofreqSingleLayered (see class docblock)

FreqAngSweep2LayeredViewerGUI handle class

% FreqAngSweep2LayeredViewerGUI -- interactive viewer for a
 % CalcFreqAngSweep2Layered result (isoFreq 2D sweep: angle x real
 % reference frequency omega0, each omega0 its own angle x kParRange Green
 % |G_33| heatmap slice -- see CalcFreqAngSweep2Layered's own docblock).
 % An omega0 stepper rebuilds IsofreqModalShapeViewerGUI +
 % IsofreqAngSweepViewerGUI against a different sweepResult.sweeps{iOmega}
 % element on every step.
 %
 % Does not embed IsofreqCombinedViewerGUI, for the same reason
 % DoubleSweepViewerGUI does not embed CombinedViewerGUI: the composite
 % class has no parentContainer support, only its two children do. This
 % class instead directly builds+wires IsofreqModalShapeViewerGUI/
 % IsofreqAngSweepViewerGUI into its own layout on every omega0 step, a
 % direct copy of DoubleSweepViewerGUI.buildAngularContent/onWavChanged
 % substituting sweeps{currentOmegaNo} for sweeps{currentWavNo}.
 %
 % UNLIKE DoubleSweepViewerGUI, there is no second mode to switch to (no
 % dispersion-curve view exists for isoFreq -- no Ritz, no branch
 % tracking): every omega0 step rebuilds the same one view, always
 % resetting to the map. There is no "drilled-in" state worth carrying
 % over either, since the underlying CalcIsofreqSingleLayered object at a
 % given angle is a genuinely different object at a different omega0.
 % Single window only.
 %
 % See docs/reference/architecture/gui_viewers.md for the full isoFreq viewer family.
 %
 % GuliweRR v6.0

Properties

index into sweepResult.omega0List
obj.sweepResult 
CalcFreqAngSweep2Layered, already calc()'d

ClassificationBar handle class

% ClassificationBar -- the viewers' classification row: method, standard, Reclassify
 %
 % One row of controls, built into a row of a viewer's own layout (the second
 % top-bar row of SingleAngleViewerGUI, AngSweepViewerGUI and DispersionViewerGUI):
 %   Classification method .. Green | Refined | Ritz (layered: Green | Ritz)
 %   Standard   .. high | low | custom; custom enables a numeric field on the
 %                 method's own score scale (the scale 'high' is compared on)
 %   dB label   .. the selected standard's value in dB (20 x value)
 %   Reclassify .. classifyTarget.classify(method, threshold): re-thresholds the
 %                 stored candidates, no recomputation (Refined solves its spot
 %                 directions on first use and prints the cost)
 %   status     .. the active method and standard, or the stale banner
 % After a successful Reclassify the bar refreshes itself and calls OnReclassified,
 % which the owning viewer uses to redraw and to pass the refresh on (nested
 % single-angle viewer, linked modal-shape viewer).
 %
 % UNAVAILABLE METHODS. uidropdown has no per-item Enable, so:
 %   - a method that does not exist for the geometry is left out of the list
 %     (Refined on a layered stack: there is no spot score for stacks);
 %   - a method that exists but needs a recompute stays in the list, greyed
 %     (italic grey item style), and selecting it reverts to the previous method
 %     with the reason on the status line (Green and Refined on a 'ritzOnly'
 %     result: they need calcMode 'full').
 % Keeping such items visible says that the method exists and why it is off, which
 % removing them would hide. The dropdown tooltip lists every method's
 % availability: the reason, or Refined's first-use cost.
 %
 % STANDARDS. 'high' and 'low' are the method's own named standards
 % (Vis.classificationScoreScale reads the values off the score classes). For
 % 'green' and 'refined' on a half-space, 'low' is compared with a separate
 % structure-relative score; the label says so. Changing the method resets the
 % standard to that method's default (CalcCommon.defaultThreshold) and the custom
 % value to its 'high' value, since the scales differ between methods.
 %
 % STALE RESULTS. A result classified by an older rule set (CalcCommon.isStale)
 % shows a "stale -- recompute" banner and Reclassify is disabled; classify would
 % refuse it anyway. A result from before the active method was stored (empty
 % activeMethod / activeThreshold, only ever a stale one) shows its sawClassMethod
 % (if unset, calcMode's own) and that method's default standard in the controls: a
 % starting point for the recompute, not a statement about how it was classified (the
 % banner says so).
 %
 % The bar reads its state (calcMode, activeMethod, activeThreshold, staleness)
 % from `result` and calls classify on `classifyTarget`, by default the same
 % object. A layered wavevector sweep (CalcWavevecAngSweep2Layered) passes one of
 % its CalcAngSweepLayered slices as `result` and itself as `classifyTarget`, so
 % every wavelength is reclassified and its 2D maps reassembled. Both are fixed at
 % construction; the owner sets its reclassify handler with setReclassifyHandler.
 % The methods offered come from CalcCommon.classificationMethods.
 %
 % GuliweRR v6.0

Properties

obj.OnReclassified (default: []) 
@() called after a successful Reclassify (setReclassifyHandler)
obj.classifyTarget 
object whose classify(method, threshold) Reclassify calls
obj.customField 
numeric uieditfield, enabled for 'custom' only
obj.grid 
the bar's own uigridlayout
obj.isLayered 
true for a layered stack
obj.lastMethod (default: '') 
method dropdown value before the last change (revert target)
obj.result 
CalcSingleHS/CalcSingleLayered or CalcAngSweepHS/CalcAngSweepLayered
obj.unavailable (default: struct) 
field = method, value = the reason it is greyed

Methods

 % INPUT: 1. parent         .. uigridlayout to place the bar in
 %        2. result         .. CalcSingleHS/CalcSingleLayered or
 %                             CalcAngSweepHS/CalcAngSweepLayered, classified

txt = ClassificationBar.standardText(method, standard, customValue, isLayered)

 % >> txt = ClassificationBar.standardText(method, standard, customValue, isLayered)

 % INPUT: 1. method      .. 'green' | 'refined' | 'ritz'

GuiCommon value class

% GuiCommon -- shared internals for the GuliweRR viewer-window family.
 %
 % Mechanical GUI steps only — status-line colouring, the crosshair marker,
 % click-point extraction, the shared layout scaffolding, the min/max
 % range-field pairs. Nothing here decides what a window shows or how a result
 % is interpreted (each ...GUI class's own job) and nothing here does physics.
 % Stateless static class: never instantiate it, never call it through Gui.*.
 %
 % Static helpers rather than a shared base class. The HS/Layered vs. Plate vs.
 % isoFreq viewer families genuinely differ in which controls exist at all, in
 % what a click means, and in what the y-axis is; static calls leave each
 % class's decisions visible at its own call site, where inheritance would hide
 % them in an override chain.
 %
 % Error-ID convention: the constructor-argument validators raise errors on the
 % calling constructor's behalf. They take a `className` argument and throw
 % <className>:constructor:<condition> (e.g.
 % ModalShapeViewerGUI:constructor:invalidWindowMode), the three-part form of every
 % other error, so the identifier stays the caller's own and greppable from it.
 %
 % Duck-typed helpers: captureAxisRange/restoreAxisRange read a viewer's
 % xMinField/xMaxField/yMinField/yMaxField (plus cMinField/cMaxField where the
 % viewer has them) by NAME rather than taking six handle arguments — the same
 % small interface CombinedViewerGUI/DoubleSweepViewerGUI rely on.
 %
 % GuliweRR v6.0

Properties

GuiCommon.CONTROLS_WIDTH (default: 330) 
px, shapeControlsSplit's control column: fixed, wide enough for the Angle / Mode (with its velocity readout) / Source + Display rows
GuiCommon.CROSSHAIR_COLOR (default: [0.72 0.2 0.95]) 
selection crosshair -- bright purple, stands out on grey/white axes and busy maps; not orange, which would clash with the ColMapGrayOrange colormap
GuiCommon.DIVIDER_COLOR (default: [0.6 0.6 0.6]) 
thin between-halves divider panel
GuiCommon.GREY_ITEM_COLOR (default: [0.55 0.55 0.55]) 
font of a greyed dropdown item (greyDropdownItem)
GuiCommon.OVERLAY_WEAK_ALPHA (default: 0.35) 
ring opacity of a 'weak' entry in a classification overlay (strong rings: 1)
GuiCommon.SIDE_PANEL_WIDTH (default: 230) 
px, plotWithSidePanel's control column: fixed, so a wide window widens the plot, not the range fields and checkboxes
GuiCommon.STATUS_ERROR_COLOR (default: [0.6 0 0]) 
dark red -- errors and blocked actions
GuiCommon.STATUS_OK_COLOR (default: [0 0.45 0]) 
dark green -- confirmations/info

Methods

 %
 % Mechanical GUI steps only — status-line colouring, the crosshair marker,
 % click-point extraction, the shared layout scaffolding, the min/max

 % Stateless static class: never instantiate it, never call it through Gui.*.

 % isoFreq viewer families genuinely differ in which controls exist at all, in
 % what a click means, and in what the y-axis is; static calls leave each
 % class's decisions visible at its own call site, where inheritance would hide
 % them in an override chain.
 %
 % Error-ID convention: the constructor-argument validators raise errors on the
 % calling constructor's behalf. They take a `className` argument and throw
 % <className>:constructor:<condition> (e.g.
 % ModalShapeViewerGUI:constructor:invalidWindowMode), the three-part form of every
 % other error, so the identifier stays the caller's own and greppable from it.
 %

[shapeParent, controlsParent, bottomParent, splitLayout] = GuiCommon.dividedTopBottom(parent, rowHeight, padding)

 % divider, a map/curve region below
 % >> [shapeParent, controlsParent, bottomParent, splitLayout] = GuiCommon.dividedTopBottom(parent, rowHeight, padding)
 %
 % INPUT: 1. parent    .. uifigure or uigridlayout region to build into
 %        2. rowHeight .. 3-element RowHeight, e.g. {'44x', 3, '56x'}

 %                        is exactly why it is a parameter

 %                           its Layout.Row
 %
 % The divider exists because the two halves otherwise sit flush against
 % each other with no visible boundary.
[dropdown, prevBtn, nextBtn] = GuiCommon.dropdownStepperRow(parent, rowNo, labelText, itemStrs, colWidths, onSelect, onPrev, onNext) 
% Label + index-valued dropdown + << / >> buttons, on one row

 %
 % ItemsData is always 1:numel(itemStrs), so .Value is the INDEX into
 % whatever axis the caller is stepping (angles/wavelengths/wavVecs/
 % omega0List), never the displayed string.

 %                              uigridlayout region to build into

 % free-text label, on one row
 % >> [field, prevBtn, nextBtn, trailLabel, rowLabel] = GuiCommon.indexStepperRow(parent, rowNo, labelText, colWidths, onChange, onPrev, onNext)
 %
 % Limits [1 Inf] / RoundFractionalValues 'on' is the shared "this is a
 % 1-based index" setup. ModalShapeViewerGUI relaxes both at run time when
 % it re-purposes its field as a continuous velocity entry
